# Solver Plan: CPU Performance, then GPU Parity

> **Status:** the CPU solver is correct, stable, and it sleeps. Lever 1 (island sleeping) is done and measured — pyramid 50 runs 12.48 → 9.91 ms and `parity_pile_cpu` 2.52 → 1.42 ms, with whole islands freezing bitwise and zero wake churn anywhere. Lever 5 (sweep ordering) was implemented, measured, and **refuted**: the stacking ceiling is dual-bound, not propagation-bound, and the dial that diagnosis points at — iterations — stands a **180-row, 16,290-box pyramid** at `Physics.solver_iterations=40`, 100% asleep from frame 360. On a fully sleeping scene the frame is now almost entirely broadphase rebuild plus the serial contact-cache store, which is what levers 2 and 3 attack; both are scoped in detail below. Lever 4 remains open.
>
> The work still splits in two, in this order. **Phase 1 is CPU performance** — lever 1 done, lever 5 answered, levers 2–3 scoped and next, lever 4 open. **Phase 2 is GPU parity**, deferred until we are happy with Phase 1, and preserved in full at the bottom of this document because it is where most of the hard-won knowledge lives.
>
> Read *Rules the measurements depend on* before adding a metric. Every wrong theory recorded here came from ignoring one of them.
>
> **End-of-day state, 2026-08-11:** pyramid 50 runs 5.57 ms, `parity_pile_cpu` 2.05, the 16k pyramid 96.1 p50 / 695 p95 — standing, 100% asleep, bit-identical dump signature through every change. The 16k is reachable three ways: the `pyramid16k_cpu` scenario, the `--engine-setting` launch line, or — since the settings-override unification (`plans/settings-override-unification.md`, phases 0–2 shipped) — the in-game Settings menu (`Dev Spawn → pyramid.base_count = 180`, `Physics → solver iterations = 40`), then entering Pyramid. **2026-08-12:** the chunk-indexed bucket rewrite of the serial merge is in and measured — pyramid 50 median **4.84 ms** (was 5.57), drop canary **0.108–0.120** (down from 0.142–0.162 — the removed per-substep `bucket_sort` job spam was fixed overhead on tiny scenes; the quiet-machine validity band in the rules section is re-baselined accordingly), pile clean-cluster 2.13 (+4%, different valid trajectory per the reordering), and the 16k **stands on the new trajectory** (settle f205 by velocity scan, apex compression ~2.1 m, 0 of 16,291 shed, solve ran 242 of 600 frames). Every scenario is bit-identical run-to-run (world-state hash, 2–6 runs each). The quiet-machine round (Editor closed, canary 0.104–0.109): the serial stage **halved** — `broad_phase::emit` 16.5 ms p50 at 16k against the old merge tag's 32 for the same residual coverage, with `pair_loop` 118 CPU-sum (was ~146) and `store` 22 (in band). Whole-frame 16k p50 is statistically flat — median 96.1 over runs of 83.1/107.5/96.1 against the 96.1/695 baseline, p95 ~698 flat as expected (settle frames are solve-dominated) — the asleep frame is DRAM-bound in the parallel pair loop, so ~15 ms off the serial Amdahl path moves the median less than ambient memory traffic does; the 83.1 run is the uncontended shape. The per-candidate lookup hoist landed and verified the same day — all hashes bit-identical (pyr50 `0xbbcdd5c793498629`, pile `0x1aa7dab07b4ce919`, 16k `0x2fc6baa9ec4f2dfb`), 16k p95 682–701 → 640, pile 2.02. The persistent grid was then demoted by its own gate — the superlinearity curve was measured (exponent ~1.5, but only 0.6 ms of 16k prize left post-optimizations; crossover ~50k+ bodies, scheme recorded in lever 2 for when it's needed). Lever 4 experiment (a), event-driven anchor re-form, is implemented on both backends and **measured the same day in a load-immune round** (trajectory and work-count data only — dumps and per-tag frame counts are deterministic under background load, so the round ran with a game and the Editor open by design): determinism held at re-baselined hashes everywhere (pyr50 `0x51ec704bdbc73c14`, pile `0x3174ef980deb7416`, pyr110 `0x93e807d86aed0389`, drop `0x81020d38da418ad7`; 16k not re-run), drift is flat post-settle, the 120 ceiling is unmoved (dual-bound reconfirmed), but a new light-contact chatter class keeps the apex pair (pyr50), seven bodies (pyr110), and 17 of 216 (pile) awake forever where 100% slept before — experiment (b), a 0.9-band hysteresis on the saturation edge, was then measured the same way: pyr50 improves (bulk settle 173 solve-frames, faster than pre-change) but the apex pair still chatters (full-swing oscillators defeat magnitude hysteresis), and **pyr110 mass-rewakes** — the whole island froze by f360 then 43% of it woke at f480, doubling p50 to 115–136 — so **lever 4 is answered like lever 5: no.** Held anchors store lateral tension that the α=0 stabilize pass keeps pulling on; continuous re-anchoring was the damping all along. The revert is applied — pre-(a) semantics restored byte-exact, so the verification round must reproduce the old hashes (pyr50 `0xbbcdd5c793498629`, pile `0xf27ecd23b4f556cf`) and 100%-sleep-zero-transition dumps; the full record is in the lever section. Read that section before picking work. Read the memory files named in the lever sections before touching sleep, replay, or cache code — every one records defects that were measured in, fixed, and must not be reintroduced.

## Where the solver stands

GSE's CPU solver is a faithful port of **Augmented Vertex Block Descent** (Giles, Diaz, Yuksel, SIGGRAPH 2025). The reference implementation is `github.com/savant117/avbd-demo2d`; the constants match it exactly — `PENALTY_MIN` 1.0, `PENALTY_MAX` 1e9, `COLLISION_MARGIN` 0.0005, `STICK_THRESH` 0.01, `beta` 1e5, `alpha` 0.99, `gamma` 0.99.

Verified matching, so do not re-audit: the augmented Lagrangian core (`f = clamp(penalty·C + lambda, fmin, fmax)` with the multiplier in the primal force, Hessian from penalty alone), the dual update and its ramp gating, `post_stabilize` semantics (alpha 1 through the main loop then 0, no dual update in the stabilize pass), frozen Jacobians and the truncated Taylor series, `c = cn - c0·alpha` ≡ `C0(1-α) + J·dp`, adaptive warmstart, BDF1 velocity from `(predicted - old)/dt` derived *before* the stabilize pass, warm-start merge by feature key, and contact-anchor recycling on stick.

### Deliberate divergences from the paper

**The mass-scaled penalty floor is ours and it is load-bearing.** `Solver.cppm` floors normal penalty at `contact_effective_mass / h²`; the reference floors at a flat `PENALTY_MIN`. Removing it to match was tried and is strictly worse on the 50-row pyramid:

| config | p50 | penetration @f60 |
|---|---|---|
| floor kept, 15 iters | 13.8 ms | **0.69 cm** |
| floor kept, 40 iters | 18.2 ms | **0.05 cm** |
| no floor, 15 iters | 24.4 ms | 3.96 cm |
| no floor, 40 iters | 37.3 ms | 1.12 cm |
| no floor, 80 iters | 65.3 ms | 1.15 cm |

Without it penetration **plateaus near 1.15 cm regardless of iteration count**, at up to 4.7x the cost. The reason is structural: the Hessian is `outer(J,J)·penalty`, faithful to the paper, so a penalty near 1 N/m makes the contact contribution vanish and the Newton step becomes inertia-dominated. **The floor conditions the linear system; lambda still carries the load, exactly as AVBD intends.** The reference gets away with `PENALTY_MIN` because its demo scenes are small and 2D. Do not remove this again.

Everything else is an addition the reference simply lacks: Newton step clamps (`max_linear_step` 0.1 m, `max_angular_step` 0.5 rad — AVBD applies the solve unclamped), Hessian diagonal regularization, a linear speed clamp, restitution, sleeping, substepping, and the Jacobi option.

**Inertia is a real tensor now, derived from the collision shape — landed 2026-08-12.** The 2D→3D port had kept 2D's scalar semantics: `motion_component` carried one `moment_of_inertia` (default 163 kg·m² for anything that never set it) and `inv_inertial_tensor` built `identity·(1/I)`, so the R·I⁻¹·Rᵀ conjugation was a no-op and every body was rotationally a sphere. The field is deleted. `physics::mass_properties_of(shape, mass)` (`BoundingBox.cppm`) returns the body-space diagonal per shape — box `m(y²+z²)/12` per axis, sphere `2mr²/5`, capsule split by volume into barrel and caps with the hemispheres parallel-axis-shifted to the capsule center (`axial = m_b·r²/2 + m_c·2r²/5`, `transverse = m_b(h²/3 + r²/4) + m_c(h² + 3hr/4 + 2r²/5)`, axis local +Y to match `capsule_endpoints`) — and `inv_inertial_tensor(moments, orientation)` finally does work. **Nothing authors the tensor any more**, which is the point: the system derives it from `collision_component::shape` and the body's mass at body-build time, once per frame in `build_body_states` (folded into the pass already walking collisions for AABBs, hoisted above the `resolve_collisions` gate so non-colliding bodies — ragdoll bones before the flip — still get one) and once per frame into a `body_moments` array in `update_vbd`, re-conjugated by the live orientation each substep. The four spawn sites that used to hand-compute a scalar just set mass; `sandbox::sphere` never set it at all and had been running on the 163 default.

**New invariant, load-bearing: `update_orientation` ⇒ `inv_inertia` invertible.** `Solver.cppm:1172` and `vbd_solve_color.slang:460` both invert the tensor unguarded inside the `update_orientation` branch. A shapeless or degenerate-shape dynamic body now produces an exactly-zero tensor where it used to inherit the 163 default, so both build paths clear `update_orientation` via `is_rotatable` rather than letting a zero matrix reach `inverse()` and NaN the sim. What moves numerically: cubes are algebraically unchanged (`m·s²/6` from both the old `m·dot(size,size)/18` and the new `m(s²+s²)/12`), so the cube harness should reproduce up to float rounding order; non-cubic boxes gain real anisotropy; spheres go 163 → `2mr²/5` (100 kg at r = 1 m: 163 → 40); capsules stop being box-averaged bounding boxes, so ragdoll limbs and the character proxy resist long-axis spin far less and locomotion re-tunes. The mass-scaled penalty floor is now direction-dependent through the effective-mass projection.

**The gyroscopic term stays dropped, by decision (2026-08-12).** `ω×Iω` was *exactly* zero under isotropy and is now merely ignored: prediction is still `q_target = exp(ω·dt)·q`, so precession and the Dzhanibekov flip do not exist. Explicit integration of the term is energy-gaining and an implicit sub-solve is a per-body Newton iteration in the hot predict loop; neither was taken, and copying either from a reference is explicitly not the bar. Open question, deliberately unanswered: the augmented-Lagrangian angular block already assembles `I/h²` into `h_tt` and solves it with the Schur complement every iteration, so a solver-native treatment would more likely live in that block than bolted onto prediction — unproven, not yet designed.

**Geometric stiffness is not a divergence.** It was scoped as missing and that was wrong on both counts. For contacts the reference's `Manifold` never writes `H` — `Force::Force` zero-initialises it and `computeDerivatives` sets only `J` — so `G = diagonal(0,0,0)·|f| = 0` for every contact; `manifold.cpp` says outright that the truncated Taylor series discards the second-order term as insignificant for contacts. Only `Joint` writes `H`, and GSE already implements that path in `accumulate_geometric_stiffness` (`Solver.cppm:1635`), called from four sites. Adding G to `accumulate_contact` would *create* a divergence.

### Failed experiments, recorded so they are not retried

**A load-aware normal floor** (`max(m_eff/h², |lambda[0]| / max_penetration)`) does not work at any tuning. Penetration dropped as designed — 2.27 cm to 0.09 cm in the first frames — then the pyramid **exploded**: apex rising, speed pinned at the 15 m/s clamp by frame 60, 1116 of 1275 blocks scattered. It also launched the `physics_stress` box grid, because warm-started `lambda` during an impact holds the impulsive force, not the resting load. Gating on `approach_speed >= -restitution_threshold` fixed the grid but not the pyramid. The reason it cannot work: stability bounds `k ≲ m_eff/h²`, and setting the load floor to that bound and solving for penetration gives exactly the penetration the existing floor already produces. There is no headroom.

**A corrected early exit** — feeding it absolute penetration instead of the frame-relative delta, threshold retuned 1 cm to 2 mm — recovers nothing and costs slightly more (`parity_pile_cpu` 2.52 → 3.57 ms, pyramid50 13.8 → 20.0). At 2 mm nothing qualifies, since base 50 settles at 0.82 cm; scenes that would qualify are fixed-overhead dominated. Loosening the threshold only lets quality drift back toward it, which was the original bug. **Use `solver_iterations` as the cost dial instead** — it is meaningful again now that the exit is gone.

### The stacking ceiling, priced

At 15 iterations × 2 substeps the standing ceiling sits between base 110 and 120:

| base | blocks | 15 iters × 2 sub | p50 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | 5,050 | stands, sleeps by f360 | 68.3 |
| 110 | 6,105 | stands, sleeps by f360 | 91.1 |
| 120 | 7,260 | **collapses** | 185.1 |
| 140 | 9,870 | collapses | 211.8 |
| 180 | 16,290 | collapses | 245.5 |

Every collapse detonates **after** f120 — 140 and 180 are nearly calm at f120 (max < 0.5 m/s) before the unconverged bottom-row load buckles them, and 120 fails from a handful of early-ballistic bodies whose failure spreads — so a standing verdict needs the full 600 frames, not a glance at the onset.

The constraint is dual-side: sweep order propagates positions in one pass (lever 5 proved this and it did not help), but lambda and the penalty ramp advance once per iteration, and resting-load lambda growth is what a tall stack needs. Iterations therefore buy rows, and island sleeping makes them nearly free once settled. The cost **inverts** at the margin: base 120 at 40 iterations runs p50 **94.7 ms** against **185.1** at 15 — the converging configuration sleeps by f240 and coasts while the cheap one collapses and burns full-cost frames forever. Substeps work too (120 at sub=4 it=15 stands, 168.0 ms) but iterations are the better deal per dual update.

**The 16k pyramid stands**: base 180 at `Physics.solver_iterations=40` — 100% asleep from f360, zero churn, apex within 2.05 m of spawn (pure contact compression, ~1.15 cm/row), nothing shed, no holes, p50 257.6 ms dominated entirely by settle. The settings range for `solver_iterations` caps at 40; raising it is a one-line annotation change in `System.cppm` if deeper stacks ever need it.

### Where the time goes

`pyramid_cpu` at base 50, **awake/settle frame**. Parallel tags are CPU-time summed across 16 workers; `end_frame` and `age_and_prune` are serial, so their numbers are wall time on the critical path.

| section | µs | |
|---|---|---|
| `gs_color_iter` | 145,354 | parallel, ≈9 ms wall — the solve itself |
| `pair_loop` | 29,622 | parallel — broadphase |
| `contact_dual_update` | 20,011 | parallel |
| `contact_penalty` | 3,420 | parallel |
| `broad_phase::merge` | 2,482 | parallel |
| **`end_frame::store`** | **1,691** | **serial** |
| **`age_and_prune`** | **367** | **serial** |
| `end_frame::bodies_copy` | 26 | serial, settled |

`contact_cache::reserve` bought ~7% (13.3 → 12.4 ms).

**Asleep frame**, base 50, after the both-endpoints-inactive contact skip: `contact_dual_update` 2,278, `contact_penalty` 196, `warm_contacts` 179, `frozen_jacobians` 158 — collapsed. What remains is `pair_loop` ~29,500 CPU-sum (the broadphase still SAT-and-manifolds every sleeping pair every substep) and `end_frame::store` 1,644 serial (kept deliberately so `age_and_prune` does not evict sleeping contacts). That pair is the asleep-frame floor, and it is levers 2 and 3.

At base 180 the same two dominate absolutely: `pair_loop` 377,369 CPU-sum, `end_frame::store` **28,549 serial** (peak 137,133), `age_and_prune` 7,549 serial. The serial Amdahl ceiling that read "about 2 ms" at base 50 is ~50 ms at 16k.

## Phase 1: the five levers

Ranked by expected value over effort when written; statuses as of 2026-08-11. Lever 1 is done, lever 5 is answered (negative, informatively), levers 2 and 3 are scoped and next — in that order, because the cache restructure in 3 is what makes the sleeping-pair replay in 2 possible. Lever 4 borrows a validated result and remains open.

### 1. Island sleeping — DONE

Shipped 2026-08-11: pyramid 50 −21%, `parity_pile_cpu` −44%, zero wake transitions in every dump, and the ceiling side effect recorded above. The design that survived measurement:

- **Islands**: union-find over the constraint graph, rebuilt every substep and flattened so the root array is a single read (thread-safe for the parallel passes). Contacts and joints both union; **locked bodies never union** — the floor would merge every pile into one island. Per-substep rebuild is immune to the body-index-renumbering-on-spawn class. Cost: 134 µs at base 50, ~2 ms at 16k (`vbd::islands`).
- **Entry is a two-threshold gate**: a body accumulates sleep when *it* is calm (own linear and angular below the sleep thresholds) **and** its island holds no member above `wake_thresh` (10× = 0.5 m/s, linear, post-solve). Entry-blocking and waking are the same predicate at the same threshold, so there is no oscillation band.
- **Wake is island-wide**: any awake member above `wake_thresh` (pre-solve) zeroes every sleeping member's counter — the old pairwise rule was exactly this restricted to one hop. The unconditional pairwise **joint** wake loop is kept: a slow-but-awake jointed neighbour must still wake its partner, or a driven torso leaves limbs frozen.
- **Both-endpoints-inactive contact skip** in `warm_contacts`, `contact_penalty`, `frozen_jacobians`, `contact_dual_update`, keyed on the coloring-time inactive snapshot so a mid-substep island wake sees a self-consistent frozen view. `end_frame::store` deliberately still runs for skipped contacts — the cache round-trip is what stops `age_and_prune` evicting sleeping contacts and stripping the wake warm-starts.

**Strict all-member entry was tried first and failed — do not go back to it.** Gating on every island member being sleep-calm never bootstraps at scale: base 50 is one 1275-body island, someone is always over 0.05 m/s (at f300 exactly one body, at 0.059), and without the per-body ratchet the calm mass never freezes, so the jitter *regrows* (75 bodies over threshold at f500, 191 at f599) — 0% asleep ever, p50 29.7 against 12.3. Per-body accumulation is load-bearing: each sleeper becomes a rock that damps its neighbourhood. The two-threshold gate keeps the ratchet and still kills the churn.

Why sleeping islands are cache-stable: `c0` is recomputed from frozen positions each substep, so `c = cn − c0·α = 0` in the main loop — lambda frozen, penalties decay to the floor, and waking warm-starts from the true resting load. Known retained gaps, all pre-existing: restitution can inject velocity into a sleeping body without waking it; removing support under a sleeping island does not wake it (no end-touch events); a woken body sits out one substep because coloring runs before the wake pass.

### 2. Broadphase — replay DONE 2026-08-11, structure still open

The sleeping-pair replay shipped and closed most of this lever's asleep-frame case. In the pair loop, a both-inactive pair skips the ECS finds, SAT, manifold, and per-point hash lookups; its constraints re-emit from the cache in canonical orientation via `for_each_pair`, with pair metas and contact points computed from frozen anchors so grounded flags and collision results stay truthful. Falls back to the real narrow phase whenever the cache has nothing for the pair — correctness never depends on the cache being warm. Verified behaviorally exact: the pyramid dump signature is bit-identical through the replay path.

Three refinements were measured in, each fixing a defect the first cut shipped: replay emits only entries refreshed exactly last substep (the staleness window otherwise resurrects features the narrow phase had already dropped — ghost contacts inflated asleep counts ~1.5×); the cache sorts its entry array on **quiesce** (a substep with no new keys) rather than on a tail threshold, which had been re-sorting every settle substep at 41 ms a pop; and `for_each_pair` refuses oversized unsorted tails (> 4096) and forces narrow-phase fallback rather than scanning quadratically on churning scenes. A fourth change generalized lever 1's per-contact skip: an **active-contact list** built once after coloring replaces the both-inactive branch in all four contact loops — the dual update runs iterations × substeps passes per frame, so at 40 iterations the skip branch alone had grown to 77.7 ms CPU-sum on asleep frames; with the list it is zero.

Where that leaves the numbers (quiet machine, Editor closed): pyramid 50 median **5.57 ms** (12.48 at the start of the day), pile **2.05**, 16k p50 **96.1** with p95 695 (245.5 / 1084 this morning). The 16k asleep frame is now bounded by `pair_loop` at ~146 ms CPU-sum (AABB scan + replay walks) and two serial stages proportional to replayed contact count: the k-way bucket **merge** (~32 ms — serial by construction, exists to make worker-bucket order deterministic) and the cache **store/touch** (~19 ms of DRAM-bound probes, lever 3's documented remainder).

**The store/touch remainder is folded into the replay walk — implemented 2026-08-12, awaiting its round.** `for_each_pair` now stamps `last_seen = m_stamp` on each entry it emits (after the emit gate reads the pre-stamp value — same-thread, sequenced), and the constraints it emits carry `replayed = 1` in the former `pad_end` u32 (same ABI slot, zero on every other path, GPU never reads it). `end_frame::store` skips both-inactive replayed contacts outright — zero hash probes — and keeps the touch-then-store fallback for both-inactive contacts the narrow phase built: cache-cold pairs, gate-stale entries, and the >4096 unsorted-tail refusal all land there. **The fallback is load-bearing for exactness — never delete it**: a narrow-phase-built sleeping contact whose store is skipped would leave the cache holding values the next build re-decays (double decay) and the trajectory diverges. Thread safety is single-writer-by-construction, the chunk-bucket argument: each pair is processed by exactly one worker, a pair's entries are stamped only from its own walk, cross-pair probe sequences read key fields only (distinct memory locations from the stamp), and `m_stamp` is constant through the parallel region because `advance()` runs serially in `begin_frame`. Expected effect: the 16k asleep-frame serial store (~19–22 ms, ~360k dependent DRAM probes) collapses to near zero, with the stamp writes landing in parallel on key slots the emit gate already has hot. Acceptance: behaviorally exact — every hash must HOLD at its pre-lever-4 value (pyr50 `0xbbcdd5c793498629`, pile `0xf27ecd23b4f556cf`, 16k `0x2fc6baa9ec4f2dfb`), which simultaneously verifies the lever-4 revert; the store-tag collapse is readable under background load (zero work is unmistakable at any load), while the precise whole-frame p50 movement waits for a quiet round.

**VERIFIED 2026-08-12 (loaded machine, trajectory + work-count instruments).** All three hashes returned exactly — pyr50 `0xbbcdd5c793498629` ×2, pile `0xf27ecd23b4f556cf` ×2, 16k `0x2fc6baa9ec4f2dfb`, drop `0x81020d38da418ad7` ×2 — one round proving the lever-4 revert byte-faithful and the store-fold behaviorally exact, sleep behavior included (hash equality is bitwise per-frame state). The 16k `end_frame::store` asleep-frame median collapsed **24.0–26.4 ms → 0.602 ms** (before = same-day pre-lever-4 runs in the same scenario dir, `gs_color_iter` 242 frames matching on both sides); the store p95 (~65 ms) is the settle/awake frames still doing full stores, as designed. Whole-frame 16k p50 read 95.4 under heavy background load, i.e. at the quiet baseline (95.7–96.1) — per the standing Amdahl note, serial-path savings move the DRAM-bound asleep-frame median less than ambient traffic does, so the whole-frame movement needs a quiet round if anyone wants the number. The serial remainder on an asleep 16k frame is now the `emit` tail (~19–21 ms) plus `pair_scan` (~19 ms CPU-sum, parallel), which is the next target.

**The emit-tail decision (open).** Three ways forward, priced. (1) *Narrow*: shave `emit` in place — the pair-meta walk (airborne flags, collision results) chunks cleanly, but the core is `add_contact_constraint` appending ~360k constraints into the graph in deterministic order; realistic ceiling is maybe half of ~20 ms. (2) *Structural — sleeping-subgraph persistence*: on a fully-asleep frame the constraint graph built each substep is bit-identical to the previous one; persisting the frozen portion across substeps eliminates `emit`, the replay walks (~100+ CPU-sum in `pair_loop`), and the island/coloring rebuild for frozen content in one stroke — the terminal asleep-frame optimization, but it must confront the body-index-renumbering-on-spawn class (the per-substep rebuild is the current immunity), wake-edge availability, and every recorded coloring/island invariant; a real project. (3) *Declare Phase 1 done and open Phase 2*: the Vulkan defect that defined Phase 2 is fixed (missing STORAGE_READ visibility in the auto-barrier derivation, per its memory) — the remaining GPU work is the DX12-only 2-of-129 pile nondeterminism residue and promoting the parity ladder to a standing regression gate. **The attribution round ran 2026-08-12 on the first genuinely cold machine (canary 0.097–0.100 — below the recorded band; the "quiet" 95.7–96.1 rounds carried ~20 ms of ambient contention, likely the open Editor).** The true asleep 16k frame is **p50 75.5/76.4 ms without dump writing, 78.7 with** (dump ≈ 2.5 ms, not the suspected 20; hash verifies without the dump, so routine rounds can drop it). Attribution: VBD owns essentially the whole frame — every system's `update_wall` ≈ the frame span, physics is the schedule's pole. The pieces: `vbd::solve` wall **26 ms flat on every frame including fully-asleep** (children sum to ~10–11: iterations 3.2, coloring 5.1, islands 1.8 — leaving **~15 ms of untagged serial per-substep bookkeeping** inside `solve()`, prime suspects the active-contact-list build and the all-body passes, ×2 substeps); broadphase pair_loop 88 CPU-sum (~10–18 wall, the replay walks), `emit` 18.4 serial, pair_scan 19 CPU-sum, collect/sort/validate ~3; store 0.5 (post-fold), copies/writeback ~3. So the CPU prize is real: **~50+ of the 76 ms is machinery that exists to rebuild identical state under a fully-sleeping scene.** Two moves fall out, ordered: (2a) a **fully-asleep substep fast path** — when the active set is empty and the pair set validates, the substep's solve and broadphase processing are no-ops by definition; skipping them is behaviorally exact (hashes must hold) and cheap to build, attacking the 26 ms solve wall, the replay walks, and emit in one guard; wake sources (spawns, impulses, driven joints, edits) all flip bodies active before the guard evaluates. (2b) full sleeping-subgraph persistence — the same idea per-island for the partially-asleep gameplay case (one awake island in a big sleeping world), which the frame-level guard does not cover; bigger project, same invariants to respect (renumbering class, wake edges, coloring). Then (3) Phase 2.

**The k-way merge is gone — implemented and measured 2026-08-12** (pyramid 50 5.57 → 4.84, drop 0.15 → 0.11, `emit` 19.1 ms p50 at 16k vs the merge's 32 under Editor contention; 16k p50 pending a quiet round; all scenarios hash-identical run-to-run and the 16k stands, settle f205, 0 shed). The pair loop now writes to chunk-indexed buckets: the caller partitions the object range into `worker_count × chunks_per_worker` contiguous chunks itself and parallelizes over chunk indices, so bucket contents are deterministic by construction and concatenation in chunk order replaces the per-bucket sort, the cursor heap, and the `merged_points` copy outright. `merged_pairs` and its sort went the same way — both consumers walk the buckets through `views::join`. Bucket `c` is written only by the job that iterates chunk `c`, whatever the task pool's internal job grouping, so the single-writer property does not depend on matching `parallel_invoke_range`'s chunk formula (the geometry mirrors it 1:1 anyway, so stealing granularity is unchanged). The `merge` and `bucket_sort` tags are deleted; the remaining serial tail — the pair-meta walk and `add_contact_constraint` emission — is tagged `vbd_cpu::broad_phase::emit`. What the measurement round must know: contact emission order is now the serial sweep's `(i, j)` order rather than canonical `(body_a, body_b, feature)` order — partition-independent, since concatenating contiguous chunks in index order reproduces the serial sweep exactly, so worker count and `broad_phase_chunks_per_worker` do not affect trajectories — and dump signatures re-baseline once against older sorted-order builds while run-to-run determinism is unchanged, which is the property the merge existed to provide.

**The per-candidate lookups are hoisted — implemented and verified 2026-08-12.** All four world-state hashes came back bit-identical (behaviorally exact, as designed). Quiet numbers: 16k `pair_loop` 117 → 100.5 ms CPU-sum p50 asleep (settle p95 474 → 418, max −32%), whole-frame 16k p95 682–701 → **640** and p99 → 670 with p50 flat at 95.7; pyramid 50 p50 4.72 with p95 28 → 23.8; pile p50 2.02 — below the pre-chunk-bucket 2.05 for the first time; drop 0.100. The trade to know: `collect_objects` absorbed the resolution serially, 0.59 → 4.07 ms at 16k (one flat_map find + one motion find per object instead of two per candidate) — net roughly neutral on asleep frames, clearly positive on awake ones; collect is trivially parallelizable with the same chunk-bucket pattern if it ever leads a profile. `collision_pair` now carries what the pair loop used to resolve per candidate: the transform and collision pointers (the narrow-phase four), the solver body index (previously two `flat_map` binary searches per candidate, paid on asleep frames too), and the dynamic-body restitution (previously two `motion.find()`s per manifold pair). `collect_collision_objects` resolves each once per object and drops objects with no solver body — the pair loop rejected those before emitting anything, so behavior is identical, and the acceptance test is bit-identical world-state hashes on every scenario. `add_scene_contacts_to_solver` lost its now-unused `id_to_body_index`, `transform`, and `collision` parameters. The carried pointers are valid for the one substep the objects vector lives; nothing may hold a `collision_pair` across substeps.

**Pair-set reuse across substeps — implemented and measured 2026-08-12; correct everywhere, a modest win at base 50, neutral at 16k, and it taught the broadphase lesson that matters for the grid.** Hashes: drop, pyramid 50, *and* the 16k all HELD bit-identical — stronger than predicted, because lattice scenes never reorder in x, so the build-order candidate list equals the re-swept order at every substep; only the pile re-baselined (`0x1aa7dab07b4ce919` → `0xf27ecd23b4f556cf`, identical across 3 runs), and the 16k dump scan reproduces the known trajectory exactly (settle f205, 177.441 m, 0 shed). The serial goals all landed: collect 4.07 → 1.77, sort 1.82 → 0.92, validate 0.64 at 16k. The surprise is parallel: processing detached from the sweep costs ~45% more per substep at 16k (`pair_loop` 103.5 processing-only vs 100.5 for the old fused sweep+process, plus 18.7 of `pair_scan`) while at base 50 the same split is a mild win (3.5+0.6 vs 4.3 fused) — same trajectory both scales, so the penalty is **memory locality, not algorithm**: the fused loop processed each candidate while the sweep still had its data hot, and at DRAM-bound scale the split pass re-pays every fetch. `pair_scan` runs +30% over a tight sweep, exactly the wider x-band, ruling out candidate growth. Net at 16k asleep ≈ neutral (−3.2 serial, +21.7 CPU-sum ≈ +1.4 wall); whole-frame reads (106/112 vs 95.7 post-hoist) are ambient-dominated — same-work pyr50 p95s ran ~20% hot the same evening, and the quiet-machine spread at 16k has been 83–112 all day. Do not invest in re-fusing or prefetch games; the persistent grid replaces this sweep wholesale and inherits the lesson: **keep processing fused with discovery at scale.** The broadphase is now three functions with one authority each: `build_pair_set` (first substep of each fixed step: the min.x sort plus a sweep fattened by `2 × max_linear_speed × sub_dt × (substeps−1)`, emitting `candidate_pair` u32 index pairs into the sorted objects vector via the chunk-bucket pattern — jointed-pair filtering happens here now), `pair_set_valid` (reuse substeps: every object's *current* world AABB must sit inside its build-time AABB fattened by the per-body travel budget, else rebuild), and `add_scene_contacts_to_solver` (every substep: pure processing of the candidate list — replay-or-narrow per candidate, no sweep, no sort, no collect). The validation deliberately checks the AABB, not a position delta: rotation grows an AABB at zero positional drift, and the solve's Newton steps can exceed the speed-clamp bound during depenetration, so the a-priori travel bound is a *budget* that decides fattening, while the containment check is what correctness actually rests on — a violated budget just costs one rebuild, which is what every substep cost before. Sleeping scenes validate trivially forever; `substeps=1` degenerates to today exactly (zero budget, rebuild every substep). Objects and candidates are locals of `update_vbd` spanning one run — the run window, per the pointer-lifetime rule. Expected on measurement: collect, sort, and the enumeration sweep halve at substeps=2 (enumeration now tagged `enumerate`/`pair_scan`, processing keeps `pair_loop`, the containment check is `validate`); fat-margin extra candidates emit nothing (SAT rejects at the unchanged speculative margin), so first-substep contacts are unchanged, but reuse-substep emission order follows the build-time sort rather than a re-sweep — hashes re-baseline on scenes that reorder in x mid-fixed-step (settle, piles), while `parity_drop_cpu` and fully-asleep steady states should hold bit-identical.

**The persistent 3D structure is DEMOTED — the curve was measured 2026-08-12 and the prize evaporated.** The sweep's superlinearity is real and matches the prediction: `pair_scan` per-object p50 runs 386/460/579/743/904/1,155 ns at base 20/50/80/110/140/180, local exponent ~N^1.5 at the top (the x-band grows with column height ∝ √N). But post merge/hoist/reuse, enumeration is 18.8 CPU-sum of a ~125 CPU-sum asleep frame at 16k — a linear grid buys ~0.6 ms wall there, against 37 ms of remaining serial store+emit and lever 4's iteration-dial prize. The grid's crossover is ~50k+ bodies; build it when a scene needs that scale, mirroring the GPU scheme (`collision_broad_phase.slang`: spatial hash, statics in a separate brute-force list so the floor never pollutes cells, min-overlap-cell dedup rule) and keeping processing fused with discovery per the pair-set-reuse lesson. The original scope, for reference:

Island sleeping changed the shape of this lever. The structural weaknesses stand — sweep-and-prune on `min.x` only, rebuilt from scratch **every substep**, a one-box-deep pyramid sharing one z so the band prunes almost nothing, four ECS `find()`s per surviving candidate — but the dominant waste is now that **every sleeping pair is SAT-tested and manifold-generated every substep to reproduce a result that is frozen by construction**. On a fully asleep base-50 frame `pair_loop` is ~29.5 ms CPU-sum of the ~10 ms frame's parallel work; at 16k it is 377 ms CPU-sum. A sleeping pair's geometry cannot change, so its narrow-phase output is bit-identical every substep — the whole pipeline from AABB test to manifold is recomputation of a constant.

Moves, in order:

1. **Sleeping-pair replay.** For pairs where both bodies are inactive, skip the SAT, the manifold, and the ECS lookups entirely; re-emit the contact constraints from the cache. Requires the cache to enumerate entries by pair (lever 3's restructure provides it — do that first). Correctness rests on three invariants: frozen geometry makes replay exact; the replayed contacts must still enter the graph (islands and wake propagation depend on the edges existing); and replay must apply the same pair-orientation swap the cache lookup performs. Expected effect: the asleep-frame `pair_loop` collapses to the AABB scan, at 50 and at 16k alike.
2. **Hoist the per-candidate ECS lookups.** `collect_collision_objects` already resolves every object once; carry the transform and collision pointers through the sort instead of `find()`ing each of the four per candidate inside the hot loop. Trivial, and pays on awake frames where replay does not apply.
3. **Pair set once per frame, not per substep.** Between substeps a body moves at most `max_linear_speed × sub_dt` (≈ 6 cm at defaults) — fatten the AABB margin by the per-frame travel bound and reuse the pair set across all substeps. Narrow phase stays per substep for awake pairs; the *candidate enumeration* halves.
4. **Persistent 3D structure.** A uniform grid (2 m cells against 1 m boxes; mirror the GPU solver's existing grid scheme rather than inventing a second one) or a refit BVH, persistent across frames — sleeping islands never move, so their cells never touch. This is the structural replacement and the biggest awake-frame win at 16k; measure the current structure's superlinearity first so the replacement has a curve to beat.

**Measure.** `vbd_cpu::broad_phase::*` tags; pair-test and narrow-phase invocation counts per frame; the asleep-frame floor at base 50 and 180. Scale-test base 10 through 180 — the x-band scan should degrade superlinearly and the replacement should not. The `jointed_pairs` exclusion and speculative margin semantics must survive every move unchanged.

### 3. Kill the serial store — DONE 2026-08-11, remainder assigned to lever 2

Shipped as a three-array flat cache (`m_index` u32 handles / `m_keys` 32-byte key+stamp slots / `m_payloads`), entity-id keys canonicalized to one stored orientation, `last_seen` stamps with compaction-on-pressure replacing the per-substep `age_and_prune` sweep, and `touch` (stamp-only refresh) for both-endpoints-inactive contacts with store fallback on miss. `remove_body` deleted (zero callers); entity-id keys kill the body-index-renumbering aliasing class outright, and compaction sorts entries by key so a pair's contacts are contiguous for lever 2's replay. Results: pyramid 50 9.89 → 8.66–8.92, 16k p50 257.6 → 168.9, net 16k serial cache cost 36.0 → 34.7 ms (store 34.7, prune 0), prune gone at every scale.

Four container iterations were measured getting here; the traps are worth the ink. Pair-only hashing (free per-pair enumeration) clustered every pair's features into one probe run and cost 2.2× on the pile — never tax the hot path for a speculative consumer. The old `unordered_map` was accidentally strong at 16k: nodes allocated in first-insert canonical order get revisited in that order every substep, so the prefetcher hid the pointer chase. Keys stored beside payloads make key *verification* cost payload traffic. And the terminal lesson: at 16k the frame's working set flushes L3 between stores, so **any per-contact hash probe is a dependent random DRAM access** — 360k of them ≈ 35 ms no matter the layout. The two true fixes are carrying handles (blocked: `contact_constraint` is GPU ABI) or not doing per-contact cache work on asleep frames at all — which is lever 2's sleeping-pair replay, now the designated owner of the remaining ~35 ms.

The original scope, for reference:

At base 50 this was "about 2 ms of serial work"; at 16k it is `end_frame::store` at **28.5 ms serial p50, 137 ms peak**, plus `age_and_prune` at 7.5 ms — the Amdahl ceiling grew to ~50 ms. The mechanism is unchanged: `contact_cache` is a node-based `std::unordered_map` keyed `(body_a, body_b, feature)`, `store` is a serial `insert_or_assign` per contact per substep, and `age_and_prune` sweeps the entire map per substep to increment a counter, holding `max_age + 1` substeps of entries.

Moves, in order:

1. **Open-addressing flat table.** One allocation, linear probing, key packed from the body pair and feature (the hash already builds exactly that u64 pair). Kills the per-insert allocation and per-lookup pointer chase. Add the per-pair enumeration lever 2's replay needs — entries for one pair land contiguously or via a per-pair head index.
2. **`last_seen` stamp instead of the age sweep.** Stamp entries with the substep counter on store or touch; a stale entry is a miss at lookup; compaction runs on load-factor pressure, not per substep. `age_and_prune` stops existing as a per-substep traversal.
3. **Store only what changed.** A both-endpoints-inactive contact's cached value is identical to what the store would rewrite. With replay (lever 2) refreshing `last_seen` on the pairs it emits, the store loop runs over awake contacts only, and the asleep-frame store cost goes to ~zero. This deletes the one deliberate inefficiency lever 1 left behind.
4. **Parallel store — only if still needed.** Contacts are already sorted canonically by `(body_a, body_b)`, so contiguous ranges shard cleanly to workers if the store is still on the critical path after 1–3. Measure before building it; the expectation is that 1–3 shrink the store below noise on settled scenes.
5. **Audit: the cache is keyed by body index across frames.** Indices renumber when a body despawns (swap-remove), `remove_body` purges the removed index but any entries under the *swapped* body's old index alias the new occupant — the same class as the GPU spawn blowup. Verify whether the despawn path compensates; if not, re-key by entity id (the owner ids are in hand at both store and lookup sites) and the class is gone permanently.

**Measure.** The two serial tags at base 50 and 180, asleep and awake, plus suite p50s. Success looks like: asleep-frame serial cost independent of contact count, and the 16k asleep frame bounded by the broadphase AABB scan alone.

### 4. Friction anchors that re-form — ANSWERED 2026-08-12: (a) and (b) both measured, both degrade sleep; REVERTED

Solver2D's `TGS_Sticky` achieves stable stacking with **no impulse warm starting at all** — only persistent friction anchors, pulled together tangentially and **reset when they exceed the Coulomb limit**, then re-formed. Catto's conclusion is that strong friction is what makes stacking stable.

GSE recycles anchors on `sticking`, which is correct, but `sticking` latches *off* and never returns: once a contact slips past `stick_threshold` its tangential floor drops to `penalty_min` and it cannot recover. That is a one-way latch where the reference has a cycle.

**Mechanism map, 2026-08-12 — the latch theory is wrong in detail; read this before implementing.** `sticking` is recomputed fresh every substep at `end_frame::store` (`Solver.cppm:954`: normal lambda engaged ∧ tangential gap under `stick_threshold` ∧ tangential lambda under the cone) — there is no stored latch. The floor gate being inert (measured earlier) has a structural reason: warm-started tangential *penalties* are carried per contact through the cache and ride far above `penalty_min`, and the dual update ramps them on violated rows regardless of the flag — so `sticking`'s only real effect is **anchor recycling**. And a non-sticking contact already re-forms *continuously*: fresh manifold anchors every substep with warm cone-clamped lambda carried. The remaining delta against Solver2D's `TGS_Sticky` is therefore narrow: event-driven reset (re-anchor exactly when the cone saturates, keeping the grip point otherwise) versus GSE's continuous re-form, plus whatever the reference gains from per-iteration anchor handling inside the solve — which GSE's frozen Jacobians forbid mid-substep by design. The plausible experiments, in order: (a) event-driven re-form — recycle anchors also for cone-saturated contacts but reset them to current positions at build exactly on the substep the cone first saturates, so grip points persist through *dynamic* friction instead of resetting every substep; (b) hysteresis on the sticking test (enter below the cone with margin, exit only on exceedance) to kill any enter/exit chatter. Both are build-time changes, no frozen-Jacobian violation. Expectations should stay modest: the stacking ceiling is dual-bound (lever 5's refutation), so the measure is lateral drift per row over time and pile/stack settle quality at fixed iterations, not the ceiling.

**Experiment (a) implemented 2026-08-12, both backends, unmeasured.** The one-bool `sticking` state is now a three-flag state machine, computed at the same store sites (`end_frame::store` on CPU, `vbd_update_sticking` on GPU): `sticking` keeps its exact test and now gates *only* the mass-scaled tangential row floor; `saturated` = normal engaged ∧ tangential lambda at or over the cone; `keep_anchor` = engaged ∧ gap under `stick_threshold` ∧ (under the cone ∨ already saturated at the previous store). Anchor recycling at build keys on `keep_anchor` instead of `sticking` (`reuse_cached_anchor` in `System.cpp` / `collision_narrow_phase.slang`). Net behavior change: the substep the cone *first* saturates still re-forms the anchor at the current position — the TGS_Sticky reset event — but *ongoing* saturation now keeps the grip point, so a contact pinned at the cone by structural load stops re-anchoring every substep, which was the per-substep ratchet behind lateral creep. Genuine continuous sliding still re-forms every `stick_threshold` (1 cm) of accumulated gap through the unchanged gap clause, so held anchors live inside the envelope sticking contacts always had: gap < 1 cm, force cone-clamped — no new force or gap regime, only a different choice of when the anchor resets. Cross-substep state rides the existing u32 `contact_constraint::sticking` (no ABI change): bit 0 = sticking, bit 1 = saturated-at-last-store (the edge-detection input), and on the GPU only, bit 2 = keep_anchor — the GPU needs it in the constraint because `contact_data` is copied to `warm_start_buffer` after `vbd_update_sticking` and is therefore its own cache, where the CPU keeps all three as bools in `cached_lambda`. The two CPU row-floor reads mask `& 1u`; the sleeping-pair replay forwards bits 0|1 so a woken island resumes with correct state; `swapped_payload` needs no change (the flags are orientation-independent). Old behavior is recoverable by dropping the `∨ prev_saturated` term — `keep_anchor` then collapses to `sticking`. Files: `System.cpp` (build + replay), `Solver.cppm` (store + two row floors), `ContactCache.cppm`, `collision_narrow_phase.slang`, `vbd_update_sticking.slang`. Expect **every scenario hash to re-baseline** — any cone saturation during settle changes the trajectory, and every scene saturates cones while settling; run-to-run determinism is unaffected (the state machine is a pure function of solver state). Experiment (b), hysteresis on the sticking test, is deliberately not in — measure (a) alone first; the enter/exit chatter (b) targets still resets on every other cone crossing under (a), so if drift numbers improve but chatter shows up in the dump as alternating re-anchors, (b) is the next dial.

**Measured 2026-08-12 — a load-immune round.** The entire round ran with a game and the Editor open, deliberately: every number below is trajectory or work-count data from the state dumps and per-tag frame counts, which background load cannot alter (the sim is fixed-step deterministic — load taints wall-clock only). No ms figures were collected; those wait for one quiet round, or a paired interleaved A/B if they are ever wanted under load. Determinism held everywhere — two runs per scenario, bit-identical world-state hashes — and every scenario re-baselined as predicted: pyr50 `0x51ec704bdbc73c14`, pile `0x3174ef980deb7416`, pyr110 `0x93e807d86aed0389`, drop `0x81020d38da418ad7` (the 16k was not re-run and re-baselines on its next run). Results against the measure list:

1. **Drift** — per-row-third mean horizontal displacement from spawn: pyr50 bottom/mid 3.04/2.92 cm, flat from f180 through f599 to the second decimal; top third ~5.4 cm with ±0.1 wobble caused by the stragglers below. pyr110: 13.1/11.4/12.4 cm, bottom/mid flat from f240. All of it is settle-phase accumulation; post-freeze creep is zero. The magnitude comparison against the old behavior still needs a prior-commit build (the old end state was 100% frozen too, so its drift also plateaued — the question (a) answers is *how far rows slide before freezing*, and the before-pair was never recorded).
2. **Ceiling** — base 120 at 15 iterations still collapses: 6091 of 7260 scattered, apex −49 m, and the lever-5 signature reproduced exactly (nearly settles — 6.7% frozen at f180 — then buckles). The ceiling is dual-bound; (a) does not move it, as predicted.
3. **Pile invariants** — settle plateau from ~f120, apex drop stable at 1.491 m, 1 of 216 shed past 2 m, drift flat at 2.5 cm.
4. **The new defect: a permanent straggler class.** The pre-change baseline slept 100% with zero transitions; (a) leaves a small set that never sleeps. pyr50: the two apex blocks (rows 48–49) change position every frame f400–599 at up to 0.11 m/s. pyr110: seven bodies (the apex pair plus a row-38 block at 0.21). Pile: 17 of 216 simmering at up to 0.56 m/s, frozen fraction 92.1% and slightly *falling* late (0.926 → 0.921). Bulk sleep is intact (pyr50 99.8% frozen, pyr110 99.9%) and the bulk-solve work counts are flat-to-better — `gs_color_iter` ran in 192 of 600 frames at pyr50 (180 recorded pre-change), 305 at pyr110 (pre-change slept by f360), 600 at the 120 collapse — but the stragglers keep the serial solve path and their pairs' narrow phase alive forever. The mechanism is the one the implementation paragraph predicted: light-load contacts (apex pairs — small normal lambda ⇒ small cone) cross the cone boundary repeatedly, the reset event re-arms on every other crossing, and each reset injects a micro-slip that keeps the pair rocking. That makes experiment (b) — hysteresis on the saturation edge (enter sticking below the cone with margin, exit only on sustained exceedance, so a light contact cannot re-arm the reset every crossing) — the concrete, now-measured next move rather than a speculative one.

Dump-analysis note for future rounds: velocity-based settle scans are unreliable under island sleeping — a frozen body keeps its last nonzero velocity in the dump (the known retained gap), so pyr110 reads a stale 0.49 m/s "final max speed" while 99.9% frozen. Freeze-fraction from position bits is the authoritative settle instrument.

**Experiment (b) implemented 2026-08-12, both backends, awaiting its round.** A magnitude dead band on the saturation state, in the two store sites only (`end_frame::store`, `vbd_update_sticking.slang`) — no new state, no consumer changes, no ABI motion: saturation still *enters* only at the cone (`λt ≥ bound`, preserving exactly one TGS reset per genuine slip event), but *exits* only when the contact clearly relaxes (`λt < 0.9·bound`); between the two, `saturated` holds its previous value via the bit-1 memory that already existed for edge detection. `sticking` becomes the complement (`engaged ∧ gap_ok ∧ ¬saturated`) so the flags stay mutually exclusive — its only behavioral delta vs (a) is the row floor reading `penalty_min` inside the dead band after a saturation, which the mechanism map already established is inert. `keep_anchor` keeps its exact formula (`engaged ∧ gap_ok ∧ (¬saturated ∨ prev_saturated)`). Why this kills the measured chatter: the (a) limit cycle was enter-at-bound → reset → relax a hair under → exit immediately → re-enter → reset again, one micro-slip injected per cycle; with the band, the hair-under state stays saturated, so the next touch of the bound has `prev_saturated` set and injects nothing — the contact sits anchored at ≤ μN until it genuinely relaxes below 0.9·bound, and can finally calm below the sleep threshold. Full-swing oscillators (the pile boulders that unload completely each rock) legitimately exit the band and still earn one reset per swing — degrading toward the pre-(a) continuous re-form, which demonstrably slept. The 0.9 factor is a raw mirrored literal in both store sites, like the other thresholds; promote it to `solver_config` only if the round demands tuning sweeps (that is an ABI-growing shader_struct change — do it deliberately, both backends in one rebuild). Success criteria for the round: late-window movers go to zero on pyr50/pyr110 (was 3 and 7), pile frozen fraction returns to ~100% with no late decline (was 92.1% falling), drift stays flat, invariants intact, 120 at 15 iterations presumably still collapses (dual-bound — not (b)'s job), determinism ×2, and every hash re-baselines yet again (the dead band changes trajectories everywhere).

**(b) measured 2026-08-12, same load-immune protocol — partial success at small scale, a new failure mode at 110, verdict: the lever is answered and the answer is no.** Determinism ×2 held (pyr50 `0x7a886eb22e9b8741`, pile `0x12ef5341cfeaa4c5`, pyr110 `0xfbcd04e609e0286b`, 120 `0xe569dd76775f4119`; drop bit-identical to the (a) round — no contact ever enters the dead band there, correctly a no-op). pyr50: bulk settle *faster than pre-change* (`gs_color_iter` 173 of 600 vs 180 pre / 192 under (a); 99.6% frozen at f180 vs 86.4%), drift 2.77/2.85 bottom/mid (vs 3.04/2.92), but the apex pair still chatters — row 48 intermittent at 164 of 200 late frames instead of continuous. Pile: improved end state, 95.4% frozen rising monotonically (was 92.1% falling). **pyr110: froze fully by ~f360, then the entire single 6105-body island mass-rewoke** — frozen fraction 0.568 at f480, every block moved in the f400–599 window, re-frozen 99.8% by f599; `gs_color_iter` 452 vs 305, and the awake-frame burn doubled the measured p50 (47 → 115–136 ms, load-independent conclusion since run-to-run was 47.32/47.25 under identical load in the (a) round). 120 still collapses (5032 shed, apex −67.7).

Why hysteresis cannot fix the apex pair: it is a *full-swing* oscillator — the friction bound is one block's weight times μ, so λt traverses the entire [0.9·bound, bound) band every rock cycle, exits legitimately, and re-arms the reset. Magnitude hysteresis debounces boundary *hover*, not full unload-reload cycles. Why 110 mass-rewoke (leading hypothesis, fits all three scales but not separately instrumented): a held anchor carries its accumulated slip gap — up to `stick_threshold` — and the post-stabilize α=0 pass applies `clamp(penalty·gap, μN)` tangentially toward the stale grip point every substep. Pre-(a), continuous re-anchoring kept that gap ≈ 0, so the force did not exist and passive damping won (the pre-change solver slept 100% with zero transitions — measured). Held anchors store lateral tension in the settled structure; near-sleeping stragglers keep injecting micro-slips; at 110 rows the release popped a body over `wake_thresh` once, and island-wide wake plus a 6105-body island did the rest.

**The refutation, stated for the record like lever 5's:** the event-driven re-form family (hold grip through saturation, reset on events) trades the hypothesized settle-phase ratchet for measured sleep pathologies — permanent light-contact chatter under (a), structure-scale rewake under (b) — and sleep is the load-bearing optimization of this entire phase. The hypothesized benefit was never demonstrated: the pre-change drift baseline does not exist (pre-change scenes end 100% frozen, so their drift plateaus identically), meaning the settle-phase ratchet creep this lever targeted was never actually observed in GSE. The revert was applied the same day: all five files restored to pre-(a) text exactly (single-bool `sticking`, recycle keyed on it, no bit packing), verified free of orphaned references. Because the restoration is semantically byte-exact, the verification round has exact expected values: pyr50 must hash back to `0xbbcdd5c793498629` and the pile to `0xf27ecd23b4f556cf`, with pyr50 100% frozen from ~f240 and zero late-window movers. If the lever is ever reopened, the root-cause-shaped experiment is (c): exempt the tangential rows of held-saturated anchors from the α=0 stabilize pass (never position-correct accumulated dynamic-friction slip) — a solve-core change on both backends, priced accordingly, and only worth it with a demonstrated creep problem in hand.

**Measure (original scope).** Lateral drift per row over time on the pyramid at base 50 and 110 at 15 iterations; the 15-iteration ceiling (does 120 stand?) as a secondary; pile settle invariants. GPU note: `vbd_update_sticking.slang` mirrors the CPU test — any semantic change must land on both backends or it widens the Phase 2 gap; this one landed on both in the same change, including the bit-2 asymmetry noted above.

### 5. Sweep ordering — ANSWERED, and the answer is no

Implemented as the plan specified (serial height-ordered Gauss-Seidel, direction alternating each iteration, behind `Physics.use_ordered_sweep` so the comparison runs without rebuilds) and measured on 2026-08-11. Both success criteria failed:

| experiment | result |
|---|---|
| base 50, ordered, 15 iters | stands, sleeps — quality-neutral vs colored (same frozen state) |
| base 50, ordered, 4 iters | **collapses** — slow slump to ~12 rows of rubble. "4 ordered ≈ 15 unordered" is false |
| base 120, ordered, 15 iters | settles *deeper* than colored (f240: 32.9% asleep, max 0.23) then buckles anyway — ceiling unmoved |
| serial cost | ~6.5× colored wall time at base 120 (1.2 s vs 185 ms per settle frame) |

The tridiagonal analogy fails for a specific reason worth keeping: sweep order propagates **positions** in one pass, but the thing a tall stack actually needs is **lambda growing into the resting load**, and the dual update plus penalty ramp advance once per iteration regardless of order. The ceiling is dual-bound. The confirmation is in the ceiling table above: iterations (more dual updates) and substeps (dual updates plus a larger h²-scaled floor) both stand base 120 where ordering could not, and iterations at 40 stand base 180.

The mode stays behind its setting as a diagnostic instrument — it is fully deterministic, which the comparator likes — but do not invest in parallelising it. Two implementation notes for whoever touches it: the sweep must include unconstrained bodies (the colored path's catch-all newton-steps every active body, and that inertia-only step is what applies full gravity when `accel_weight` lags — filtering them out reintroduces the GPU's half-gravity class), and coloring must still run because `compute_coloring` is what builds the per-body constraint lists the stepper reads.

**The higher-ceiling alternative** — multilevel/multigrid on the contact graph — survives this refutation and is strengthened by it: coarsening moves *load* (the dual) globally per cycle, which is exactly the bottleneck ordering could not touch. Still genuinely a research project; now it is also clearly the only structural way to stand tall stacks at *low* iteration counts, should that ever matter more than the dial does.

## Open leads, priced (2026-08-12 EOD)

Every open thread in one place so none is lost; the statuses are decisions, not a queue.

**The gating decision — production-solver policy.** Which solver is authoritative, per platform and per scene class, once the GPU reaches parity. Every remaining CPU-perf item prices off this, so it should be made deliberately, not inherited. The structural reasons the CPU likely stays default-authoritative even at full parity: the GPU solver has **no sleeping** (an idle world burns the full solve every frame, on the same GPU the renderer is fighting); gameplay reads eat the ~2-frame snapshot readback lag; and server/headless simulation, min-spec fallback, and cross-vendor lockstep determinism are all CPU-shaped. Independent of parity, the GPU has **scale gaps**: `max_contacts` 262,144 against ~360k contacts at 16k, and `warm_start_lookup` is a linear per-contact scan over the warm-start buffer — quadratic-shaped at scale. Parity on the 217-body ladder is therefore not replacement at 16k. Acceptance bar for any parity claim: the shadow-step regime, per-backend run-to-run bit-stability, the ladder invariants — and the 16k capacity asterisk stated out loud.

**The fully-asleep fast path (2a) is IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-13, awaiting its round.** It landed as a *call-level* guard at the top of `update_vbd`, not a per-substep one, on a proven invariant: the all-asleep predicate cannot change within one call — impulses apply (and zero counters) before the guard, motor components are frozen during the run, and sleep counters only change at writeback — so if substep 0 would skip, every substep would, and the whole call early-returns before the body build, `mass_properties_of` pass, and staging maps. The predicate: no motor with `|velocity_drive_target| > 0.01 m/s` (conservative mirror of the motor-wake condition — a live drive just declines the skip and takes the normal path, which then fires the real wake), and every dynamic body's persistent sleep counter at or over `vbd::limits.sleep_threshold` (a dynamic body missing a transform or counter entry conservatively blocks the skip). Why skipping is exact: a fully-asleep world is a fixed point of today's update — no awake member exists to drive island wake, motors skip sleeping bodies, driven joints between sleeping pairs do nothing, spawns arrive with zero counters (blocking the skip), and the recorded no-wake gaps (support removal, injected velocity, kinematic edits of sleeping bodies) behave identically frozen under both paths. Skipping `begin_frame` also freezes the cache clock (`advance()` never runs), so every entry stays at emit-gate distance 1 and wake resumes replay with warm starts intact. Deferred side effects checked one by one: collision results and airborne flags keep their frozen values (today they are cleared and identically recomputed), joint `rest_orientation` first-init defers to the first real substep and computes the same value from frozen orientations. The skip is visible as the `vbd_cpu::asleep_skip` trace tag — its frame count is the verification instrument. CPU-only by design: the fast path is the sleeping feature's capstone and the GPU solver has no sleeping to skip. **Verification protocol note:** a strict before/after hash pair is not available — the shapes/inertia work re-baselined all trajectories in the same window — so the round instead verifies: determinism ×2 at the new hashes, `asleep_skip` frame count matching the dump's fully-frozen frame count, bitwise-frozen positions through the entire skip window, and sane settle invariants; a strict pair remains possible by building once with the guard hunk stashed, if wanted.

**VERIFIED 2026-08-13 (cold machine, dump-v2 64-byte records — offline parsers must detect stride).** Determinism ×2 on every scenario at the new-tree hashes (drop `0x7cc260431836eef4`, pyr50 `0x5e425d736cc5a274`, pile `0x8484552e6931abd7`, 16k `0x9fc86d4b31fdfeef`). The skip engages exactly on the frozen tail and nowhere else: pyr50 last position-bit change at f231 then 368 frames bitwise-frozen with **zero wake events**; 16k last change f277 then 322 frames frozen, zero wake events; both stand with 0 shed. Frame medians: **pyr50 4.6 → 0.64–0.75 ms, 16k 76 → 5.4 ms (~14×), drop 0.097 → 0.084–0.088** — the remaining 16k asleep frame is the rest of the engine plus the ~2.5 ms bench dump-writer, with `vbd_cpu::update` p50 at 291–456 µs (the guard scan over 16k bodies; optimizable via a cached awake-count if it ever matters). Two instrument notes: the `asleep_skip` tag is invisible in profile txts — a zero-duration span is filtered — so **`vbd_cpu::update` p50 is the skip instrument** (µs-scale median = skipping; ms-scale = not); and two observations belonging to the shapes/inertia arc, not this change: the 16k settles later on the new-tensor trajectory (f277 vs the f205–242 era, settle p95 ~850 ms), and **the pile never reaches full sleep on this tree** — diagnosed in full on 2026-08-13, and the first-guess attribution ("real-tensor rolling") was WRONG. The dumps say: the late movers **chatter in place** (mean speeds 0.05–0.2 m/s but net displacement 2–55 *millimeters* and net rotation ≈ 1° over 200 frames — millimeter-amplitude oscillation, ~99% of the velocity is direction-flipping), they are not rolling; of 35 movers, 17 are linear-blocked, 9 angular-blocked, 9 below both thresholds (island-calm/counter-blocked) — the thresholds are not mis-tuned, the bodies genuinely vibrate above them. A 2400-frame run shows decay 110/61/34/27/17/9 movers at f120–1000, then **exactly 4 bodies flatlined from f1500 past f2100**, last change f2371 — not a permanent limit cycle, but ~4× the pre-change time-to-sleep with a pathological quartet tail; functionally never on gameplay timescales. Attribution: the pile is **216 uniform cubes**, so the inertia-tensor fix is a mathematical no-op for it — the tensor change is excluded, and the regression window (08-12 21:00 pile slept 100% bit-exact at `0xf27ecd23…` → 08-13 09:31 chatters) contains the SAT axis-preference rewrite and COM rung 1. Prime suspect: **SAT axis flips at near-degenerate face-face contacts** — a settled pile is wall-to-wall arbitrary-angle face contacts where the preferred axis can flip frame-to-frame, churning normals and warm-start feature keys (cold restart + micro-impulse per flip); the pyramid, also cubes but axis-aligned by symmetry, still sleeps at f231, consistent with the mechanism only firing at arbitrary angles. Handed to the shapes arc as a spawned task with the contact_trace instrument suggestion (watch a straggler's feature keys/normals for frame-to-frame churn) and the success criterion (100% sleep within ~600 frames at unchanged thresholds — do not paper over with threshold bumps; the energy source is the defect).

**RESOLVED 2026-08-13 — diagnosis corrected, fix landed, verification round pending.** The archived per-round dumps overturn both the window and the suspect. The CPU SAT axis machinery is byte-identical since July 30 (`NarrowPhaseCollisions.cppm` untouched; the tolerance rewrite exists only in the slang mirror, which `parity_pile_cpu` never executes), and the whole 08-13 tree delta is bit-inert on the pile — `i1_pile` (08-12 16:00) ≡ `fp_pile` (08-13 09:32) ≡ current, all 600 frames position-bit-identical. The failure is older and systemic: `pile_gate2` (08-11, lever-1 era) is the only archived run that ever slept (100% by f511); every re-roll since fails — `pile_flat2` (lever 3) 18 movers @f599, lever-4 `pile_a` 17, post-revert `hb` 10, `i1`/`fp`/current 27 — each round accepted on hash determinism while the sleep invariant went unchecked. The mechanism, verified in source and consistent with every dump: the broadphase x-sort puts near-equal-`min.x` pairs (vertically stacked columns — exactly the observed straggler tower at y 1.25/1.75/2.25/2.75) on a knife edge, so the pair's slot order flips under micrometer drift; `build_clipped_face_contacts` broke the reference-face tie at 1e-4, so the reference face follows the slot (and independently flips when the alignment difference crosses ±1e-4); a reference flip rewrites every manifold point's `feature_id` (reference clip-edge tags vs incident vertex ordinals — face↔vertex type swaps on the same physical point), the entity-canonicalized cache lookup misses, and the loaded support contact restarts at the penalty floor — the measured ~10-frame sag-catch cycle (synchronized vy bursts to −0.15 m/s down the column). Two discriminating probes on the current binary: `Physics.solver_iterations=40` converges through the cold restarts and sleeps the pile by f704 (movers 17/7/4 at f240/400/599 vs 61/35/26 at 15) — unconverged-residual energy, not thresholds; `use_ordered_sweep=true` chatters identically to f1553 — roll-robust, no reorder rescues it. Fix: the reference face is preference-stable — `prefer_reference_a = body_a < body_b` threads from the pair loop (CPU) and the narrow-phase kernel (GPU, same body indices, so both backends prefer the same physical box) into `build_clipped_face_contacts`, and the 1e-4 tie-break became a 0.02 alignment-preference margin (frame-to-frame Δdot noise is ≲1e-3; a genuinely better face still wins beyond the margin). Every trajectory re-baselines again, both backends. Round: `parity_pile_cpu` 2400f ×2 — success is 100% sleep by ~f600, deterministic hash, and gate2-class settle (single-digit movers by f240); pyr50 must still settle ~f231 and sleep (axis-aligned Δdot is exactly 0 ≤ margin, so the canonical box is now always reference — a new valid roll, not a defect signature).

**Round 1 verdict: churn fix necessary but not sufficient — the remaining pump is the warm-start reuse gate.** Pile deterministic ×2 (`0x796757369dfde77e`, dumps bit-identical), pathological tail gone (full sleep f1548, no flatlined quartet) but 25 movers at f599; pyr50's re-roll surfaced 4 apex-cluster movers (rows 47–49 — the lever-4 item-8 light-contact class) through f599. The stragglers in both scenes are angular micro-rockers (av bursts 0.05–0.29 rad/s over the angular gate while linear stays under 0.05). Mechanism: `reuse_cached_normal = λ[0] < −1e-3 ∨ gap < −1e-4` is phase-correlated with the rock — a contact point reads loaded-or-penetrating on its compressed frames (reuses the ramped cached penalty → stiff) and light-and-separated on its released frames (cold floor → soft). Stiff-compression/soft-release stiffness modulation is a parametric pump: net positive work every cycle, which is exactly the residual it=40 grinds through. The gate also fought the solver's own design — the penalty pass already decays inactive contacts *gradually* (γ per substep clamped at the activity-gated floor), while the rebuild path crashed them to the floor instantly: two derivations of one fact, disagreeing. Fix, both backends: the rebuild now always restores cached penalties (`max(cached, floor)`, all three rows) when an entry exists; λ reuse keeps the exact old gates (their load-bearing job is blocking attractive normal λ on separated contacts; penalty is force-neutral at separation by the `min(f, 0)` clamp, entering only the Hessian). Also restructured the reference-preference plumbing per the no-threaded-bool-params rule: `shape_data` carries `body_index`, `generate_shape_manifold` derives the preference internally, and only the raw-geometry helpers take the flag.

**Round 2 verdict: pump kill CONFIRMED on the pyramid, unconditional restore REFUTED on the pile — penalty memory needs a distance band.** pyr50 sleeps hard (settle f240–288, zero movers from f300) — the apex chatter is gone, proving the reuse-gate pump was its energy source. The pile went the other way, deterministically (`0x4c0dec770fb845b5` ×2, dumps bit-identical): early settle far MORE agitated (173/130 movers at f120/f180 vs 107/48) and a 4–5-mover tail persisting through f2399. Mechanism of the harm: restored penalties enter the Hessian even at zero force (the accumulate skip needs `penalty < 1e-6`, and the floor is 1 N/m, so only floor-level rows are effectively negligible) — during the pile's chaotic settle every genuinely-separated speculative pair (gaps to the 20 mm margin) dragged stale ramped stiffness into the Newton conditioning, over-damping steps exactly when convergence is scarcest. The lattice pyramid has no speculative-pair soup, so it only saw the benefit. Refinement, both backends: penalties restore when `λ[0] < −1e-3 ∨ gap < stick_threshold` — the grip envelope (1 cm) swallows the whole observed rock amplitude (≤4 mm), so stiffness stays continuous through the cycle and the pump stays dead, while clearly-separated pairs stay at the floor and contribute no Hessian.

**Rounds 3–4 closed the warm-start question: the pile is convergence-bound at 15 iterations, full stop.** Banded all-rows restore (R3): pyramid perfect (f288), pile 30@f599 with a 15-mover plateau. Banded normal-only restore (R4): pile recovers to 22@f599/sleep f2084 but the pyramid apex chatter RETURNS — the apex cure needs tangential stiffness continuity too. Across pre-fix, churn-only, and normal-only configs the pile reads 22–26 movers at f599 invariantly; only iterations=40 ever collapsed it (4@f599, sleep f704). Decision (user-approved): keep the R3 configuration and implement open-lead #2, the **adaptive iteration budget** — `iterations` (15) stays the floor, new `solver_config::max_iterations` (40) is the cap, and after the floor the loop breaks on absolute convergence (`convergence_threshold_linear/angular`, tightened 0.01→1e-4 m and 0.01→1e-3 rad — the dual update's α-relaxed |c| reads ~1% of held penetration, so settled scenes sit at tens of µm and break at the floor for zero extra cost) or on a stalled trend (<1% improvement on both axes per iteration, the plan's global-trend-only caution — protects overload plateaus from burning the cap). CPU-only: the GPU dispatch stream stays fixed-count; recorded as a follow-up if GPU settle scenes ever need it. `min_iterations` remains dead. Standing-verdict round still owed: pyr110/120 stand, 16k settle/asleep cost, drift invariants.

**Round 5 (adaptive budget + R3 restore): SHIPPED STATE.** Pile: 96/38/24 movers at f120/180/240, 8 at f599 (96.3% asleep), 100% sleep from f1073, deterministic ×2 (`0xc2efec09f7b2f363`), p50 0.22–0.32 ms (asleep-dominated), settle p95 6.5–6.9 ms (extended-budget frames, transient). The final four stragglers are one vertical tower (y 1.75–2.75, including original-diagnosis owner …445967) whose envelope now DECAYS to death (v 0.21→0.10→frozen) — genuine damped relaxation of the slowest structure, not the flatline chatter class, which is gone. pyr50: settles **f66** (historical best was f231 — the converged transient stops the settle bouncing itself), zero movers after. pyr110: sleeps from f231, stands (apex 108.91, normal compression), asleep p50 2.1 ms, settle p95 312 ms — the priced settle cost at 6105 bodies. Every energy-source defect is dead: reference-face re-key churn (fix 1), the reuse-gate stiffness pump (fix 2, proven by the pyramid flipping broken/fixed with the restore in rounds 1–4), and the convergence deficit (fix 3). Remaining tail = real physics of tower rocking at correct cube inertia. Still owed before the arc fully closes: pyr120-stands check, 16k cost round (settle will pay the extended budget — watch `gs_color_iter` and whether the stall-break holds it near the floor once calm), drift invariants, and the `parity_drop` canary re-baseline (all trajectories re-rolled).

**Constants exposure + sweep (2026-08-13).** Every AVBD-reference constant is now a `Physics.*` setting (unit-typed, one derivation in `solver_config_from_settings`, `prepare` reconfigures unconditionally — hot-reloadable, per-run sweepable). The 21-config sweep over pile-1200f/pyr50-400f found: **`speculative_margin` is the master settle lever — 0.02→0.01 sleeps the pile at f64** (from f1073; pyr50 bit-identical; p95 7.5→1.5 ms) but deepens the parity_drop impact spike 27→38 mm at 5.7 m/s, so the adoptable form is a **per-pair velocity-scaled margin**, not a smaller static; `collision_margin` 2 mm (pile f446, overlap recovery f478) and `stick_threshold` 2 cm (pile f433, pyr bit-identical) are strong default candidates with visible-gap / grip-envelope trades; `solver_gamma` is second-order; and `solver_alpha` (unread under post_stabilize), `solver_beta` (floor-dominated), `penalty_max` (unreached), and the convergence thresholds within 5e-5..5e-4 (stall-clause-dominated) are **bit-inert** on cube scenes — recorded so nobody re-sweeps them. Winner determinism ×2 held; quantity settings parse bare canonical-unit CLI values.

**Velocity-scaled speculative margin SHIPPED (2026-08-13, three gate rounds).** Final form, both backends: per-pair window = `clamp(closing_speed × sub_dt × 2, speculative_margin_floor, speculative_margin)`, closing speed = relative velocity projected onto the center line clamped at zero (|v_rel| fallback under 1e-6 m center distance). Two refinements the rounds forced, both now design rules: the floor must exceed settling pairs' gap-oscillation amplitude (2 mm left a 7-mover tail from window-boundary contact churn — the third instance of the envelope-covers-oscillation rule; 10 mm, the sweep's static optimum, is the default), and raw |v_rel| scaling is insufficient (fall-phase windows open to the cap and reproduce baseline landing dynamics — the closing-speed projection is what narrows tangentially-jostling pairs while keeping head-on approaches at full width). Gate: pile 100% asleep from **f653** (arc: f2371 at diagnosis → f1073 after the three-part fix → f653 now), 4 movers at f599, deterministic ×2; `parity_drop` bit-identical to the pre-campaign baseline (27 mm spike); pyr50 bit-identical, settles f66; pyr110 stands, sleeps f231. GPU narrow phase mirrored; broadphase/grid stay on the cap as the conservative superset. The pile criterion (100% sleep within ~600 frames) is met within measurement grain; the owed standing-verdict round (pyr120, 16k cost, drift, canary re-baseline) still stands.

**Convergence levers — shared-algorithm, pay on both backends:**

1. **Lambda pre-seeding at spawn — IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-13 behind `Physics.lambda_pre_seed` (default OFF), awaiting its round.** A topological pass down the support graph seeds each fresh resting contact's normal lambda with the static load above it; the dual starts at the answer and settle collapses to error-correction. Physically legitimate prestressed initialization — the AL does not care where the multiplier starts. NOT the refuted load-aware floor: that manipulated stiffness, this initializes the multiplier. The implementation: `solver::seed_static_loads` runs inside `solve()` between `warm_contacts` (which computes the approach speeds the gate reads) and `contact_penalty` (whose mass-scaled normal floor then activates on the seeded lambda). Eligibility per constraint: **fresh** (bit 2 of the `replayed` u32, set by the narrow-phase build path when the cache had nothing — the replay path stays 1, and the end_frame store-skip now masks `& 1u` so a fresh-bit contact on a cache-cold sleeping pair still takes the load-bearing touch-then-store fallback), **near-rest** (`approach_speed > −0.2 m/s` — impacts fail the gate, which is what separates this from the load-aware floor's failure mode: no impulsive-load confusion is possible because impact-born contacts are never seeded), and **support-aligned** (`|normal.y| > 0.7`, the grounded-check threshold). The pass: early-out scan for any candidate (zero cost on non-spawn substeps), then per-body support lists, bodies sorted by descending height with index tie-break (deterministic), and a single descent accumulating `load = own weight (if affected_by_gravity) + carried load`, split equally across a body's support points; eligible contacts get `lambda[0] = −share`, and the share flows to the lower body through every support contact, seeded or not, so load propagates correctly through warm contacts too. Once-only by construction: next substep the contact is cached, bit 2 unset. Seeding is normal-only (no tangential prestress — lateral direction unknown; the seeded normal instantly gives friction its cone, which is most of the lateral benefit anyway).
