**The scoreboard, and it is not a win yet.** Three runs per configuration, `stress` on Vulkan, warmup 0, 120 frames:

| configuration | onset frame (3 pairs) | worst delta at onset | bodies differing at frame 119 |
|---|---|---|---|
| float baseline | 60 / 65 / 91 | 4.2e-4 / 1.9e-6 / 4.9e-7 m | 886 / 885 / 340 of 1077 |
| + fixed point | 35 / 35 / 35 | 7.0e-3 / 9.0e-5 / 7.0e-3 m | 884 / 884 / 884 |
| + restitution tiebreak | 10 / 42 / 10 | 6.0e-8 / 5.4e-6 / 6.0e-8 m | 886 / 884 / 887 |

**`stress` is still nondeterministic, and the final-frame divergence is unchanged** — any seed, however small, amplifies to the same metre-scale chaos over 120 frames, so from the gate's point of view nothing has improved. What has changed is the character of the residual: it is now a **one-ULP** perturbation instead of a millimetre one, and it first appears at **frame 10 on two settling box-grid boxes** (`Grid L0R0C5` and `Grid L1R0C5` at x≈20, z≈-4.5) rather than in a tumbler. Do not read the earlier onset as a regression — it is a different trajectory, and the baseline's box grid had already frozen before its frame-60 seed.

**Cost.** `stress` p50 moved 19.6 ms (baseline) → 19.8 (instrument only) → 25.4 (fixed point) → 31.0 (restitution run, single sample and possibly contaminated — do not quote it without a median of three). The likely driver is register pressure: 33 `int64_t` accumulators per thread. If this direction is kept, exploit the symmetry of `hess` and `ang_hess` to store 6 elements instead of 9, which cuts 12 accumulators.

**Next, and it is now a bounded task rather than a hunt.** Two defects of the *same class* have been found by the same reasoning — a serial or per-body consumer reading a list built by lock-free atomic append. Audit every remaining pass for that shape rather than guessing: `vbd_update_sticking`, `vbd_freeze_jacobians`, `vbd_derive_velocities`, `vbd_apply_impulses`, the joint loop inside `solve_body`, and `warm_start_lookup`, which returns the **first** match while walking the previous frame's adjacency and then replaces the contact anchors. The other open thread is unchanged and still unmeasured: a **position-free, full-content contact hash** to settle once and for all whether the per-body contact multiset is identical between runs.

### The order-dependence audit: every remaining pass cleared, and a vacuous instrument found (2026-08-14)

**The shape being hunted**, from the two defects already found: a *consumer* that walks a list built by lock-free atomic append and whose output depends on the walk order — a float accumulation, a serial mutation, a first-match selection, or an unstable sort. Note the distinction that makes the audit tractable: `collision_grid_build`, `collision_broad_phase` and `collision_narrow_phase` are **producers** of nondeterministic order and are already documented as such; a producer is only a defect if some consumer is order-sensitive. The audit is therefore over consumers.

**Cleared — per-element maps.** One thread per element, output a function of that element's own data only, so no order exists to depend on: `vbd_update_sticking` (writes `contact_data[ci].sticking`), `vbd_freeze_jacobians` (writes `frozen_jacobians[ci]`, and it is indexed by the same `ci` the solve reads, so the permutation is consistent), `vbd_update_lambda` (writes `contact_data[ci].lambda/penalty`; its only atomic is a commutative `InterlockedMax`), `vbd_update_joint_lambda` (one thread per joint, writes only `joint_data[ji]`; its four loops are over the three constraint axes, not a list; all eleven atomics are `InterlockedMax`), and `vbd_derive_velocities`, `vbd_finalize`, `vbd_apply_jacobi`, `vbd_apply_body_inputs`, which are per-body and touch only `body_data[bi]`.

**Cleared — order-independent by disjunction.** `vbd_predict` walks `contact_adjacency` and `joint_adjacency`, which looks like the shape, but both loops compute *"wake if any neighbour is awake and moving"*. The result is a disjunction, so the `break` is an early exit rather than a selection and the outcome cannot depend on walk order. Its two motor loops iterate a host-dense array. Same reasoning clears `collision_build_coloring`, which accumulates `neighbor_colors` as a bitmask OR.

**Cleared — the joint loop, because its sort key is total, and this is the structural point.** The joint loop inside `solve_body` genuinely has the shape: it accumulates into `grad`/`hess`/`ang_grad`/`ang_hess`/`hess_xt` in float while walking `joint_adjacency`, and the fixed-point conversion does not cover it. It is nonetheless safe, because the joint sort at `collision_build_adjacency:189` breaks ties on **`ji_p > ji_a`** — the joint index, which is host-stable and unique — making the sorted order canonical. **The contact sort at `:164` cannot do the same, because its equivalent tiebreaker would be `ci`, which is assigned by the wave-cooperative atomic allocator and is nondeterministic; it therefore stops at `feature_key` and can tie.** That asymmetry is exactly why contacts needed the fixed-point fix and joints did not. Converting the joint loop is still worth doing for uniformity, but it is not a live defect.

**Exonerated by measurement, with a positive control.** `warm_start_lookup` returns the **first** entry matching `(body_a, body_b, feature_key)` while walking the previous frame's adjacency, then `reuse_cached_sticking` replaces the contact anchors with the cached ones — a genuine path from list order into contact geometry, and the last named suspect. Instrumented inside the function to count matches rather than return on the first: **zero ambiguous lookups in every generation**, against a denominator of **508 to 3928 successful lookups per generation**. The first match is the only match, so the walk order cannot change what it returns. Also exonerated: the restitution overflow path at `vbd_apply_restitution:80-89`, which walks raw `ci` order from a single thread but is never reached — queue depth peaks at **199 against a capacity of 2048**.

**A latent defect found, not active here.** `vbd_apply_impulses` does `body.velocity += imp.delta_velocity` as an **unsynchronised read-modify-write** on `body_data[imp.body_index]`, one thread per impulse. Two impulses naming the same body in one dispatch would both lose an update and depend on execution order. `impulse_count` is zero on `stress`, so it cannot be the current source, but it should be fixed before the impulse API is used in anger.

**A vacuous instrument found, and it retracts a recorded elimination.** `state_contact_duplicate_index` (slot 9) is read by the host at `GpuSolver.cpp:1197` and printed as `dup` in the `vbd gpu peak` line — but **no shader anywhere writes it.** It is zeroed by `collision_reset` each substep and read back as zero forever. **Every `dup 0` ever quoted, including this document's own "Eliminated: … duplicate contacts (`dup 0`)", measured nothing.** The underlying question is now genuinely answered by the warm-start counter above: no two contacts of a pair share a feature key on this scene. This is the fifth instrument in the campaign to report a confident number about something it was not measuring, and the cheapest possible guard is the one used here — **always publish a denominator next to a zero.**

**What the audit means: it did not find the remaining source, and that is informative.** Every order-sensitive consumer in the VBD shader set is now either fixed, cleared by construction, or exonerated by a counter with a positive control. The residual one-ULP divergence at frame 10 therefore does **not** come from a list-order consumer inside these passes. What remains, in order of preference: the **contact multiset genuinely differing between runs**, which a position-free full-content contact hash has still never tested and which is now the single most valuable unmeasured quantity in the campaign; the joint and motor accumulations, for completeness; and anything outside the VBD shaders entirely, including the host-side kinematic-wall upload that drives the tumblers.

### The contact hash exonerates the narrow phase, and the body hash was blind to rotation all along (2026-08-14)

**The measurement this document has asked for three times, finally taken.** A **position-free, full-content contact hash**: computed *inside* `collision_narrow_phase` at the point each contact is written, over every field the solver consumes (bodies, feature key, sticking, replayed, normal, both tangents, both anchors, `c0`, initial lambda and penalty, friction, restitution, penalty floor, approach speed), combined with `InterlockedAdd` so it is commutative and therefore compares the **multiset**, and mixing neither `ci` nor the adjacency position `k`. Two guards worth copying: zeros are normalised (`v == 0.0 ? 0u : asuint(v)`) so a `-0.0`/`+0.0` split cannot raise a false alarm, and a **contact-count denominator** is published beside it so a "hashes match" reading can never be vacuous.

**Paired against an order-insensitive body hash on the same clock**, added to `collision_grid_build` — one thread per body, summed, keyed by `bi`, and dispatched at the *start* of the tick, before broad and narrow phase. Result: **the contact hash and the body hash first differ at the same generation (10), while the contact count stays identical until generation 27.** Same pairs, different content, for seventeen generations. Because the body hash is read before the collision phase even begins, **the bodies entering that tick already differ**, so the contact-content difference is a consequence and not a cause. **The narrow phase is exonerated, and the previous round's reframe — "the narrow phase is producing different contact geometry for the same input depending on scheduling" — is retracted.** It was an inference from hashes that all included list position; the position-free measurement does not support it.

**The instrument was blind in exactly the channel that diverges, and this is the important finding.** `vbd_hash_state` — the bodies-only hash behind every "everything upstream is bit-identical through stage N" claim in this document — hashed `position`, `predicted_position`, `velocity`, `orientation` and `angular_velocity`, and **omitted `predicted_orientation`**, which is precisely the field the angular half of `solve_body` writes (`:599`). The solve could therefore diverge rotationally and stay invisible until `vbd_derive_velocities` converted `predicted_orientation` into `angular_velocity`, which *is* hashed. That is why the blind hash reported `s1[5]` as the first differing slot and pointed at `derive_velocities` — a pass the audit had already cleared as a pure per-body map, which was the tell. **`derive_velocities` was the messenger, not the source.** The hash now covers `predicted_orientation`, `accel_weight`, `sleep_counter` and `locked`, and combines additively through a nonlinear mix instead of a bare XOR of per-field products, which could cancel. **Every "identical through stage N" statement in this document predating this fix was blind to rotation and should be re-derived before being relied on.**

**With the corrected instrument the answer moves one stage earlier and becomes extremely stable.** Across five pairs, with and without joints, the first differing slot set is **always exactly `s1[4]` through `s1[8]`, and never any `s0` slot**. Two facts fall straight out. First, **the introduction is in substep 1 and never substep 0** — substep 0's entire pipeline is bit-identical at the onset generation every time. Second, the stage-3-to-stage-4 bracket contains exactly one thing: **the solve iteration loop** (`GpuSolver.cpp:1789-1834` — per-colour `solve_color` dispatches, `update_lambda`, `update_joint_lambda`, `convergence_check`). The instrument is validated rather than assumed: its earliest differing generation tracks the state-dump onset frame with a consistent one-generation readback lag (gen 9 ↔ frame 10, gen 44 ↔ frame 45).

**Joints and the island dispatch are exonerated.** `parity_nojoints_gpu` — the same workload with no joints, so the island dispatch never executes and the solve takes the plain per-colour branch — is **equally nondeterministic across three runs, with the identical `s1[4..8]` signature**. The island path, the sequential colour sweep inside it, and the joint accumulation are all cleared as necessary conditions.

**Where that leaves the hunt, stated as the remaining possibility rather than a conclusion.** At the onset generation the coloured solve receives bit-identical bodies, bit-identical contacts, a valid conflict-free colouring, and an order-invariant (fixed-point) accumulation — and still produces a different `predicted_orientation`. `solve_body` is a deterministic function of `body_data`, `contact_data` and `frozen_jacobians`, and it reads `body_data[other_idx]` live for every contact neighbour. The colouring guarantees a body and its contact neighbour are in different colours, so the only way a live read can return a different value is if **consecutive colour dispatches overlap** — a missing or insufficient UAV barrier between them. This document records that giving every colour its own pass "changed nothing", but that test predates the corrected hash, the fixed-point accumulation and the restitution fix, and it was judged by an instrument that could not see rotation. **It should be re-run before anything else.** A useful corroborating detail: `use_jacobi=true`, the one configuration that never diverges, is also the one in which no thread reads another body's state within a dispatch.

**A methodology trap paid for, worth one line.** A regex ending in `$` applied to CRLF log lines matched nothing, and the analysis printed "no slot ever differs" — a false all-clear that survived only because the result looked too good and was checked against the line count. Strip `\r` before parsing engine logs, and treat any unexpected "identical" as a parse failure until proven otherwise.

### ROOT CAUSE FOUND: the colour-list flatten is filled by atomic append (2026-08-14)

**`collision_build_coloring.slang:258-271` assigned each body's position within its colour list with `InterlockedAdd(gs_color_fill[c], 1u, slot)` — atomic arrival order.** The colour *assignment* is deterministic; the *order of bodies inside each colour list* was not. Replacing that loop with a fill in body-index order moves the 120-frame onset from frame ~10 to frame 115, and before the fix onsets across pairs ran 9, 10, 35, 42, 44, 45, 60, 65, 91; after it, 115 and 84. **CORRECTION, and it matters: the first two sets of three 60-frame runs came back 3-of-3 bit-identical and this was briefly written up as "stress is deterministic". A later set of three, on the same build, came back 2-of-3. Determinism is MUCH improved but INTERMITTENT — the residual below fires roughly one run in three at 60 frames and more often at 120.** Three identical runs is not proof of determinism when the failure rate is ~1 in 3; the gate's "3 rounds of 6 runs" convention exists for exactly this reason and should be applied before any determinism claim.

**Production implementation, verified.** The single-threaded fill was replaced with a parallel deterministic rank: per-colour `WavePrefixCountBits`/`WaveActiveCountBits` for the within-wave rank, per-wave per-colour totals scanned across waves by `max_colors` threads, carried across chunks in `gs_color_fill`. Wave composition is a function of thread index, so the rank is deterministic, and it reproduces body-index order exactly — **the parallel version's state dump is byte-identical to the single-threaded version's**, which is the correctness proof. Cost is nil: median p50 29.24 ms against 29.35 for the serial fill. **The flatten was never a performance problem; the earlier "~17% cost" attributed to it was a single-run comparison against a different physics trajectory and is withdrawn.**

**How it hid for so long, and the lesson.** Every check this document ever ran on the colouring measured **validity** — zero conflicts, zero fallback, an exact partition of the dynamic bodies, correct Jones–Plassmann priority. All of those were true, and all of them are *per-run invariants*. **Nobody ever compared the colour data between two runs.** A colouring can be perfectly valid and still be a different colouring, and "the colouring is CLEAN — closed, do not re-open" was written on the strength of validity alone. **Validity is not determinism; a per-run invariant cannot detect a run-to-run difference.** That is the single most transferable lesson of this campaign.

**How it was found — the instrument was widened until it stopped lying.** Each time a blind spot was removed, the answer moved earlier, and the sequence is worth recording because it is the method:
1. Hash covering `position`/`predicted_position`/`velocity`/`orientation`/`angular_velocity` → first differing slot `s1[5]`, pointing at `vbd_derive_velocities` — a pass already cleared by audit as a pure per-body map. **The contradiction was the tell.**
2. Added the omitted `predicted_orientation` → answer moved to `s1[4]`, the solve loop. `derive_velocities` was the messenger, converting an unhashed field into a hashed one.
3. Made the hash **total over every field of `body_state`** → still `s1[4]`, gen 9, all of `s0` identical. So every body field was bit-identical entering the substep-1 solve.
4. Folded in `contact_data` (all fields) and `color_data` → the earliest difference jumped from gen 9 to **gen 3, the first traced generation**.
5. Bisected to `color_data` alone → differs in **56 of 56 generations from gen 3**.
6. Bisected again, hashing only the body→colour map order-insensitively → assignment first differs at gen **51**, i.e. *after* the bodies, so the assignment is a consequence. **What differed from gen 3 was the list order.**

**Why a permuted within-colour list changes results at all**, given that bodies in one colour share no contact: the list index *is* the dispatch thread index, so permuting it changes which thread — and therefore which workgroup — solves each body. The empirical result is unambiguous and reproducible; the precise micro-mechanism is not yet pinned and is worth a follow-up, but it does not block the fix.

**What this also explains, finally.** `use_jacobi=true` has been bit-identical throughout the entire campaign and nobody could say why. **Jacobi short-circuits ahead of the colour branch and never reads `color_data` at all.** It was never evidence about the solve's arithmetic; it was evidence that the colour path was the problem, and it sat unread for weeks.

**Status of the fix, and what it still needs.** The current implementation is a **test-grade single-threaded fill** and costs about 17% (`stress` p50 29.3 ms against ~25). The production version should compute each body's rank within its colour in parallel — a per-colour prefix sum, or counting same-colour bodies with a lower index — which is free by comparison. **This change re-baselines every GPU hash in the gate**, since it alters solve order.

**A residual remains and it is much weaker.** At 120 frames one pair of three still diverges (frame 115, 55 bodies, max 2.19e-6 m) and `parity_nojoints_gpu` diverges at frame 84 and 53 across pairs (35 bodies, 1.50e-5 m) — both in Tumbler 0 cubes. That is a second, far slower source, now unmasked by the removal of the dominant one.

**RESIDUAL LOCALISED PROPERLY — and the first bisect round is RETRACTED IN FULL.** A valid measurement hashes every candidate buffer into a **different slot in the same run**, so one pair of runs yields all of them on one clock: contact content (keyed by `(body_a, body_b, feature_key)`, commutative, **no adjacency walk**), total `body_state`, full `color_data`, and the contact count — all written at the `s0[1]` point. Across four runs and every pairing, **all four first differ at exactly the same generation, which is also the state-dump onset frame** (45/45/45/45 in one set). **No upstream buffer diverges before the bodies.** Two independent corollaries: `color_data` now first differs at the onset rather than at gen 3, which confirms the flatten fix from a second direction; and the divergence is introduced *inside* a tick rather than carried in through any buffer, which puts it back in the `s1[4]` bracket — the solve iteration loop — with every input provably identical.

**The retracted round, recorded so the errors are not repeated.** The earlier claim that contact content differs at gen 4-8 ahead of the bodies came from two compounding mistakes. First, **the bisect compared different run pairs** — gen 8 from one pair against gen 4 from another — when the entire character of this scene is that onsets vary per pair (4 to 115). A field-set bisect is only valid if every variant is measured on the *same* pair, which means hashing them into different slots simultaneously. Second, the hash **walked `contact_adjacency` at `s0[1]`, which sits after `prepare_contact_indirect` and before `collision_build_adjacency`** — so the adjacency there is a tick old while `contact_data` is fresh, and the walk indexed stale `ci` values into current contacts. Both produce confident nonsense. The "`s0[1]` has two writers" theory offered at the time was also wrong: `spare_hash_substep` is 2, so the other dispatch writes `regrid`, and `s0[1]` has exactly one writer.

**Superseded, for context only:** Hashing the per-body adjacency walk plus contact content plus `frozen_jacobians` differs from gen 3 while bodies hold to frame 53. Removing `frozen_jacobians` moves it to gen 8; removing `lambda`/`penalty` as well moves it to gen 4. **Those last two readings are inconsistent — dropping fields made the hash differ *earlier*, which cannot happen for a real content difference — and in both cases the first differing slot is `s0[1]`.** `s0[1]` is written during the collision phase, where the buffers being read are transitional: the adjacency belongs to one tick while `contact_data` and `frozen_jacobians` are being rewritten for the next. A hash spanning that seam is permutation-sensitive by construction and means nothing. **Before drawing any conclusion about the residual, pin exactly what `s0[1]` observes** — this is the same "check where the hash dispatch sits relative to the pass it describes" trap the campaign has now paid for six times, and the inconsistent bisect is the tell that it has been hit again. The honest state is that the residual is localised no further than "somewhere upstream of the bodies, visible from roughly gen 4-8, in the contact/jacobian family". Untouched and still worth comparing run-to-run at a *well-defined* point: `frozen_jacobians`, `contact_offsets`/`contact_counts`, `jointless_color_data`, and the indirect argument buffers.

### Performance baseline after the determinism work, and where the time actually goes (2026-08-14)

**All numbers are medians of three 60-frame `parity_stress_gpu` runs on Vulkan, same machine, same session.**

| build | p50 | deterministic? |
|---|---|---|
| parallel flatten + fixed-point accumulation + restitution tiebreak | **29.24 ms** | intermittent (2-3 of 3) |
| same, fixed-point accumulation removed | **21.88 ms** | no (1 of 3 pairs) |
| same, canonical `(other_body, feature_key)` adjacency sort instead of fixed point | **21.69 ms** | no (0 of 3 pairs) |

**Determinism currently costs 7.4 ms per frame — 34% — and it is all the fixed-point accumulation.** That is 33 `int64_t` accumulators live across the contact loop (roughly 66 VGPRs), and it is paid in the worst possible place: `solve_body` runs 40 iterations × 2 substeps per tick, so the cost is multiplied ~80× while the thing it defends against is a per-tick ordering property.

**The obvious cheaper substitute was tried and does NOT work.** Making the adjacency sort a provable total order — `(other_body, feature_key)`, which the warm-start ambiguity counter proved unique, so no float comparisons and no ties — costs nothing and runs once per tick. With fixed point removed it left all three pairs diverging. So the residual nondeterminism is **not** the per-body contact walk order, and canonicalising that order cannot replace the fixed-point sum. Reverted; the original sort key is restored.

**Two other measurements taken.** The `vbd_hash_state` diagnostic work costs only **0.35 ms** (28.89 against 29.24 with the shader early-returning). But **the 17 `hash_state` dispatch sites in `GpuSolver.cpp` are completely ungated** — there is no trace or debug flag on any of them, so every shipping frame records 17+ single-workgroup dispatches (`vec3u{1,1,1}`) with a full barrier either side, purely for diagnostics. The *work* is cheap; the dispatch and barrier overhead was not isolated and needs the host-side gate to measure. `solver_config` is the `Physics.*` settings struct and is the right home for that flag.

**The structural cost, for whoever takes the performance lever.** The solve loop issues, per tick, `adaptive_iterations` (40) × 2 substeps × (num_colors ≈ 5-7 colour dispatches + `update_lambda` + `update_joint_lambda` + `convergence_check`) ≈ **800 dispatches**. This document already established that the 1.6-2.3× slowdown from the adaptive budget is real work and that these scenes never converge early, so the iteration count is not free to cut without losing CPU parity. The levers that remain, roughly in order of expected value: gate the diagnostic dispatches; find a determinism mechanism cheaper than a per-iteration fixed-point sum (the residual must be identified first, since it is what forces fixed point to stay); reduce the per-iteration dispatch count by folding the per-colour dispatches into fewer launches; and only then revisit the iteration budget.

### Residual, per-iteration resolution: a rare event that can fire at any iteration (2026-08-14)

**Method.** A `vbd_hash_state` dispatch added inside the solve iteration loop writing `[sub][it]`, giving a body-state hash after each iteration. **Caveat that must be read before the numbers: the existing stage dispatches also write `[sub][stage]`, so the slots alias.** Stages 0-3 are recorded before the solve loop and stages 4-10 after it, so the in-loop write lands later for slots 0-3 and the stage write lands later for 4-10 — **only slots `[sub][0..3]` carry iteration hashes; 4-10 are still the old stage hashes.** Any future use of this probe should move it to a dedicated slot range.

**Result, reading only the valid half.** One pair (`parity_nojoints_gpu`, 90 frames) is **identical after iteration 0 and differs after iteration 1** of substep 1. Three other pairs have iterations 0-3 all identical, with their first difference landing in a stage slot. So the residual is **not a systematic first-iteration difference — it is a rare event that can fire at any iteration**, which is the signature of a race rather than a deterministic ordering artefact, and it is consistent with the observed ~1-in-3 run-pair failure rate.

**What this leaves standing.** At the firing iteration the coloured solve has bit-identical bodies, contacts, colouring and contact count (measured on one clock, above), an order-invariant fixed-point accumulation, and a colouring with zero conflicts that is now deterministic. `solve_body` writes only `body_data[bi]` and reads `body_data[other_idx]` for contact neighbours, which the colouring places in other colours — so a same-dispatch read/write collision should be impossible. Either that guarantee is violated in some case not covered by the conflict scans, or the race is below the shader level. **The joint loop is NOT the answer** — it is float-summed and uncovered by fixed point, but `parity_nojoints_gpu` never executes it and still fails.

### The residual is occupancy-sensitive, which reframes what fixed-point is actually buying (2026-08-14)

**A six-run round, all fifteen pairings, `parity_stress_gpu` 60 frames: 1 of 15 identical.** Every earlier determinism claim in this session rested on three-run samples, which gave 3-of-3 twice and 2-of-3 once. **Fifteen pairings is the first estimate worth trusting, and it says the residual fires in the large majority of pairings — the three-run samples were luck.** Any determinism claim on this scene needs the gate's 3-rounds-of-6 convention; three runs cannot distinguish a 90% failure rate from a 0% one with any confidence, and this document now contains two retracted claims that came from exactly that mistake.

**The build difference that matters, and the hypothesis it raises.** This six-run round differs from the earlier 3-of-3 rounds in one respect: the grad-check instrument was **removed** from `solve_body` (six `float3` accumulators plus two atomics per body-solve). Removing register pressure made determinism **worse**. Combined with the fixed-point result — 33 `int64_t` accumulators, roughly 66 VGPRs, which "fixed" determinism at a 34% cost — the pattern points somewhere uncomfortable: **fixed-point accumulation may be suppressing a race by cratering occupancy rather than fixing an ordering problem.** That is consistent with everything else in this section: the residual fires at any iteration, not systematically; the canonical total-order adjacency sort (which genuinely fixes ordering, for free) did nothing; and all solve inputs are provably identical on one clock. **If that hypothesis holds, the 7.4 ms is buying luck, not correctness, and it should not ship as a determinism mechanism.**

**Cheap way to test it before anything else.** Vary occupancy without changing semantics — pad `solve_body` with unused registers, or lower the workgroup size — and measure the failure rate over 15 pairings at each setting. A monotonic relationship between occupancy and failure rate confirms a race and kills the fixed-point justification; no relationship exonerates occupancy and sends the hunt back to the shader. Either answer is worth more than another bisect.

### The determinism/performance recipe, and the race narrowed to inside one colour dispatch (2026-08-14, late)

**All figures are `parity_stress_gpu` / `parity_nojoints_gpu` on Vulkan, warmup 0, six runs scored as identical pairings out of fifteen. Three-run scoring is banned from here on — it cannot distinguish a 90% failure rate from 0%, and it produced two retracted claims earlier today.**

**The accumulator sweep, which refutes the occupancy hypothesis outright.** Varying *which* accumulators use fixed point separates "registers" from "order-invariance", because the two hypotheses predict opposite orderings:

| config | fixed point on | `int64` regs | identical (60f) | p50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | all five | 33 | 1/15 | 27.8 ms |
| B | the three Hessians only | 24 | 3/15 | 28.3 ms |
| C | gradients only | 6 | 7/15 | 21.75 ms |
| **D** | **gradients only + canonical adjacency sort** | **6** | **15/15, then 10/15 on a repeat** | **21.8-22.0 ms** |
| E | none + canonical sort | 0 | 0/15 | 21.33 ms |

**Fewer registers gave better determinism, so occupancy is not what fixed point was buying — the hypothesis from the previous section is refuted.** What matters is *which* quantity is order-invariant: making the **gradients** invariant helps (C 7/15 against B 3/15 at four times the register cost), and making the Hessians invariant actively hurts (A 1/15 against C 7/15). **Config D is the recipe**: deterministic colour-list flatten, gradients-only fixed point, and the canonical `(other_body, feature_key)` adjacency sort which makes the float Hessian sums deterministic for nothing. Both D ingredients are load-bearing — C alone is 7/15, E alone is 0/15. **D is better than the previous best on both axes: 15/15 against 1/15, and 21.99 ms against 27.8 ms, a 21% speedup.**

**The canonical sort was verified rather than assumed** — a position-sensitive per-body walk-order hash first differs at exactly the onset generation, the same generation as body state, contact content and contact count. So the walk order really is canonical until the physics diverges.

**D is not a complete fix, the frame count matters, and even fifteen pairings is noisy.** At 120 frames stress drops to 6/15 and `nojoints` to 0/6. **A determinism claim must state its frame count.** And a repeat of the 60-frame D round returned **10/15 where the first returned 15/15** — so the honest figure is roughly 10-15 of 15, against 1/15 for the previous best. Fifteen pairings is enough to rank configurations; it is *not* enough to certify a build as deterministic. Only a run of many rounds with zero failures should ever be called that. `parity_drop_gpu` remains bit-identical, so the settled scenes are unaffected by the change.

**The race is inside a single colour dispatch.** Serializing the coloured path — one thread solving that colour's whole body list sequentially — gives **6/6 identical** (at 387 ms, 17× slower). Giving each colour its own render-graph pass, re-tested properly at 15 pairings this time, gives **0/15**: **cross-colour overlap is definitively not the cause**, which also settles the question left open earlier when it was judged at three runs.

**And the obvious explanation for an intra-colour race is ruled out.** Re-verified on the current build, per generation: bodies appearing in more than one colour or in none = **0**, and same-colour contact pairs = **0**. `solve_body`'s only write is `body_data[bi]`, so with an exact partition and no same-colour contact pair, two threads in one dispatch should share nothing. **They demonstrably interfere anyway.** The remaining candidate worth checking first: `solve_body` guards contact indices with `if (ci >= max_contacts) continue` but **never against the current contact count**, so a stale `ci` left in `contact_adjacency` would be read as a live contact out of `contact_data` — and `collision_reset` zeroes stale contacts to `body_a = body_b = 0`, which passes the `>= pc.body_count` guard and aliases onto body 0.

### Vulkan sync validation finds real hazards — but indirect dispatch is NOT the cause (2026-08-14, night)

**Use `Graphics.validation_layers_enabled=true` on Vulkan for this class of question; it detects synchronization hazards directly and nobody in this campaign had pointed it at the solver.** On `parity_nojoints_gpu` it reports genuine, repeatable hazards:

- `vkCmdDispatchIndirect` in `vbd_solve_iterations_stage`, `vbd_narrow_phase_stage` and `vbd_freeze_jacobians_stage` **read the indirect argument buffer with a READ_AFTER_WRITE hazard against the `vkCmdFillBuffer` that clears it** in `vbd_clear_state_buffers_stage` — across command buffers, so the render graph's inter-pass barriers are missing the indirect-command-read stage.
- A WRITE_AFTER_WRITE between the `vbd_clear_state_buffers_stage` fill and a `vbd_hash_state_stage` fill.

**These are real defects in the barrier derivation and should be fixed on their own merits.** They are also a beautiful fit for the symptom — a stale workgroup count would launch a varying number of groups, so a varying set of bodies would be solved, which would explain the intra-dispatch "race", explain why serializing fixed it (thread 0 of group 0 does all the work regardless of the count), and explain Jacobi's immunity (it uses plain `dispatch`).

**The fit is wrong. Measured, at fifteen pairings, with every indirect dispatch in the solver replaced by a direct one: `nojoints` 0/15, `stress` 6/15 — no improvement.** An intermediate round that converted only the per-colour dispatches gave `stress` 15/15, which looked like a fix and was not: converting the rest returned it to 6/15, so that 15/15 was noise. **Indirect dispatch is eliminated as the cause of the residual.** Do not re-run this; fix the barriers because they are wrong, not because they will fix determinism. (The diagnostic patch also over-dispatched with hard-coded bounds of 16384 contacts / 32768 pairs against real caps of 262144 — unsafe in general, and reverted.)

**Also eliminated this round: stale contact indices.** `solve_body` guards only `ci >= max_contacts`, never the current contact count, so a stale index in `contact_adjacency` would read a dead contact — but a direct count says **0 stale indices and 0 zeroed contacts walked** in every generation. Closed.

**Where the residual now stands, after everything.** Serializing a colour dispatch is deterministic (6/6); parallel is not. Within a dispatch the colour partition is exact, there are zero same-colour contact pairs, no stale indices, canonical walk order, identical contact content, and the workgroup count is now provably irrelevant. `solve_body`'s only write is `body_data[bi]` with `bi` unique per thread. **Every mechanism by which two threads in one dispatch could interact has been measured and excluded, and they still interact.** The one premise never tested is the lowest-level one: that `body_data[bi]` writes from different threads do not overlap in memory — i.e. that the shader's `body_state` stride exactly matches the host's. A padding mismatch would let a write to body *a* smear into body *a+1*, would be nondeterministic under concurrency, would vanish under serialization, and would be invisible to every content hash taken so far because the corruption is *of* the hashed data rather than upstream of it. **That is the first thing to check next.**

### `scripts/divergence_sweep.py` — the determinism metric that actually holds still (2026-08-14, night)

**Built to replace the identical/not-identical pairing count, which the section below shows is unusable.** The script runs a scenario N times with `--engine-bench-state-dump-out`, computes the **first frame at which any body differs bitwise** for all N(N-1)/2 pairings, and reports the median, min/max, quartiles and the p50 alongside. Identical pairings are right-censored at the frame count and counted separately, so a censored result is visible rather than silently flattering.

```bash
python scripts/divergence_sweep.py --exe out/build/x64-mingw-gcc-RelWithDebInfo/Sandbox/Sandbox.exe --scenario parity_stress_gpu --runs 6 --frames 300 --setting Graphics.backend=vulkan --label mybuild
```

**Run it at 300 frames, not 120.** At 120 frames on the current build 6-10 of 15 pairings are still identical, so the median pins to the censoring ceiling and most of the signal is thrown away. At 300 frames nothing is censored and the measurement is uncensored and comparable.

**Validation — this is why the metric is worth the trouble.** Two rounds of six runs on the *same* build, at 300 frames: **median onset 61 and 59**, quartiles 59/61/92 and 52/59/121. The binary pairing count on that same build across three rounds gave **15/15, 10/15, 3/15**. The continuous metric is stable to within a few percent where the binary swings across its entire range, because it uses every pairing's magnitude instead of collapsing each to a bit.

**A trap the sweep harness fell into, worth one paragraph.** The first automated sweep drove the shader between configurations by string replacement, inserting the fixed-point decode before an anchor of `"    if (has_motor) {"`. That substring occurs **three times** in `vbd_solve_color.slang`, and the first occurrence is the motor *setup* block near the top of `solve_body` — so the decode landed at line 350 while the contact accumulation is at line 429, and every fixed-point configuration ran with `grad` reset to zero before the contacts were summed. The physics was silently wrong, not crashed. **The tell was the contact peak: 4325 for the float config, 16017 for one fixed-point config, 1057 for another, on the identical scene.** A scene's contact count is a cheap, sensitive canary for "the solver is doing something insane" and should be checked on every automated sweep before any result is read. The harness now asserts that the decode's line number exceeds the accumulation's, and that the anchor matches exactly once.

### The config sweep, re-run on the new metric — one robust result (2026-08-14, night)

**Seven configurations, six runs each, `parity_stress_gpu` at 300 frames on Vulkan, scored by median first-divergence frame across all fifteen pairings. Contact-count canary healthy and consistent across all seven (4125-4387), so this sweep is valid — unlike the first attempt.**

| config | grad FP | hess FP | canonical sort | onset median | quartiles | p50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none_orig | – | – | – | 76 | 76/76/115 | **25.8 ms** |
| grad_orig | yes | – | – | 90 | 57/90/94 | 28.4 ms |
| hess_orig | – | yes | – | 52 | 52/52/52 | 45.5 ms |
| all_orig | yes | yes | – | 45 | 45/45/45 | 50.1 ms |
| none_canon | – | – | yes | 51 | 51/51/51 | 25.7 ms |
| grad_canon | yes | – | yes | 105 | 10/105/123 | 28.8 ms |
| all_canon | yes | yes | yes | 45 | 45/45/45 | 49.6 ms |

**The robust result: fixed-point accumulation of the Hessians is bad on both axes and should never be used.** All three Hessian configurations sit at 45-52 median onset *and* 45-50 ms — **roughly twice the frame cost of every other configuration** — while the non-Hessian ones run 25-29 ms. The perf gap is far outside any noise this campaign has seen, and the determinism is no better and probably worse. **This retires Config A ("all five accumulators in fixed point"), which was the shipping recommendation two sections ago, and removes about 20 ms of frame time relative to it.**

**The caution, which matters as much: the four non-Hessian configurations cannot be ranked at this sample size.** Their medians (76, 90, 51, 105) look like a clear ordering and are not one — `grad_canon` is the *same build* that measured medians of 61 and 59 during metric validation and reads 105 here, a 70% swing. Its quartiles give the reason: 10/105/123, i.e. one run diverged at frame 10 while others held past 120. **The onset distribution is heavy-tailed, so at six runs the median moves with how many outlier runs happen to land in the sample.** The metric is a large improvement over the binary count and it is still not sharp enough to separate configurations that differ by less than roughly a factor of two. Do not quote the 76/90/51/105 ordering.

**Consequently: gradient fixed point costs about 3 ms (25.8 → 28.4 without the canonical sort, 25.7 → 28.8 with it) and its determinism benefit is unproven at n=6.** Settled by a ten-run round over the four non-Hessian configurations, forty-five pairings each:

| config | grad FP | canonical sort | onset median | quartiles | p50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none_orig | – | – | 76 | 76/76/76 | 25.87 ms |
| grad_orig | yes | – | 115 | 90/115/115 | 28.78 ms |
| none_canon | – | yes | 51 | 51/51/66 | 25.85 ms |
| grad_canon | yes | yes | 121 | 61/121/121 | 28.68 ms |

**Gradient fixed point earns its ~3 ms**: it raises the median time-to-divergence from a very stable 76 to 115, and from 51 to 121 when the canonical sort is present. The effect reproduces across both rounds and both sample sizes. **The canonical adjacency sort does not earn its place and has been reverted**: on its own it makes things *worse* (76 → 51, stable in both rounds), and combined with gradient fixed point it moves 115 → 121, inside the noise. It changes contact processing order and re-baselines every GPU hash in the gate, so it should not ship for an unmeasurable gain.

**Shipped configuration and its verification.** Deterministic colour-list flatten (parallel prefix-sum rank) + gradient-only fixed-point accumulation + the restitution feature-key tiebreak, with the Hessians in float and the original adjacency sort. Ten runs, forty-five pairings, 300 frames: **median onset 90, p50 28.64 ms, contact canary 4377, and `parity_drop_gpu` still bit-identical.** Against `all_orig` — Config A, the configuration recommended earlier the same day — that is **43% less frame time (28.6 against 50.1 ms) and twice the time-to-divergence (90 against 45)**.

**A caveat that applies to every number in this section, and a limitation of the metric itself.** The final round read median 90 where the sweep read 115 for the identical configuration, so even forty-five pairings moves by roughly a quarter; only `none_orig` is tight enough (76/76/76 twice) to quote without a range. More importantly, **the onset metric measures time-to-*visible*-divergence, which is the nondeterminism rate multiplied by the rate at which chaos amplifies it.** Any change that alters the trajectory — the canonical sort certainly does — changes both terms, so a config that scores worse is not necessarily *less deterministic*, it may simply be more chaotic. That confound is why the canonical-sort result should be read as "no demonstrated benefit" rather than "actively harmful", and why comparisons are only safe between configurations whose arithmetic is nearly identical.

### The scoring metric itself is too noisy — read this before trusting any table above (2026-08-14, night)

**The same Config D build, verified by source inspection to contain all three fixes, scored 15/15, then 10/15, then 3/15 identical pairings across three rounds of six 60-frame runs.** Nothing changed between rounds but time. **A round of six runs is therefore not a stable measurement of the failure rate, and every determinism number in the tables above is a single round.**

**What that invalidates and what survives.** The configuration *ranking* (A 1/15, B 3/15, C 7/15, D 15/15) is one round each and cannot be trusted — the spread within one build covers the entire range that supposedly separated the configs. Do not quote it as evidence that gradients-only fixed point beats all-five. **What does survive is independent of the pairing score:** the p50 figures, which are medians and consistently separate the high-register configs (~28 ms) from the low-register ones (~21 ms); the colour-flatten fix, which is confirmed by a *hash* showing `color_data` no longer differs from gen 3 rather than by a pairing count; the canonical sort, confirmed by a walk-order hash; and the eliminations, which rest on direct counters reading exactly zero rather than on scores.

**The metric to use instead, and it should be built before any more hypothesis testing.** A binary identical/not-identical over six runs throws away almost all the information in a run. **Score the first-divergence frame instead** — it is continuous, it is already computed by the comparator, and it separates builds that a binary cannot: Config A diverged around frame 10 while the current build diverges at 45-115, which is a large, stable, and obvious difference that the pairing count renders as a coin flip. Median first-divergence frame over the fifteen pairings of a round, repeated over three rounds, is the metric this campaign should have been using all along. **Until that exists, no further single-round experiment on this residual is worth running** — the noise floor is larger than the effects being chased.

### The residual: a race that is not a write-write race, and two slot-collision traps (2026-08-15)

**Semantic equivalence test, which sharpens the serialization result.** Bodies in one colour share no contact, so serial-in-list-order must produce the *same* answer as parallel if the colouring guarantee holds. Measured over 40 frames: two parallel runs were **bit-identical to each other**, and the serialized run **first differs from parallel at frame 10** (2 bodies, 5.8e-6 m). That is consistent with parallel simply being racy — its answer drifts from the correct serial one — and it does **not** require a semantic difference. It also does not prove the race is inside `solve_color`: serialization makes the frame 17× slower (333 ms against 21 ms), so it could equally be hiding a race elsewhere by giving everything else time to finish. **That timing confound is unresolved and is the main reason the serialization result should not be over-read.**

**The colouring guarantee holds where it is actually used.** The earlier conflict scan walked `contact_data` globally, which is not what `solve_body` does — `solve_body` walks the per-body adjacency and derives `other_idx` from each entry, so an entry that does not belong to that body would send it to an unrelated body. Re-measured exactly that way, per generation: **same-colour neighbours reached through the adjacency = 0, and orphan adjacency entries (where the body is neither `body_a` nor `body_b`) = 0.** The guarantee is intact at the point of use.

**A direct race detector on the body write finds nothing.** `solve_body` writes `body_data[bi]`, then `AllMemoryBarrier()`, then reads the element back and compares position and orientation, counting mismatches. **Zero in every generation.** The barrier defeats store-forwarding so a concurrent clobber would be visible, but the write-to-read window is very small, so this is strong evidence rather than proof. **Combined with the two results above: there is no write-write race on `body_data`, and no read of a same-colour body.**

**Two slot-collision traps, both of which nearly produced a false "RACE DETECTED".** The clobber counter was first placed at `state_tick_hash_base_index + 7`, which `collision_narrow_phase` already increments once per contact — so it read back the contact count (129, 252, 253, 254, 294, …) and looked exactly like a race scaling with scene complexity. Moved to `+ 2`, it read back large hash values, because a `vbd_hash_*` harness writes that slot through the `state_pass_hash_base_index + substep * stage_count + iteration` expression with the spare substep, which does not textually match a grep for `state_tick_hash_base_index`. **Every tick-scope slot is occupied by some diagnostic; one had to be freed deliberately (the probe's body-hash write) before the counter could be trusted.** The tell both times was that the numbers matched a quantity already known from another instrument. **When a new counter's values look like a familiar sequence, suspect the slot before believing the finding** — and enumerate writers by *index expression*, not by symbol name.

**Where the residual stands.** A race exists in the sense that serializing removes it, and it is **not** a write-write race on `body_data`, **not** a same-colour contact read, **not** stale contact indices, **not** the indirect workgroup count, **not** cross-colour dispatch overlap, and **not** occupancy. The untested hypothesis that the serialization confound leaves open is a race **outside** `solve_color` entirely — with the kinematic wall upload, a readback, or another queue — which a 17× longer frame would mask. That is where the next session should look, and the Vulkan sync-validation hazards already recorded are the natural starting point since they are real defects in exactly that area.

### The barrier hazards decoded: every fill was mis-staged, and the reach is far wider than the indirect args (2026-08-15)

**The actual derivation gap is the *source* side of the barrier, not the destination, and the earlier "missing indirect-command-read stage" reading is retracted.** The full validation messages name the missing scope precisely: the reads at `VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_2_DRAW_INDIRECT_BIT` conflict with "a prior write (`VK_ACCESS_2_TRANSFER_WRITE_BIT`) at **`VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_2_CLEAR_BIT`**", and the WRITE_AFTER_WRITE is CLEAR-vs-CLEAR. Vulkan executes `vkCmdFillBuffer` in the CLEAR stage, not COPY — the engine has both flags (`pipeline_stage_flag::copy` → `eCopy`, `::clear` → `eClear`, faithful in `Vulkan/Types.cppm`) — but `recording_context::fill_buffer` noted its access at `copy`. Every barrier the graph derived around a fill therefore carried a src (or dst) scope that does not contain the fill's actual execution stage, intra-pass and cross-pass alike. The dst-side `draw_indirect` coverage was right all along; `dispatch_indirect`'s note flows into the pass usage lists and the cross-pass planner copies stages verbatim (verified by reading `append_prev_pass_barriers` end to end). Fixed with one word: `fill_buffer` now notes `pipeline_stage_flag::clear`. DX12 is untouched by construction — its barrier translation derives states from *access* flags only (`gpu::state_of(bb.src_access)`), never from stage bits, and its fill really is implemented as copies.

**Why this is a live nondeterminism mechanism and not just the two reported hazards: the per-tick clears race their compute consumers scene-wide.** `vbd_clear_state_buffers_stage` fills `frozen_jacobian_buffer`, `solve_state_buffer`, and `solve_deltas_buffer` **every tick**, and on reseed ticks fills contact/pair/state/grid/colour/indirect buffers too, all with barriers whose masks did not cover CLEAR. An unordered clear can land before, during, or after the compute passes that produce and consume those buffers, on a schedule the GPU picks per run. This fits the entire residual signature at once: it is outside `solve_color`; it fires at any iteration (whichever dispatch the late clear collides with); serializing a colour dispatch masks it because a 17× slower frame drains the queue between dispatches; and converting indirect→direct alone could not cure it because the indirect-argument read was only one of the racing consumers. It is also Vulkan-only, which matches DX12 stress variance having survived every Vulkan-side fix as a separate class. Validation-run duplicate limits mean the two recorded hazards were the visible tip; the same mis-staging covered every fill in the solver.

**Second finding, same log: the solver's fill and copy targets were spec-invalid — missing TRANSFER usage flags.** `vkCmdFillBuffer` requires `TRANSFER_DST` and `vkCmdCopyBuffer` requires `TRANSFER_SRC`/`DST` on the buffer's creation usage; the validation run flagged both across the VBD set, and a creation-site audit found twelve buffers wrong: `contact` (filled, src-only), `contact_counts`/`contact_offsets`/`contact_adjacency` (filled + copied both directions in the cross-slot lineage copies, had *neither* flag), `collision_pair`, `collision_state`, `grid`, `grounded`, `color`, `jointless_color`, and both indirect dispatch buffers (filled, no `transfer_dst`). All fixed at the `create_buffers` declarations. The one-time WAW between `vbd_hash_state_stage` and the clear stage is the `device_local_seeded` seeding fills (recorded inside the first hash pass) colliding with the same tick's reseed fills — first tick only, zeros over zeros, but genuinely unordered until now. Also fixed while there: the Vulkan buffer path promoted concurrent sharing with a duplicate queue-family list (`video_encode` aliases the graphics family, so the raw per-queue-type array always carried a dup — `VUID-VkBufferCreateInfo-sharingMode-01419`); the list is now deduplicated and exclusive sharing is kept when only one family remains.

**Third finding, same log: headless frames violate the fence and binary-semaphore contracts — in exactly the mode every determinism measurement runs.** Three defects in `gpu::frame`: (1) the graphics in-flight fence is reset only inside the swapchain path of `begin`, so headless submitted it while still signaled every frame — which makes any later wait on it vacuous, the same vacuous-wait shape as the readback-retirement race this campaign already paid for (the solver's `wait_for_slot` survives because its load-bearing leg is the *compute* fence, which was handled correctly — verified by reading both host-write heads and all eight `slot_ready` consumers); (2) the graphics submit unconditionally waited `image_available`, which nothing signals without an acquire — a UB wait whose stall-or-not resolution is a per-frame scheduling perturbation; (3) it unconditionally signaled `render_finished`, which no present consumes, re-signaling a signaled binary semaphore. All three now gate on `m_swapchain`; headless resets the graphics fence in `begin` right where the windowed path does, so the frame-age rule's assumptions are unchanged.

**Closed in passing: the shader-vs-host `body_state` stride hypothesis** (the "one premise never tested" from 08-14 night). The Slang struct is *generated from* the C++ declaration (`[[= shaders::shader_struct]]`), every member is 4-byte aligned so the host layout has no padding to disagree about, and `parity_drop_gpu`'s bit-identity with the CPU already excludes any write smear. Do not re-raise it.

**Measured: the fixes hold, the residual does not move.** Post-fix sync validation on `parity_nojoints_gpu` AND on `parity_stress_gpu` (which exercises the joint/island passes nojoints never runs): **zero hazard messages, zero fence/semaphore errors, zero usage errors** — only the four pre-existing extension-dependency VUIDs at instance/device creation remain. The nojoints 40-frame world hash is unchanged by the fixes. Determinism: two post-fix sweeps read median 57 (onsets 10/57/58/97) and 115 (94/115) — the same band as the pre-fix 61/90/115. **The hazards were real defects, now gone, and they were not the residual's driver.** The canonical adjacency sort was re-tested on top of the clean barriers — median 52 (10/52/121) — which **refutes it a second time, now without the contaminated-baseline excuse: the per-body contact walk order is not the source.** Reverted again.

### The residual is event-gated, seeded at a repeatable first-impact, and expresses only in long runs (2026-08-15)

**Onsets are not diffuse — they collapse onto a handful of exact event frames per round,** and the earliest one is the same two bodies every time: the 15 pairings of each 6-run round land entirely on frames {10, 57, 58, 97}, {94, 115}, {10, 52, 121}, and every frame-10 pairing is **`Grid L0R0C5` + `Grid L1R0C5`** — the same settling box-grid stack the post-restitution-fix round recorded, third sighting across three builds. Field-level structure at the seed: **positions bit-identical except one ULP on L0R0C5.pos.y (5.96e-8, the exact recorded signature), velocity and angular velocity differ at 1e-5..8e-4, both boxes still descending** — a first-impact tick, and a flip that moves velocity while leaving position untouched points at the velocity-writing path (restitution impulse application / velocity derivation), not position integration. The later clusters (57/58/97/115) are tumbler-cube clumps — 18-body simultaneous onset sets, identical across pairings — and the minimal observed seed anywhere is **one quaternion component differing by one ULP** (Tumbler 0 Cube 164). Body labels recovered by hashing spawn names with FNV-1a 64 (`Grid L{}R{}C{}`, `Tumbler {} Cube {}`, `Domino {i+1}` — 1-based).

**The flip requires the execution profile of a long run.** Eighteen 40-frame runs on the same build — twelve untraced plus six traced — are **bit-identical to the last bit, one equivalence class**, while 300-frame runs on the same build flip at frame 10 roughly once per 6-run round. A frame-10 divergence that appears only when the run is 300 frames long cannot be a function of the simulation state (identical by construction at frame 10); it has to ride the run's execution-timing personality — GPU clock/thermal trajectory is the plausible carrier (the outlier run was the cold first run in one round, the second run in another). Two corollaries: **short-run determinism checks are structurally blind to this class** (they will pass forever), and the masking effects already recorded (serialization's 17× slowdown, the contact-trace observer effect at 40 frames) are the same phenomenon — anything that changes per-frame host/GPU cadence moves the race in or out of its window.

### Jacobi is clean at full length; the pass-hash bracket re-confirms the solve loop; a stamp detector finds no iteration-grain staleness (2026-08-15, later)

**Jacobi at 300 frames: 15 of 15 pairings bit-identical** (`Physics.use_jacobi=true`, 6 runs, canary 3982, one hash ×6). Same hardware, same clocks, same scene, same buffers, same run length — deterministic. This kills any hardware/DVFS-memory-system story in general form and localises the residual to **what the coloured Gauss-Seidel path does that Jacobi does not: live cross-dispatch reads of neighbour state within an iteration, at ~800 barrier-separated dispatches per tick.** It also hands the campaign a deterministic control config (not a shipping candidate — the CPU is Gauss-Seidel, so flipping the GPU to Jacobi trades away the parity target; its 8.9 ms p50 vs ~29 ms also implies different work that has never been quality-audited).

**The pass-hash bracket, re-taken on traced 300-frame pairs, lands exactly where it always has:** first differing tokens at the onset generation are `s1[4..8]` with all of `s0`, `s1[0..3]`, entry, warm inputs, adjacency, and colouring bit-identical, and `srcbody == dstbody` within each run. The substep-1 solve iteration loop, with provably identical inputs, on clean barriers.

**A per-body iteration-stamp detector was built to catch cross-dispatch staleness in the act** (stamp = f(substep, iteration, colour) written by each coloured solve into the GS-fallow `solve_deltas`, neighbour reads verified against the colour schedule's promise, counters + a packed worst-sample in two new header slots 56/57 — header bumped 56→58 in `vbd_limits`, host plumbing through `solver_diagnostics` and the peak line, which now prints `stale X of Y first F`). **Result: behind-by-one mismatches — the signature of a read one iteration stale — are ≈ zero across ~5.6 M checks per 60-frame run.** There is a residual ~2% mismatch class, but it is systematic, constant-rate from first contact, and its worst sample is pinned at substep-boundary stamp values across three sample variants (neighbour jointless, awake at counter 9, 15+ contacts, reading as unsolved-this-substep) — the structure of a gap in the checker's model of the dispatch schedule, not the scatter of a race. Three mechanical audits that could have explained it as a real defect all came back clean: colouring eligibility excludes only locked and contact-less bodies; `vbd_prepare_color_indirect` writes `[2+color]` with round-up counts exactly where the dispatches read; the converged flag is zeroed by reset every substep and cleared by derive. **The instrument is REVERTED from the hot path** (shipping config keeps "no probes in the hot path"); the header slots, diagnostics fields, and peak-line format stay reserved so the shader-only probe can be re-applied without a rebuild. The stamp recipe and its three paid-for false-positive traps (stabilize shares the pipeline and stamps through the *global* lists with out-of-loop iteration numbering; packed samples must not be host-summed; a [1.0,1.001) float marker is only safe in a buffer nothing else writes) are worth keeping.

**Acceptance of everything shipped today:** drop VK ×2 = `0x14f82f0ca02de8d7` — the recorded three-way CPU==VK==DX12 identity hash, unchanged, so the barrier/usage/fence fixes re-baselined nothing; mound DX12 ×2 deterministic (`0xcaf44f8b6f8f15ff`) on the shared frame-code changes; sync validation zero-hazard on nojoints and stress.

**Where this leaves the residual.** Every software mechanism reachable by instruments has now been measured: inputs identical on one clock, orders canonical or order-invariant, barriers validation-clean, no iteration-grain staleness detectable in-dispatch, Jacobi immune, expression gated on long-run timing personality. What remains is below the API's semantics on this driver/hardware pair (RTX 5090, driver 596.75) — some interaction of the ~800-dispatch-per-tick Gauss-Seidel stream with the memory system that neither sync validation nor in-shader detectors can see. Three moves are available, in cost order: **(1) driver update, then re-run the 300-frame sweep ×3 rounds; (2) pin GPU clocks (`nvidia-smi -lgc`) and re-run — if the flip rate collapses, the DVFS-transition carrier is confirmed; (3) restructure the solve loop to drastically fewer dispatches** (fold the per-iteration colour sequence + lambda update into fewer launches), which shrinks the exposed surface as a side effect of a perf win the plan already wants ("fold the per-colour dispatches into fewer launches" is lever 3 of the perf list). The DX12 stress variance should be re-measured with the divergence-sweep metric before assuming it shares this class.

### Driver A/B: the flip rate is a function of driver version; the Grid seed convicts whichever coloured sweep runs last (2026-08-15, driver 596.99)

**Move (1) was run: driver 596.75 → 596.99, same exe, same scene, same shaders. The residual persists and fires ~5× MORE often** — three 6-run rounds read medians 51 / 10 / 10 (onsets collapse onto the same event frames; the frame-10 Grid event now fires in 2-3 of 6 runs instead of ~1 in 6), p50 29.5 → 25.5 ms, canary healthy in all 18 runs. **Jacobi on the new driver: 15/15 bit-identical again.** The two-driver × two-config matrix is now complete: Gauss-Seidel's flip rate moves with driver version while Jacobi is invariantly clean on both. **That is direct evidence the mechanism lives in the driver's handling of the coloured-GS dispatch pattern, not in the shaders or the engine's sync** — our code and the API-level sync are identical across both cells of the driver axis.

**The higher rate made the Grid seed catchable under trace, and the catch names the pass.** Six traced 300-frame runs produced two flipped runs (agreeing with *each other* to frame 51 — the flip is bistable, not diffuse) against four clean ones. On one clock at the seed tick (gen 9): **`s1[4]` solve, `s1[5]` derive, and `s1[6]` restitution are bit-identical; the first difference is `s1[7]` — the post-stabilize coloured sweep** — and the traced body's line shows position moved 2 µm with velocity/angular bit-identical, exactly stabilize's position-only signature. Every gen-10 contact difference is downstream of that. **Ablation check: with `Physics.post_stabilize=false` the frame-10 event still exists and is still `Grid L0R0C5`** — the *event* (a razor-edge contact state at that exact frame) is the invariant; the *introducing pass* is whichever coloured-GS sweep touches it (stabilize when present, since it runs last; the main solve loop otherwise, which matches the older tumbler-event brackets landing on `s1[4]`). There is no single pass to fix — the class is the coloured-GS dispatch sequence itself.

**Latent defect flagged in passing (not the Grid seed's cause, but real): the stabilize sweep solves ALL bodies through the global colour lists — including jointed bodies — and the colouring separates only contact neighbours, so two joint-connected bodies can share a colour and race their `body_data` reads/writes inside one stabilize dispatch.** The main loop is immune (jointed bodies ride the serialized island path); stabilize is not. Jointed scenes on GPU should fix this before trusting stabilize (exclude jointed bodies from the stabilize colour sweep and stabilize them on the island path, or extend the colouring's conflict edges to joints).

**Standing next moves: (a) the clock-pin discriminator** (`nvidia-smi -lgc <base>,<base>` + one sweep round, then `-rgc`; needs admin — user action) — rate collapse would confirm the DVFS carrier and complete an NVIDIA bug report (the repro is now excellent: same exe, two drivers, different rates, Jacobi control clean, sync validation clean, bistable single-contact seed); **(b) the dispatch-fold restructure** (perf lever 3) as the engineering path that shrinks the exposed surface regardless of the driver's behavior; **(c) re-measure DX12 stress with the sweep metric** before assuming it shares the class.

### Intra-pass barrier batching: 25× fewer barrier commands, 30-60% faster frames, and the frame-10 seed is gone (2026-08-15, the dispatch-fold restructure)

**The restructure target turned out to be barrier commands, not dispatches.** Portable compute cannot phase-order work across workgroups inside one dispatch, so the per-colour launch count is structural — but `emit_intra_pass_barrier` was emitting **one `vkCmdPipelineBarrier2` per hazarded buffer per note**: with the shared ~31-buffer binding table re-pushed before every dispatch and nearly every buffer noted read+write, that is ~25-30 barrier commands per boundary × ~1500 boundaries ≈ **40,000+ barrier commands per tick**. The fix batches pending hazards per resource (masks OR-merged) and flushes them as **one** `pipeline_barrier` call at the next recorded command — identical synchronization semantics, ~25× fewer commands. Implementation notes: flush sites at every work-consuming recorder command (dispatch/draw/indirect/copy/fill/blit/AS-build/bind-index/explicit-barrier/layout-transition), pending image barriers must flush **before** any layout transition (they capture the layout at note time), the pending vectors ride the move constructor/assignment (the coroutine pass-handoff moves would silently drop them otherwise), and five recording methods lost a mistaken `const`. The `update_lambda`-into-solve fold was **rejected on exactness grounds**: `vbd_update_lambda` ratchets λ/penalty on **every** contact including both-sleeping replayed pairs (neither endpoint solves, so a later-endpoint fold would silently freeze replay warm data — the verified pile-sleep machinery depends on it).

**Verified:** sync validation on stress = **zero hazards** with batching (the proof no hazard was dropped); drop VK ×2 deterministic; canary healthy and identical across runs. **Measured: p50 25.5 → 18.0 ms at 300 frames (stress, VK, stock clocks), ~10.8 ms at 40 frames — the recorded stream was substantially barrier-command-bound. And the frame-10 Grid seed is GONE: 45 pairings across three 300-frame rounds with zero onsets at 10** (pre-batching it fired in 8-9 of 15 per round), medians 10/51/51 → 51/72/51 with the event ladder above 51 unchanged (51/72/76/91 — same trajectory, so the batching changed no arithmetic, as designed). **New causal datum for the driver story: the flip probability scales with barrier-command pressure** — collapsing the command stream 25× extinguished the most frequent event and shifted the residual's expression to later, rarer events. Stress is still not fully deterministic (the 51-event persists, presumably the same class), but the direction is proven: shrinking the recorded stream suppresses the defect's expression.

**Driver-update side effect, recorded for the gate: driver 596.99's shader compiler changed ULP-level arithmetic — the stress event ladder moved (57/58 → 51) on the same exe, and drop's hash is now `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` (×2). Every absolute GPU hash in the gate re-baselines on a driver update.** Remaining stream-shrink levers, in order: gate the 17 ungated per-tick diagnostic hash dispatches; cap `max_colors` 16 → ~10 (kills ~480 empty colour launches/tick; guarded by the fallback+conflict counters but it is a behavior-risk change — propose first); ExecuteIndirect/device-generated-commands for the iteration loop is the nuclear option.

### The remaining levers: hash dispatches gated behind `Physics.trace_hashes`, the colour cap refuted by measurement, the NVIDIA report written (2026-08-15, final)

**Diagnostic hash gating shipped.** The ~17 per-tick `vbd_hash_state_stage` passes (stage hashes, pregrid/regrid, entry/alt/bodies, warm inputs, adjacency ×2, colours ×3 including the second coloring execution, srcbody/dstbody copy brackets) are now recorded only when **`Physics.trace_hashes`** is set — a new annotated setting on `physics::data`, copied through `solver_config_from_settings` into `solver_config.trace_hashes` (note the trap paid: a field added to `solver_config` alone does nothing — the Physics settings category lives on `physics::data` and maps field-by-field). `ContactTrace` warns once, after the first retired generation, if a body is being traced with hashes off (the pass-hash line would read zeros). Verified: hashes-on produces live pass-hash lines; hashes-off leaves drop's hash untouched (`0x68a34d32b077fbe0` ×2); 12×40-frame runs bit-identical. **`max_colors` 16 → 10 is REFUTED by measurement, do not do it: `parity_overlap_gpu` uses 13 of 16 colours** (pile 4, mound 4, heap 3, stress 7) — any cap that pays would push overlap into the force-assign fallback. The NVIDIA report is written at `docs/nvidia_determinism_report.md` (environment, workload shape, the seven observations including driver-version and barrier-pressure scaling, the Jacobi/serialization controls, repro offer).

**The lean stream's numbers (batching + gated hashes, driver 596.99, stock clocks): stress p50 16.3 ms at 300 frames** — from 25.5 pre-restructure on this driver (36% faster) and ~29 on 596.75 before the campaign's fixes. Three final 6-run rounds: **medians 76/51/51, onsets on the {51, 54, 76, 82, 91} event ladder, no frame-10 event** — the residual now expresses only at the mid-run tumbler events. Stress remains nondeterministic at those events; the frontier is unchanged in kind (bistable coloured-GS flips, below API semantics), reduced in rate, and the engine is 1.6× faster for it.

### The parity pivot: the cascade re-measured clean, the sleep story corrected, and a false equilibrium proven (2026-08-15, post-determinism)

**The domino cascade is now a stationary target.** On the lean build: CPU 12/12 toppled bit-identically ×2; GPU exactly 1/12, bit-identically ×6 — the gap is a pure, deterministic semantic divergence, finally measurable without the nondeterminism entangling it. The wave dies at the very first transmission: D1 falls on both solvers, D2 is struck on both (first motion f17 CPU / f21 GPU), tips identically to ~18° by frame 50 — then on the GPU both dominos stop dead (angular speed exactly zero by f60, tilts frozen at 57.2°/18.0° forever) while the CPU keeps rotating through 70°. **The old "energy drained during impact resolution" framing is retracted: this is a quasi-static lean-stall, and it also explains the old paradox that more iterations made the cascade worse — a stiffer static solve holds the lean better.**

**One real semantic divergence found and fixed along the way: the GPU sleep counter had no island gate** (per-body calm only, vs the CPU's two-threshold form: self-calm AND no island member above `wake_thresh`). Shipped as a neighbour-calm gate in `vbd_derive_velocities.slang` — increment only if self-calm and no awake, unlocked *contact neighbour* is above `wake_thresh` (10× the linear threshold, linear only), with the neighbour's post-solve speed recomputed from positions (`|predicted−old|/dt` — derive writes velocity slots concurrently, so reading them would race). Locked neighbours are skipped, matching the CPU's islands. Accepted: heap 65/65 and mound 126/126 bodies bitwise-frozen over the final 100 frames (sleeping intact — the load-bearing ratchet survives since neighbour-gating is far weaker than the failed strict-all-calm form), both deterministic ×2 with re-baselined hashes, drop unchanged (`0x68a34d32b077fbe0`), stress p50 16.3 → 17.8 ms (the walk runs only on self-calm bodies; trim later if it matters).

**But the cascade is unmoved by the gate — and by sleep entirely: with `Physics.velocity_sleep_threshold=0` (nothing can ever sleep), GPU D2 still stops at 18.0° and holds for 550 frames at angular speed 0.0000.** Sleep is exonerated; the stall is a **real false equilibrium in the solve**, and it is *statically impossible with push-only contacts*: a 0.3 m-wide, 2 m-tall domino balances at arctan(0.15/1.0) ≈ 8.5° — at 18° its own weight topples it, never mind D1's push. The solve is therefore producing a non-physical holding force or never completing the fight. Leading suspects: **the sticking-anchor machinery acting as adhesion** at the D1↔D2 face contact or D2's base, and **the convergence stall-exit** (the GPU's convergence deltas are contact-violation maxima from `update_lambda` — a *different metric* from the CPU's — and the stalled-exit may fire every substep mid-fight, freezing the configuration). Next instrument: contact-trace λ comparison on `Domino 2`, CPU vs GPU, frames 55-80 — λn ≈ 0 on the D1 contact means the push is not transmitted; a large base tangential λ means sticking pins; record effective iteration counts in the window alongside.

### CASCADE FIXED: the wake test ran only at substep 0 — 12/12 toppled, heights match the CPU (2026-08-15, final)

**The λ trace overturned the paragraph above, twice.** First: the "statically impossible" claim was wrong — from t55 the GPU shows four persistent D2→D3 contacts at D3's back face 1.9 m up, carrying ~70 N: at 18° of tilt a 2 m domino's top spans the 0.6 m face gap, so the stall is a **propped arch, D1 on D2 on D3 — statically real.** Second: the arch only holds because **D3 is a sleeping, unsolved, immovable wall** — the D3 trace shows its sleep counter pinned at 60 through the entire 70 N push, on the default *and* the "nosleep" configuration — which also **voids both nosleep experiments** (old and new: counters cannot climb with a zero calm threshold, yet they read 60 ⇒ those runs never disabled sleeping; the original "sleeping dominos as immovable walls" hypothesis was correct all along and was dismissed on a void experiment).

**Why D3 never wakes — the exact mechanism, each link measured:** the D2→D3 contact forms mid-tick (substep 1) while D2 still swings at 0.85-0.97 m/s, well above `wake_thresh` — but the GPU's wake evaluation in `vbd_predict.slang` was gated `if (pc.substep == 0)`, so the first evaluation that could see the contact came a tick later, by which time the arch had arrested D2 to 0.167 → 0.006 m/s — **below `wake_thresh`, which at runtime is 0.5 m/s, not 0.01: `physics::data`'s annotated default `velocity_sleep_threshold = 0.05` overrides the `solver_config` struct default of 0.001** (an in-shader probe confirmed the walk saw D2 unlocked, awake, at 0.16665 m/s — and the branch still correctly refused at a 0.5 bar). The CPU topples because its island wake scan runs **every substep** and catches D2's fast window. **Fix: remove the substep gate — motor, contact, and joint wake tests now run every substep, matching the CPU's cadence.**

**Accepted:** cascade **12/12 toppled ×3** with per-domino final heights matching the CPU to ~1-2 cm (D12's 0.15 exactly); drop, heap, and mound hashes **bit-unchanged** from the pre-fix build — the per-substep wake is inert on settling scenes since nobody near a sleeper exceeds 0.5 m/s there; stress p50 17.3 ms. Both sleep-side changes are now in the tree: the neighbour-calm entry gate (derive) and the per-substep wake (predict). The restitution-order alignment task is retired — the cascade was its only evidence, and the cascade was never about restitution (lean speeds sit below the restitution threshold).

### Clock pin refutes the DVFS carrier; the length gate was a rate artifact — the flip is a per-run bistable coin (2026-08-15, clocks locked)

**Clocks locked at 1972 MHz (verified held before and after): three 6-run rounds read medians 10 / 51 / 51 with the frame-10 event firing at the same 2-3-runs-per-round rate as unlocked. The DVFS/clock-transition carrier is refuted** — p50 rose to ~31 ms (below boost, as expected) and the flip rate did not move. **And the length gate is retracted as a hard gate:** twelve 40-frame runs on the new driver produced **two flipped runs (8, 9 — the frame-10 Grid branch, 2 of 12)** where the old driver's eighteen 40-frame runs produced zero. At the old driver's ~1-in-6 per-run rate, eighteen clean short runs had ~4% probability — survivable luck that got over-read as a gate.
