Run length (and tracing, and driver version, and possibly ambient session state — the two flipped runs were consecutive) *modulates the rate*; none of them gates the event. **Final black-box characterization: one marginal contact event resolves bistably inside whichever coloured-GS sweep touches it, with a per-run probability that moves with driver version and run configuration but not with GPU core clocks; Jacobi never flips; all API-level sync is validation-clean; inputs are bit-identical on one clock up to the introducing pass.** The 40-frame repro at ~17% per run (seconds per attempt) is now the cheapest experiment vehicle the campaign has ever had — and the right payload for the NVIDIA report. Software next step remains the dispatch-fold restructure; measurement next step is the DX12 stress sweep.

### STATE OF PLAY — read this first, then the dated sections above only as needed (2026-08-15, end of day)

**Shipping configuration, verified and in the tree** (driver 596.99 — a driver update re-baselines every absolute GPU hash; expect it). Solver: deterministic colour-list flatten, gradient-only fixed-point accumulation, restitution feature-key tiebreak, Hessians in float, original adjacency sort, **stabilize mirrors the main loop's joint split** (jointless via jointless colour lists, jointed via the island path — the joint-colour race is closed). Engine: **intra-pass barriers batched to one `pipeline_barrier` per command boundary** (was one per hazarded buffer per note — ~40k barrier commands/tick down to ~1.5k); fills note the CLEAR stage; twelve solver buffers carry their TRANSFER usage; headless frames honor the fence/binary-semaphore contracts; buffer concurrent-sharing family lists deduplicated. Sleep: **neighbour-calm entry gate in `vbd_derive_velocities.slang`** (counter rises only if self-calm AND no awake unlocked contact neighbour above `wake_thresh`; neighbour speed recomputed from positions — never read the velocity slot, derive writes it concurrently) and **per-substep wake evaluation in `vbd_predict.slang`** (the substep-0-only gate was the domino-cascade root cause). Diagnostics: the ~17 per-tick hash passes are recorded only under **`Physics.trace_hashes`**; `ContactTrace.body` warns if traced without it. `parity_drop_gpu` is bit-identical (`0x68a34d32b077fbe0`); heap/mound deterministic ×2 (`0x64457148b09ad048` / `0x53485b21fba22696`).

**Parity.** **The domino cascade is FIXED: GPU 12/12 toppled ×3, per-domino heights match the CPU to ~1-2 cm** (root cause = wake evaluated only at substep 0 while the CPU scans every substep; the runtime `wake_thresh` is 0.5 m/s because `physics::data`'s annotated default `velocity_sleep_threshold = 0.05` overrides the struct default — the settings-side default always wins). Settling scenes are sub-millimetre (stack 0.465 mm, pyramid ~1.7 mm as last measured — re-verify on this tree); chaotic scenes are ensemble-bounded. The restitution-order divergence (GPU canonical sort vs CPU list order) remains a theoretical difference with no measured failure attached.

**Determinism.** Stress remains nondeterministic at rare mid-run tumbler events (`{51, 76, 91}` ladder on this driver, medians 51-76) — **parked as below-API**: rate scales with driver version (596.75→596.99 = ~5× more frequent) and with barrier-command pressure (batching extinguished the frame-10 Grid seed outright — 45 pairings ×3 rounds, zero), clocks refuted by pinning, Jacobi invariantly clean 15/15 on both drivers, all API sync validation-clean, inputs bit-identical to the introducing pass. The report for NVIDIA is written at `docs/nvidia_determinism_report.md`. Never measure determinism short (40-frame zeros were survivable luck) and never conclude from one round.

**Performance.** Stress GPU p50 **16.3-17.3 ms** at 300 frames (was ~29 pre-campaign): barrier batching −7.5 ms, hash gating −1.7 ms, sleep-gate walk +1.0-1.4 ms (runs only on self-calm bodies; trimmable). Jacobi reads 9.7 ms but is a different algorithm (never quality-audited; CPU is GS — not a parity-preserving swap).

### Lean-stream profile (2026-08-15 evening, VK, stress 300 f, driver 596.99)

The bench summary now carries a **per-pass GPU section** (`ProfileSummary.cppm::write_summary`, sourced from the profile aggregator's gpu domain that the render graph's timestamp queries already feed — they were collected all along and surfaced only in the Editor panel). `--engine-bench-summary-out` on any GPU scenario prints it.

Decomposition (ema per dispatch instance; ×2 substeps for per-frame; frame p50 16.5-16.9 ms):

| stage | per frame | share |
|---|---|---|
| solve_iterations (40 iters × ~19 dispatches) | **12.2 ms** | **~72%** |
| broad_phase | 1.18 ms | 7% |
| narrow_phase | 0.57 ms | 3.4% |
| build_adjacency | 0.48 ms | 2.8% |
| post_stabilize | 0.30 ms | 1.8% |
| restitution + coloring | 0.47 ms | 2.8% |
| grid + sticking + clears + copies + everything else | ~0.5 ms | ~3% |

**Effective iterations (probe recipe below): stress consumes 37.4 of 40 iterations/substep (93%), overlap 89%, heap 43% (settle-then-sleep — the asleep fast path visible).** The adaptive early-exit is nearly inert on the hot scenes; the solve loop's cost is honest work, not skippable overhead. Confirms the recorded "these scenes don't converge early" from the budget-fix day, now measured on 596.99.

**Empty colour launches priced by direct experiment: ~0.3 ms/frame (~2%).** Throwaway host bound `num_colors = 8` (stress peaks 7 live, so physics identical — canary and joint λ unchanged): p50 median 16.294 vs 16.556 ms at bound 16 = 0.26 ms for 640 of the 720 empty `dispatch_indirect`s. Post-barrier-batching an empty indirect dispatch costs ~0.4 µs — the recorded "GPU-driven or lagged-bound" idea buys at most 0.3 ms and risks under-solving on colour-count growth. **Not worth it; parked with numbers.**

**Lever recommendation:** the only remaining item with >1 ms of headroom is restructuring the solve loop itself — fold the ~7 live per-colour dispatches (+update_lambda +joint_lambda +convergence, ~19 dispatches × 80 instances/tick ≈ 153 µs per iteration) into fewer launches (persistent-kernel / single-dispatch-per-iteration direction, cross-workgroup sync design needed; update_lambda fold stays REJECTED — the both-sleeping replay ratchet). Everything else is ≤ 0.5-1.2 ms bites (broad_phase at 1.2 ms is the largest isolated one). Any such restructure re-baselines every GPU hash.

Probe recipe (shader-only, 2 lines, reverted from the shipping tree per the diagnostics convention): in `vbd_convergence_check`, `InterlockedAdd(collision_state[state_stale_read_count_index], 1)` at dispatch top (peak-aggregated by the canary line = per-tick budget sanity) and `InterlockedAdd(collision_state[state_stale_check_count_index], 1)` after the converged-flag early-return (host-summed by the canary = total live iterations; divide by frames × substeps for the average). Slots 56/57 stay reserved for ad-hoc probes.

### Graph-owned readback channels — the scoped design (2026-08-16, after the revert)

**The measurement that killed the quick fix.** Split trace tags inside `read_body_states` (kept in-tree: `vbd_gpu::readback::slot_wait` / `::map`): the stall is **13.2 ms p50 of `wait_for_slot`** at age-1, every tick; the map is ~65 µs. And the age-gate cannot simply be relaxed: `commit_upload` host-writes into the SAME slot and reads 15 µs today only because `wait_for_slot` memoizes per generation — the read's wait absorbs the cost. Make reads non-blocking and the write side un-memoizes and pays the identical 13 ms. **A 2-deep ring (`max_frames_in_flight = 2`) with GPU batches ≈ frame time is saturated: slot reuse forces SOMEONE to wait.** Same law the diag rewrite recorded for its own buffers (triple-buffer because a flip-every-frame double overwrites what a reader holds).

**Design: the render graph owns readback/upload retirement, so no renderer or compute system ever sees a fence.**
1. **Readback channel primitive** (graph-level): a pass declares a buffer host-readable; the graph maintains an N-deep versioned ring of staging copies with per-version retirement tracked by the graph's existing frame/fence knowledge, N = frames_in_flight + 1 = 3 so the oldest version is always retired by construction (the begin-frame ring wait guarantees it — no fence polls, pure age arithmetic, deterministic in lockstep benches). Consumer API: `latest()` → span + generation of the newest retired version; never blocks, never returns an in-flight version. Writers get the oldest version from the graph automatically; never wait.
2. **Upload channels** mirror it for host→GPU inputs (the solver's body/motor/joint/collision-state input buffers): the graph hands the recorder a version whose prior use is retired — kills the write-side wait too.
3. **Solver migration**: physics_snapshot, grounded, collision_state, contact-dump readbacks become graph channels; `wait_for_slot`/`slot_ready` read-side deleted; the solver's 2-slot `m_frames` ring keeps only the GPU-side lineage (body_buffer cross-slot copy), or migrates in a second pass. Game-logic latency becomes a stable 2-3 ticks (the CPU mirror's design lag), frame stall zero.
4. **What it also buys**: the whole mound-campaign class of observation-layer races disappears by construction (nothing can read an unretired version); every future readback (editor tooling, capture, debug dumps) inherits safety for free; the bench/gate stays deterministic and re-baselines once.
5. **Cost**: one extra staging copy per channel (physics snapshot ≈ 1.3 MB — trivial), and a focused session of graph-API work: channel primitive ~200-300 lines in the graph, solver migration ~150, consumer swaps mechanical.
6. **Sim-rate policy under overload stays separate**: with stalls gone the frame never blocks on physics, and the catch-up clamp (`max_fixed_steps`) remains the dial for sim-time dilation.

**IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-16, this session — awaiting build + gate re-baseline).**
- **Primitive**: `gse.gpu:graph_channel` (`Gpu/Graph/GraphChannel.cppm`) — `readback_channel` + `upload_channel`, both N = `max_frames_in_flight + 1` = 3 versions, created via `render_graph::create_readback_channel(size, tag)` / `create_upload_channel(desc, tag)`. Retirement is pure age arithmetic against `frame::frame_count()`: a version recorded at clock R is retired once `frame_count() >= R + max_frames_in_flight`, because `frame::begin` waits every queue fence of the ring slot it reuses. No fence handles anywhere in the API.
- **Readback**: `publish_target(generation, bytes)` advances the ring, stamps generation/frame/size, and returns the staging buffer for the pass to `copy_buffer` into; `latest()` returns `{span, generation}` of the newest retired version, never blocks, never serves age < 2, returns empty until the first publish retires. Per-version published size is what pairs `read_body_states` counts with data (the old `snapshot_body_count`-vs-current-count mismatch class is gone).
- **Upload**: `write_target()` hands the host the oldest version and marks it current; `current()` is what the record path binds. Reuse safety is by construction (3 versions, ≤1 write/frame ⇒ the reused version's last bind is ≥2 frames retired); `write_target` asserts the cadence (`blocking.written_frame + max_frames_in_flight <= frame_count()`).
- **Solver migration**: the six readbacks (snapshot, grounded, collision_state+narrow-debug, contact dump, adjacency meta ×2-region, contact adjacency) are channels; the nine pure inputs (body_input, motor, motor_map, solver_config, impulse, jointed_pairs, island, body_env, static_bodies) are upload channels bound per tick via `current().slot()`; `wait_for_slot`/`slot_ready`/`retired_snapshot_slot`/`snapshot_buffer`/`snapshot_body_count` and all per-slot readback/input buffers + recorded_ring/frame stamps are DELETED. `m_frames` keeps only the GPU-lineage buffers (body/contact/joint/adjacency/grid/color/indirect/grounded/...).
- **Two special cases**: (1) collision_state is live GPU state, not an input — the per-tick host header-zero became a `fill_buffer` recorded as the tick's FIRST command (before the entry-hash dispatches, which write header slots; a later fill would erase them). (2) joint_buffer keeps its per-slot lineage (cross-slot copy at tick end); the rare refresh host-write became an upload channel + `copy_buffer(channel.current(), f.joint_buffer)` in the clear stage under the same dirty condition — CPU-wins on refresh preserved, host never touches live GPU state anymore.
- **Consumer swaps**: all `read_*`/`diagnostics`/`query_body_snapshot`/`retired_generation` keep their signatures (ShadowStep/ContactTrace/System.cpp untouched); PhysicsDebugRenderer's direct `snapshot_buffer(...).host_read()` became `read_body_states()`. `render_snapshot_slot`/`body_buffer(slot)` (GPU-side render mirror) unchanged. `vbd_gpu::readback::slot_wait` trace tag is gone (nothing waits); `::map` kept on `read_body_states`.
- **Expected on first build**: frame stall zero (the 13.2 ms p50 `wait_for_slot` and the write-side twin are structurally impossible); game-logic latency a stable 2-3 ticks (mirror consumes gen−2); ONE hash re-baseline everywhere (mirror lag 1→2 shifts what ECS sees and what re-uploads; the shift is deterministic in lockstep benches). Color-launch hint now lags one extra tick — floor-8/margin-4 still covers every gate scene (max live colours 7). Gate protocol: full round both backends, re-pin every GPU hash, 3 validated rounds before any promotion.

**UNIFICATION SURVEY + DEAD-CODE SWEEP (2026-08-16, follow-up leg).** Audited every remaining `host_read`/`host_write`/`per_frame_resource` site for hand-rolled channel policy. Verdicts:
- **Not duplicates — left on the frame ring's native idiom**: (1) the ~40 renderer per-frame buffers (camera UBOs, instance/light/bone buffers, ...) are same-frame uploads — written and consumed inside one frame, slot-indexed by `current_frame()`, retired by `begin`'s own ring wait; they hand-roll no stamps, no waits, no validity flags, and migrating them would add a third buffer per resource for zero removed invariants. (2) `render_graph::m_profile_slots` and CaptureRenderer screenshots use consume-on-ring-reuse (read the slot exactly when `begin` recycles it) — retirement is the ring wait itself; profile slots would additionally need new RHI (query→buffer resolve on both backends) to become buffer channels. (3) the Vulkan video encoder's bitstream ring is backend-internal (layering forbids `gse.vulkan` → `gse.gpu`) and is retired by encode-feedback queries, not the frame ring. The channel abstraction is specifically for CROSS-frame host↔GPU handoff with decoupled producer/consumer — these are all same-frame or consume-on-reuse.
- **Deleted dead code**: `frame::wait_for_frame_fences` (the solver was its only caller — the last hand-rolled wait surface is gone); `buffer::mark_host_dirty` (no callers; `host_dirty` itself is LOAD-BEARING — the graph reads it in `append_host_dirty_barriers` to emit host→device visibility barriers, which the upload channels rely on); `gpu_solver::total_substeps()` (no external callers, body was `1 * m_steps`); the entire dead warm-start-count chain recorded on the warm-start day — `m_warm_start_count` (always 0), the `warm_start_count` push-constant field (zero shader reads; PC struct is codegen'd from the C++ struct so both sides shrink from one authority), the `warm` locals and the 6th `make_pc` parameter across all ~52 call sites, and the `state_warm_start_count_index` header-slot name (zero readers in C++ and slang; header slot 3 is now genuinely free).

### First post-channel interactive profile + generation-gated async dispatch (2026-08-16, third leg)

**Profile read (Sandbox.53144, user's first channel build, stress-class interactive scene): the channels verify, and the degenerate loop is NOT GPU throughput.** `vbd_gpu::readback` 34 µs/f, `::map` 0.1 µs, upload+commit ~16 µs — the 13.2 ms observation stall is dead as designed. But: frame 92-100 ms with **67-73 ms inside `begin_frame::wait_fence`** while TOTAL measured GPU work is ~10-15 ms/f (solve 9.5 ms/f at 0.89 calls/f) — the GPU is ~85% idle. Physics ticks on only ~0.3 of frames. Log findings: present mode **Immediate**; **`graphics-hook64.dll` (OBS-class game-capture hook) injected**, driver present worker threads parked in waits; **"present pacing degraded after 600 presents: feedback=true samples_seen=401 samples_used=61"** — the pacer rejects 85% of its samples yet `has_feedback()` stays true, so `begin` keeps feeding its `frame_delta` into `system_clock`. Consistent story: hook+Immediate garble present timing → pacer's model collapses → sim dt runs far below wall (hence 0.3 ticks/frame = slow-motion) while the fence chain serializes ~2 frames behind (hence the 73 ms begin wait on an idle GPU). The boot-time watchdog stalls in the log are the known non-boot-critical-compile class, separate. **Engine defect exposed regardless of the hook: the pacer health check only LOGS — a pacer that fails its own `used*4 < seen` predicate keeps driving dt. Discriminator for the user meanwhile: close the capture hook app and/or switch present mode to Fifo and re-profile.**

**PACER HEALTH FIX SHIPPED (same day, fourth leg — user confirmed the slow-mo persisted on the gate build, expected since the gate is inert while the GPU idles).** Second profile (Sandbox.26552): frame 90.9 ms/11 fps, wait_fence 49 ms, ticks still 0.34/frame — same pathology. Two layers in `present_pacer` + `frame::begin`:
1. **Burst rejection at the source**: a gap-averaged pair (`id_delta > 1`) whose `per_frame < refresh/2` is an Immediate-mode flush artifact, not display cadence — rejected before it can touch `m_interval`/`m_smoothed`/`samples_used` (the sub-refresh garbage-interval injector; snapped `id_delta == 1` pairs floor at 1× refresh and never go sub-refresh). Rejected pairs no longer reset `m_observes_since_use`, so a sustained-burst regime ages the held interval toward the 900-observe cutoff instead of keeping it alive forever.
2. **Rolling health verdict owns the dt gate**: per-120-observe windows (~2 s at 60 fps) compute `unhealthy = window_seen == 0 || window_used * 4 < window_seen`; `begin` submits `frame_delta` into `system_clock` only when `has_feedback() && healthy()`. Unhealthy ⇒ the clock's existing `snap_delta(wall_delta)` fallback drives dt (verified present in `system_clock::update` — refresh-snapped wall timing, not raw). Health flips log once per transition (window-bounded, no spam); the one-shot 600-present warning now reads the same `healthy()` authority instead of duplicating the ratio.
**VERIFIED LIVE (agent build + run, same session): frame 90-100 ms/10 fps → 4.15 ms/241 fps on the same machine with the capture hook still injected and Immediate mode granted.** Build compiled clean first try (952/955 link). Flip line fired at present 239 (~2 s): "present pacing degraded (samples_seen=204 samples_used=61): dt now driven by snapped CPU loop timing"; the 600-present warning confirms healthy=false from the same authority. `wait_fence` 49-73 → 2.3 ms; 17.3k frames over ~72 s, clean exit, no asserts, no channel errors. **The "remaining external hook serialization" theory is RETRACTED — the whole degenerate equilibrium was self-sustained by the poisoned dt; killing the clock input collapsed it entirely, hook and all.** Caveat: the verification run boots the default (near-empty-physics) world — the user's heavy scene re-adds solver load and is where the async gate first sees real use.

**SHIPPED: generation-gated async dispatch (`Physics.gpu_async_dispatch`, default OFF).** The re-scope the reverted attempt was groping for, now trivial on the channel foundation:
- Readiness = `gpu_solver::latest_dispatch_complete()`: TRUE when never-dispatched or `retired_generation() == dispatch_generation()` (pure channel generation arithmetic — the common case, no fence anything); in the age-1 window it polls `frame::queue_fence_signaled(compute, recorded_ring)` (new: `wait_for_fence` with `timeout_ns = 0`); same-clock query (`frame_count() == recorded_frame`, the frozen-clock/minimized construction) reports incomplete rather than lying via a stale-signaled fence. The bootstrap deadlock of the reverted leg cannot be re-expressed: generations compare publisher-to-consumer, not slot parity.
- Gate in `update_vbd_gpu`, after the readback/mirror block (reads are channel-served and never gated — the canary-blindness trap is structurally gone): when enabled && !reset && previous batch incomplete → bank steps into `d.gpu_step_debt` (cap 4, excess drops = deliberate dilation, the spiral cannot ratchet) and fall into the existing `steps <= 0` path (which already banks impulses and keeps publishing the render snapshot + body map); when complete → dispatch `min(steps + debt, 4)`. Reset bypasses the gate (a reseed is safe to queue behind an executing batch — the upload channels made host writes unconditionally safe; the gate is pacing, not safety).
- Debt cleared on reset. ShadowStep's own solver ignores the setting. Kinematic targets still advance by consumed steps (the recorded stage-2 artifact, bounded by the debt cap; fix = advance by dispatched steps, deferred).
- **Default OFF because the skip decision follows real GPU timing: hash-comparable runs and headless benches must keep it off (headless async also re-enters the unpaced-frame-loop TDR regime). Enable interactively via the settings UI.** Note the gate does NOT fix the pacer-driven slow-motion above — it removes the catch-up batch multiplication and decouples frame pacing from physics; the sim-clock fix is separate.

### Stress frame anatomy SOLVED by per-queue attribution (2026-08-16, fifth leg — agent-run A/B)

Instrument: `begin_frame::wait_fence` split per queue (graphics/compute/video_encode sub-tags, kept in-tree). Vehicle: `render_stress` scenario (windowed, auto-spawns the stress workload; `--engine-use-gpu-solver` — bool flags are BARE; the sandbox-level `--use-gpu-solver` feeds only parity mode, a trap worth remembering; `--engine-setting Physics.gpu_async_dispatch=true` for the gate arm). Two 900-frame runs, same machine:

| | gate OFF | gate ON |
|---|---|---|
| frame p50 | 32.8 ms | 27.1 ms |
| frame p95 / p99 | 36.0 / 38.4 ms | **120.1 / 131.8 ms** |
| wait_fence::graphics (EMA) | **24.3 ms** | 23.3 ms |
| wait_fence::compute | **4.6 µs** | 2.7 µs |
| GPU solve/frame | 23.2 ms | 22.5 ms |

**FINDINGS, two of them corrections:**
1. **The begin wait is the GRAPHICS fence, not compute — the compute ring fence never blocks (µs).** The gate was aimed at the wrong edge: the frame is serialized behind physics through the CROSS-QUEUE SEMAPHORE — the render mirror (`physics_transform`) reads `body_buffer(render_snapshot_slot())` GPU-side, and that slot is written by the SAME frame's physics batch (both slots are: the batch writes its dispatch slot AND cross-slot-copies at the tick end), so graphics(F) waits compute(F)'s entire ~23 ms batch, and the graphics fence inherits physics latency. frame ≈ physics batch + graphics, serialized — 32.8 ms ✓. The user's interactive 43-49 ms waits are the same mechanism amplified by catch-up multi-tick batches.
2. **The gate as shipped is NET NEGATIVE on stress: p50 −5.7 ms but p95/p99 ×3.4.** Mechanism: skip-then-drain oscillation — banked steps drain into multi-tick batches, and every drained batch stalls graphics twice as long. It also cannot help the real edge (same-frame semaphore chain, not the ring fence). Candidate salvage: cap the drain at `min(steps + debt, 2)`; but with finding 1, the gate's premise (compute-fence coupling) is measured dead on this scene — leave OFF, decide salvage vs delete after the graphics decoupling.
3. **The pacer fix stands verified in these runs too**: windowed-bench presents get no usable timing (`samples_used=0`), health flags it, dt falls back snapped — no slow-mo, no runaway (the 10 fps class is dead).
4. **The actual lever for interactive stress: decouple rendering from the in-flight batch.** Options, needing a ruling: (a) early-copy render buffer — first pass of the batch copies pre-solve body state to a dedicated render buffer the transform renderer binds, and the graph signals it as its own submission so graphics waits only the copy, not the solve (graph submission-split work); (b) render from the CPU mirror (channel-fed, gen−2) under overload — zero GPU dependency, latency traded for frame rate; (c) accept the serialization and shrink the batch (the "tick cheaper" scope below — 23 ms is the honest price of 40 iterations × 2 substeps on this scene interactively).
Side-finding parked: with the CPU solver active, `contact_trace` burns 20.8 ms/frame p50 of self-time on stress — diagnostic overhead worth its own look.

### Render decoupling SHIPPED + gate salvaged (2026-08-16, sixth leg — agent A/B verified)

**Built per ruling (a):** `vbd_render_mirror_stage` — an `early_signal()` compute pass right after apply_body_inputs copies the pre-solve body state into `per_frame_data::render_body_buffer` (device-local, one per solver slot — alternation rides `m_dispatch_slot` like every other per-slot buffer; accessor serves `m_frames[1 - m_dispatch_slot]`, the just-dispatched slot; the renderer binds via `gpu_solver_frame_info.snapshot = render_body_buffer()`, one authority for every GPU consumer). First cut hand-rolled a separate two-buffer array with its own slot flip and was rightly rejected — per-dispatch GPU state belongs in `per_frame_data`, not a parallel ring. Graph side: `early_signal` flows builder→descriptor→`render_pass_data`; the submission builder SEGMENTS aux queues at early-signal passes (each segment gets its own timeline value, first segment carries the cross-queue producer waits, self-chain via monotonic values); graphics waits resolve VALUE-AWARE via `queue_wait_positions` (max sorted position of the producer pass each consumer queue depends on) → the earliest covering segment. Frame::end's `last_for_queue` fence logic already handles multiple submissions per queue, so ring-fence retirement (the channel guarantee) is untouched. `render_snapshot_slot`/`body_buffer(slot)` deleted (frame-info helper is the only consumer surface). Gate salvage: drain cap = at most ONE banked step per dispatched batch (`steps + min(debt, 1)`), bank cap 4 unchanged.

**Debug detour worth remembering: the first A/B showed ZERO change — graphics' max compute dependency resolved to `vbd_state_copy_stage` (pos 43), not the mirror (pos 6). A throwaway wait-resolution log (3 lines, removed after) named the cause: `PhysicsTransformRenderer` carried an explicit `.after<vbd_state_copy_stage>()` from the old read-the-lineage-buffer architecture — a stale ORDERING dependency no resource read backs. Repointed to `.after<vbd_render_mirror_stage>()`. Lesson: explicit `.after<>()` deps are invisible to resource-level reasoning — audit them when re-plumbing producers.**

**Verified (render_stress, 900 frames, windowed, GPU solver, same-block):**
| | pre-(a) gate off | (a) gate off | (a) gate on |
|---|---|---|---|
| frame p50 | 32.8 ms | 31.3 ms | **15.4 ms** |
| frame p95 / p99 | 36.0 / 38.4 | 35.1 / 38.5 | 83.0 / 91.7 |
| wait_fence::graphics | 24.3 ms | **2.8 µs** | 0.5 ms |
| wait_fence::compute | 4.6 µs | 21.8 ms | 21.3 ms EMA (median ~0, tail-contaminated) |

Reading: **graphics is fully decoupled (24.3 ms → 2.8 µs)**. Gate-off p50 barely moves because sync mode still runs 1 tick/frame of a ~23 ms batch — the wait simply moved to the compute ring fence, which is the honest, irreducible cost of sync pacing until the tick shrinks. **Gate-on halves the median frame (32.8 → 15.4, display-rate) with sim dilation under load; the p95/99 tails (83/92, was 126/136 pre-cap) are the +1 drains — a drained 2-tick batch (~47 ms GPU) occasionally stalls the ring.** Tail dial if wanted: drain 0 = flat frames, no catch-up (one-line change). No cross-queue drops, no new errors, clean runs. Next per ruling: (c) — every ms off the 23 ms tick now pays 1:1 in gate-off frames and shrinks gate-on tails.

### (c) rung 1 SHIPPED: the solve-sweep fold — bit-exact, measured (2026-08-16, seventh leg)

**Probe that justified it (same-block, render_stress):** GS colour-serialized loop 12.57 ms/substep vs Jacobi single-wide-dispatch 2.58 ms/substep at the same iteration cap and comparable per-iteration body work ⇒ the multi-dispatch structure, not solve math, dominated.

**Built: `vbd_solve_sweep.slang` behind `Physics.gpu_solve_fold` (default OFF).** One dispatch per iteration replaces the per-colour jointless loop: fixed `solve_sweep_workgroups = 32` workgroups stride every colour list, colours sequenced by a device-scope two-phase ticket barrier (arrive slot 3 — zeroed per substep by collision_reset; phase slot 53 — monotonic, wrap-safe subtraction compare; `sweep_spin_cap` bails instead of hanging; atomic-read via InterlockedAdd-0). Reuses `solve_body` by listing the solve_color body file as a second helper (**helper paths root at `Shaders/`, body files at `Shaders/Bodies/` — a helper reference to a body file needs the `Bodies/` prefix; the miss asserts in a loop at boot**). Islands/lambda/joint/convergence dispatches unchanged (update_lambda fold stays rejected; the refuted micro-fold stays refuted). Jointed scenes fold only the jointless block; post-stabilize block NOT folded (v1 scope). Delta-zero placement preserved in the last colour phase.

**ACCEPTANCE (fresh binary, verified exit codes after two false starts — a `| tail` masked a failed ninja once, and a zombie run-chain held the exe against the linker):**
- **drop: fold-off == fold-on == `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` (the pinned ledger baseline). heap: both == `0x64457148b09ad048` (also the pinned baseline). THE SWEEP IS BIT-EXACT** — within-colour work is body-parallel and the delta accumulation is InterlockedMax (order-free), as constructed.
- **Perf: drop p50 2.17 → 1.59 ms (−27%); heap 2.92 → 2.05 ms (−30%); stress p50 ~30-32.8 → 25.8 ms with the tightest tails of the day (p95/p99 28.7/29.9).** Implied batch ~23.5 → ~18 ms. The remaining gap to Jacobi's 2.6 = inter-colour SM drain (semantic, survives any fold) + lambda/island/conv launches.
- Stress hash moves under fold (open-nondeterminism scene anyway; settling scenes are the identity proof). Default stays OFF until the standing 3-round gate protocol runs on both backends; because drop/heap prove BIT-IDENTITY, promotion to default-on re-baselines NOTHING on the settling ladder.

Remaining (c) rungs: gate rounds → default-on decision; stabilize-block fold; per-dispatch gap instrument to size the next lever (multi-iteration persistence vs occupancy shaping); coloring split at scale.

**(c) remainder executed (2026-08-16, eighth leg):**
- **Stabilize-block fold shipped** — same swap as the main loop (the converged-flag interplay is safe: derive_velocities clears it before stabilize, the recorded invariant). Bit-identity RE-VERIFIED with it in: drop/heap fold-on == pinned baselines; perf gained again (drop p50 1.53 ms, heap 1.91 ms).
- **Gap instrument run and reverted (throwaway two-pass split of each iteration): sweep+island phase = 13.0 ms/frame = 94% of the folded solve; lambda+joint+convergence tail = 0.8 ms = 6%.** Verdict: launch folding is DONE as a lever class — the tail is not worth touching (consistent with the refuted micro-fold), and the next lever is INSIDE the sweep: colour/occupancy shaping (fewer, fatter colours; workgroup count tuning) or multi-iteration persistence (modest — iteration budget is 93% honest). The instrument recipe (split the iteration loop into sweep-pass + tail-pass, read the two stage rows, revert) is cheap to rebuild when needed.
- **`parity_gate.py` gained `--extra-setting Category.key=value` (repeatable, appended to every scenario run)** — the mechanism for gating candidate settings like the fold without editing entry tables.
- **Side fix while building: `scenario::info::settings` widened `char[4][96]` → `char[8][96]`** — the user's new in-progress showcase scenarios pin six settings and the four-slot capacity failed the build mid-session; the registrations were correct, the capacity was the defect.
- **DEFAULT FLIP RETRACTED SAME DAY — INTERACTIVE STALL CLASS FOUND.** User's first interactive stress run on the default-on build: perf much improved (~40 fps vs the old degenerate states) but a **15+ second `engine::render` stall** ~30 s in — watchdog dumps #3-#7 show the main thread parked in a Vulkan fence wait inside the driver, GPU stuck, NO device-lost. New symptom + new default ⇒ attributed to the sweep's spin barrier: **the grid barrier is not preemption-safe co-resident with heavy graphics** — when the scheduler preempts sweep workgroups for graphics, the resident leaders spin, and at `sweep_spin_cap = 4M` each bail burns up to ~1 s of L2-atomic spam, compounding across colours × iterations into multi-second episodes. The bench regime (light graphics) never triggers it. ACTIONS: `Physics.gpu_solve_fold` default back to **false** (describe text now carries the hazard); `sweep_spin_cap` 4M → 64K (bails in ms, not seconds — also protects bench-regime occupancy shortfalls). Fold remains opt-in and fully valid for benches/headless (all gate results below stand). **NEXT-SESSION SCOPE — contention-safe sweep:** (i) bail-fast + host-visible bail flag (a header slot the diagnostics readback surfaces) with automatic per-tick fallback to the unfolded path after a bail; (ii) or drop grid sync entirely via one-dispatch-per-colour retained but batched into a single command buffer... (already the unfolded path); (iii) or VK_KHR_cooperative_matrix-adjacent... realistically: bail-fast + auto-fallback is the pragmatic design; evaluate NVIDIA preemption behavior with a graphics-saturated A/B before re-attempting default-on. The 40 fps itself is sync pacing at the ~25 ms unfolded interactive batch — `Physics.gpu_async_dispatch` (off by default) is the built dial for display-rate frames + sim dilation; true sync 60 needs the inside-sweep occupancy lever.

- **GATE PROTOCOL PASSED AND DEFAULT FLIPPED (same day, retracted above — fold validity for bench/headless regimes unaffected): 3 required-tier rounds (drop, pair, stack, cluster, heap, mound, pyramid, domino) × `--runs 2` × both backends with the fold on — 38 checks per round, 0 required failures every round; one hash per config throughout; pyramid positional 1.721 mm = the exact pre-fold value.** The 4 report-only variations per round are the pre-existing documented drop/pair CPU↔GPU identity reports (CPU-side drift class; GPU at its pinned baselines) — not the fold. `Physics.gpu_solve_fold` now defaults ON; default-path smoke: drop with zero flags == `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` (pinned baseline), stress default p50 25.6 ms (was ~30-32.8 unfolded). The full-ladder round including the report-only heavies (pile/overlap/stress) is deferred to the next unattended gate session — required-tier is the gating contract, and bit-identity means those entries' fold behavior is expected unchanged. Next lever when scheduled: inside-the-sweep occupancy/colour shaping (the 94% phase), per the gap-instrument verdict.

### Contention-safe sweep BUILT (2026-08-16, ninth leg — unmeasured; interactive A/B owed before any default re-flip)

**Built per the eighth-leg scope (i): bail-fast + host-visible bail flag + automatic per-tick fallback to the unfolded path.**

**Shader (`vbd_solve_sweep.slang`):** new header slot `state_sweep_bail_index = 58` (`collision_state_header_uints` 58 → 59; every size/base derives from the constant on both sides — buffer size, batch-start fill, readback copy, narrow-phase debug base all shift together). The slot sits ≥ `state_tick_hash_base_index`, so `collision_reset` never touches it — batch-scoped by construction: zeroed by the batch-start fill, survives every substep, lands in the tick-end readback copy. Mechanics:
- A leader that exhausts `sweep_spin_cap` (64K) InterlockedAdd-counts itself into the bail slot instead of silently falling through unsynced.
- Spinning leaders poll the bail slot every 256 spins (~0.4% extra atomic traffic) and abandon the spin the moment anyone has bailed; a barrier entered after a bail skips arrive/spin entirely.
- Every sweep dispatch entry-checks the slot and returns whole-dispatch when set — the remaining iterations × substeps of the batch collapse to no-ops. Total burn per episode is bounded: ≤ one spin cap per resident leader, once, instead of cap × colours × iterations.
- **The bail decision is leader-mediated through `gs_sweep_bail` + `AllMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync` so the branch is group-uniform. This is load-bearing, not style: the flag has concurrent same-dispatch writers, so a per-thread read can diverge within a workgroup, and a divergent return around a group barrier is UB (part of the group parks at a sync the rest never reaches). The converged-flag plain read at entry stays safe only because its writer is a prior fenced dispatch — do not copy that shape for any flag written mid-dispatch.**
- A bailed batch leaves arrive/phase garbage; nothing reads them again (entry checks short-circuit) and the next batch's fill re-zeroes. Post-bail physics for the batch: remaining sweeps skip, so bodies hold predicted positions while islands/lambda/convergence still run — one soft batch, then unfolded.

**No-bail invariance:** the added operations are atomic reads (InterlockedAdd-0), one groupshared broadcast, and one extra group sync per barrier plus a dispatch-entry check — the write set and colour order are untouched, so **fold-on bit-identity is preserved by construction; the settling-ladder pinned baselines (drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0`, heap `0x64457148b09ad048`) are expected to hold and nothing re-baselines.** A verification round should confirm before any default discussion.

**Host:** `solver_diagnostics.sweep_bails` surfaces the slot through the existing collision-state channel (readback lag ~2 ticks, same as every diagnostic). At the synced hint point in `update_vbd_gpu`, the System latches `d.gpu_sweep_fold_bailed` on nonzero and warns once ("dropping to the unfolded colour loop..."); `solver_config_from_settings` derives `use_solve_fold = gpu_solve_fold && !gpu_sweep_fold_bailed`, and since `prepare` reconfigures from settings every tick, the fallback is per-tick automatic with ~2 ticks of detection latency (1-2 further folded batches may bail cheaply before the fallback lands). Re-arm: rising edge of `Physics.gpu_solve_fold` (toggle off→on re-tries the fold) or a world reset; both clear the latch. Known conservative corner: right after a re-arm the channel can still serve a 1-2 generation stale view whose bail count re-latches — self-limited, fails toward the unfolded path, not worth machinery. Runtime fields `gpu_sweep_fold_bailed`/`gpu_solve_fold_prev` sit beside `gpu_step_debt` (un-annotated, invisible to ini/UI). The gpu diagnostics peak line now prints `sweep bails {}`; the setting's describe text carries the new behavior.

**NOT yet measured — the standing order from the retraction holds: evaluate NVIDIA preemption behavior with a graphics-saturated interactive A/B (render_stress windowed, `--engine-use-gpu-solver`, fold forced on via `--engine-setting`) before re-attempting default-on.** What the A/B must show: the 15+ s `engine::render` stall class gone, a single warning line + fallback on contention, and bench-regime perf/hashes unchanged with the fold on.

**FIRST INTERACTIVE EVIDENCE (same day, user's tumbler runs): the bail machinery worked live** — fold-on run bailed at boot (3 starved leaders), one warning line, fallback engaged, no watchdog stall, clean exit — the 15 s stall class did not reproduce. Two findings from those runs: (1) the 20 fps class on the tumbler scene was NOT the fold — it was `max_solver_iterations = 40` on a churn scene that never converges (tick ~20+ ms) × clock catch-up (`steps` = 4 cap) × ring-fence serialization; capping iterations at 15 recovered 131-182 fps. The async gate is INERT in that attractor: it skips-when-busy but dispatches `steps + drained` when free, and `steps` is already 4 — a dispatch-quantity cap is the missing dial (not built). (2) **A single boot-transient bail latched the fold off for the whole session** — the latch was too sticky.

### Contention-safe sweep round 2: reseed re-arm + occupancy dials (2026-08-16, tenth leg — built, unmeasured)

- **Fold re-arms on reseed**: the synced-point re-arm now also fires on `gpu_solver::reseeding()` (one tick per reseed, observed at the tick after commit derives `m_apply_all_body_inputs`), so every spawn retries the fold — a retry-bail costs milliseconds under the ninth-leg machinery. Warning text + describe updated. Stale-readback corner (reseed within ~2 ticks of the original bail re-latches from a stale generation) accepted — self-corrects on the next reseed.
- **Cooldown auto-retry added same day after the first live run exposed the reseed re-arm's adversarial timing**: the spawn-tick re-arm retries one tick later — inside the spawn's own contention spike — re-bails (observed live: exactly one 1-leader bail episode at the spawn, then latched-off for the whole session since no further reseed came), so a bail now also schedules an automatic re-arm after `sweep_retry_cooldown_frames = 600` frames (~4-10 s by frame rate). ~~Justified by the same run: a bail episode produced zero slow-frame warnings — one soft batch, visually free~~ **RETRACTED by the eleventh leg: "zero slow-frame warnings" only bounded the episode below the 100 ms warn threshold — the bail was a ~10-16 ms whole-GPU event the instrument could not see.** The cooldown ticks at the synced point (per frame, GPU path only); reseed/toggle re-arms zero it. The stale-readback window (~2-3 ticks) is far inside the cooldown, so an expiry retry cannot re-latch from stale data.

### Bail = L2-atomic storm; spin backoff + retry backoff (2026-08-16/17, eleventh leg — built, unmeasured)

**FINDING (credit: the profiler peak@frame instrument, other session): each bail was a ~10-16 ms whole-GPU stall, and the "cloud spikes" were its shadow.** Evidence: across seven runs, `cloud_raymarch` peak ≈ `solve_iterations` peak on adjacent frames (different queues), cloud a touch below in six of seven — the cloud pass wasn't doing 13-14 ms of work, it was time-slicing behind the storm; the newest run's five bail warnings match five paired spikes at exactly the retry cadence. The magnitude was always predictable: the 4M-cap retraction estimated "up to ~1 s of L2-atomic burn" per bail ⇒ 64K cap ⇒ ~16 ms — the paired peaks read 13.6-14.2. Mechanism: a starving leader burns the FULL cap of back-to-back atomic reads before the flag exists for others to see, ~31 leaders on one cacheline saturate L2, and every co-resident pass inherits the duration. **The tenth-leg "clouds stretch the batch" reading had the arrow backwards for the spike class** (the 53052 window likely mixed both: real cloud load as the starve trigger, the storm as the amplifier — one run's cloud peak ABOVE solve says genuine cloud-dominant moments still exist). **Correction to the other session's mechanism claim, for the record: there is NO same-frame "abandoned attempt + fallback re-run" — a bailed batch no-ops its remaining sweeps and the fallback lands 2-3 ticks later via the readback latch; the 14 ms is the spin burn itself, which is why it is tunable, not fundamental.**

**Built:**
- **Spin backoff in `sweep_grid_barrier`**: the spin loop is now `sweep_spin_checks` phase+bail checks (512 at first build; raised to 1024 after the validation run — all three of its bails were 1-leader marginal starves against the ~2 ms tolerance the backoff had quietly imposed, vs the old cap's ~3-15 ms; waiting is nearly free post-backoff so tolerance is cheap) separated by an exponentially growing ALU-delay gap (`sweep_spin_delay_start = 64` LCG steps doubling to `sweep_spin_delay_max = 8192`; the accumulator feeds a never-true guarded atomic read so it cannot be DCE'd). A starving leader now issues ~1K atomic reads instead of 64K+ back-to-back — the L2 storm collapses ~64× while healthy barriers keep sub-µs check granularity early (delays 64-512 cover the first ~2 µs) and worst-case bail latency stays ~2 ms. Bail-flag propagation improved (checked every interval, was every 256 spins). `sweep_spin_cap` deleted; no-bail results untouched (no new writes on the healthy path) ⇒ bit-identity preserved.
- **Exponential retry backoff host-side**: cooldown 600 → 1200 → 2400 → 4800 frames per consecutive bail (cap ×8), reset by a 600-frame calm folded stretch, reseed, toggle, or reset. Warning line now prints the scheduled retry distance.

**Validation criteria (next runs): the paired solve/cloud ~14 ms peaks collapse to ≤~3 ms on bail frames (the peak@frame column shows it directly); bail warnings thin out under sustained contention (backoff cadence 4→8→16→32 s); fold uptime rises (solve/substep EMA drifts toward the folded ~2.6-2.8 at cap 6). Cloud AVERAGE cost (~1.4-2.2 ms, camera-dependent) remains real graphics work — the raymarch worst-case bound stays on the graphics list on its own merits, demoted from spike-cause.**

**VALIDATED (first run on the build, 2026-08-17 01:20): solve peak 14.2 → 8.0 ms, cloud peak 13.8 → 7.3 ms — still paired (frames 2143/2144, the shadow mechanism confirmed again) at half size, spikes 8 → 2 both rows; the residual ≈ the ~2 ms backoff-wait + genuinely elevated co-load on the trigger frame.** Retry ladder observed live: three warnings with escalating retry distances 600/1200/2400 exactly per design. Frame EMA 5.71 ms / 175 fps; cap-6 conflicts 29 total over 5.7k ticks. All three bails were 1-leader marginal ⇒ the checks bump above (tolerance ~2 → ~4 ms) aims to convert them into successful waits; expect fold uptime (and the solve EMA) to improve on the next run.
- **`Physics.gpu_color_cap` (0 = off, default)** — the "fewer, fatter colours" occupancy experiment as an opt-in. The cap rides the coloring dispatch's previously-always-zero `pc.color_count`; in `collision_build_coloring` the JP palette becomes `pc.color_count != 0 ? pc.color_count : max_colors`, and a body with no conflict-free colour under the cap takes `my_priority % palette` deterministically instead of opening a new colour — the existing conflict scan counts the resulting same-colour pairs (peak line surfaces them). Cap-off is bit-identical by construction (palette = max_colors reproduces the old ternary exactly; pc bytes unchanged since the slot was already 0). Conflicting pairs degrade toward undamped Jacobi for the iteration — small caps can destabilize stiff piles (describe carries the warning; cap 1 ≈ pure undamped Jacobi, expect divergence). Downstream needs nothing: max_used_color shrinks, the colour-launch hint follows, both folded and unfolded paths run fewer/fatter dispatches.
- **`Physics.gpu_sweep_workgroups` (0 = built-in 32, clamp 256)** — the sweep's width is now `pc.sweep_workgroups` (new push-constant field, set from config in make_pc; dispatch sites use the same resolved value, so arrive target, stride, and dispatch always agree). This is the ledger's "workgroup count tuning" half of the occupancy lever: residency oversubscription now degrades to a bail + fallback instead of a stall, so the dial is safe to probe on real hardware. Note the width helps at scale (colour lists wider than 2048 threads); small scenes are bounded by per-colour population — the cap is the small-scene lever, the width is the 16k lever.
- **Multi-iteration persistence DECLINED with data**: the eighth-leg gap instrument already priced the per-iteration launch tail at 6% of the folded solve (94% = in-sweep execution), and folding update_lambda into the sweep is on the refuted ledger (replay ratchet). Persistence across iterations buys the sweep's own launch only — noise. Do not rebuild without new evidence.
- Defaults for all of it are off/zero ⇒ the default dispatch stream and every gate hash are unchanged; the two dials are results-changing when enabled (cap changes colouring; width is bit-inert by the sweep's order-free construction but treat as experimental). Measurement owed: same-block interactive A/B on the tumbler scene — colors row, conflicts count, solve ms/substep, and stability by eye across cap ∈ {0, 6, 4, 3} and width ∈ {32, 64, 128}.

### Dynamic iterations + auto colour cap (2026-08-17, twelfth leg — built, unmeasured)

Context: post-storm-fix profile has solve 2.5 ms/f (35.7%) and clouds 2.3 ms/f (32.9%) as co-leaders at 142 fps EMA. Physics being #1 triggered the two dynamic policies designed earlier the same day. **Hard rule both follow: dynamic solver policies consume DETERMINISTIC inputs only (sim state, GPU-computed counters) — never timing — so lockstep determinism and hash-comparability survive; both default off; enabling either is results-changing (own gate round before any default-on).**

- **Motion-scaled convergence threshold (`Physics.convergence_speed_scale`, float 0-0.25, default 0, `gse::shared`).** Effective linear threshold = `max(convergence_threshold_linear, scale × max_dynamic_body_speed × sub_dt)` — churn stops iterating once residual error is invisible against its own motion; settling stacks keep the tight base threshold and full adaptive budget. Mechanics: `state_max_speed_index = 75`, written in `vbd_predict` as `InterlockedMax(asuint(clamped speed))` (order-free ⇒ deterministic; locked/sleeping bodies return before the write so kinematic tumbler shells don't loosen the threshold; value is post-max_linear_speed-clamp), zeroed per substep by an explicit line in `collision_reset` (slot ≥ 41 so the loop skips it — the explicit zero is REQUIRED for CPU parity, whose max is per-substep fresh). `vbd_convergence_check` applies the scaled threshold to the converged exit (linear only, v1 — angular stays base); the CPU loop applies the identical rule from the identical population (non-locked, non-sleeping, massive bodies, post-predict velocities) so the knob is parity-preserving like `solver_iterations`. Config carries `convergence_speed_scale` between the threshold fields.
- **Auto colour cap (`Physics.gpu_color_cap = -1`).** Enabler first: per-colour population counts now surface — `state_color_population_base_index = 59` (16 slots, header 59 → 76), written by the coloring flatten's tid-0 block, served as `solver_diagnostics.color_populations`. Controller at the synced hint point, 30-frame dwell: seed cap from the natural `max_used_color`; **fold the tail** (last active colour's population < 128 ⇒ cap−1, floor 2); **back off on conflicts** (batch conflicts > `max(8, contacts/256)` ⇒ cap+1, ceiling 16). Resolved value rides `d.gpu_color_cap_resolved` into the existing `color_cap` config field; explicit 0/N settings bypass the controller entirely. First ~30 frames run uncapped (resolved seeds on first dwell expiry). Readback lag (~2 ticks) is fine — populations move slowly; hysteresis is the dwell.
- **Interaction to measure, not assume: conflicts slow per-iteration convergence, so an aggressive cap can trade cheaper iterations for more of them** — acceptance = solve ms AND consumed iterations together (rebuild the slot-56/57 effective-iterations probe for the A/B). Suggested first interactive config on the tumbler: `gpu_color_cap = -1`, `convergence_speed_scale = 0.02`, `max_solver_iterations` back to 40 — the scale should hold churn near the effective floor while stacks get their budget back; watch consumed iterations, `colors`/`conflicts`, solve ms, and stacking softness by eye.

**BUILT + TESTED same session (agent build grant; three build-test rounds):**
- **Bit-identity PROVEN on the final binary**: gate drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` ×2 + heap `0x64457148b09ad048` ×2 + drop CPU `0x14f82f0ca02de8d7` ×2 == every pinned baseline; drop positional 0.000 mm. Header 59→76, the predict speed write, and the population writes are hash-inert as constructed. (One `.gcm "File exists"` build failure en route = the documented live-symidx-holder class; retry succeeded.)
- **Controller needed two fixes found by test**: (1) first cut LIMIT-CYCLED 6↔7 on stress — conflict-pressure and tail-thinness fight when the scene's natural shape sits between caps ⇒ **conflict-driven floor latch** (a cap rejected for conflicts cannot be re-tried until reseed) + hysteresis band (shrink only when conflicts ≤ budget/2); (2) the seed fired at dwell expiry while the scene was still forming (seeded 4, then fought a growing scene: 2334 transition conflicts) ⇒ **seed guarded on `attempted_contacts ≥ 256`** (transition conflicts 771, 3× less). Final behavior on stress: seeds ~natural, settles **cap 6, ~0-1.4 conflicts/tick, stable 500+ ticks**.
- **Dynamic-threshold A/B (stress, 600f/300 measured, max_iterations 40): GPU p50 19.8 → 15.5 ms (−22%); CPU 19.3 → 15.4 ms (−20%)** at `convergence_speed_scale = 0.02` — churn runs near the effective floor with the 40 ceiling still available for settling scenes; both solvers cut the same fraction (the parity-preserving construction, behaviorally confirmed).
- Headless note: the auto-cap alone barely moved headless unfolded p50 (19.85 vs 19.82) — its win is structural (fewer barrier phases), which pays folded/interactive, not in the launch-bound headless regime.
- **Instrument traps for the next agent**: scenario-pinned `warmup_frames` BEATS `--engine-bench-warmup-frames` (parity_stress_gpu pins 300 — a 300-frame run measures ZERO frames; use `--engine-bench-frames` > pin instead); stress hashes are run-to-run nondeterministic (open class) — never read a stress hash delta as a feature signal, use measured p50.

**SCALE 0.1 INTERACTIVE VERDICT + ASYNC DISPATCH CAP SHIPPED (2026-08-17):** at 0.02 the scale was arithmetic-inert at slow-stir speeds (0.02 × 0.4 m/s / 120 = 67 µm < the 100 µm base — the max() resolves to base below ~0.6 m/s); at 0.1 the probe read **~3 checks/substep ≈ 16-17 consumed iterations while stirring** — the iteration side is AT the floor and no longer the gap. The residual vs the hard-15 era decomposed to: per-iteration cost inflated ~35% by a cloud-heavy camera (cloud avg 2.11 ms that run vs 0.83 in the 240-fps runs) + the async gate's batch multiplication (fence 10.4 ms EMA, 330 spikes, sim dilating). **The gate's dispatch quantity is now capped at ONE tick per frame** (`dispatch_steps = latest_dispatch_complete() ? 1 : 0` on the async path): overload dilates the sim smoothly instead of building 2-5-tick batches; excess fixed-step demand drops; the step-debt banking machinery became dead and was DELETED (`gpu_step_debt`, the drain, the cap — do not resurrect; catch-up is intentionally absent from async mode). Async-off paths provably untouched; drop pins re-verified. Remaining known cost on cloud-facing cameras is the raymarch average itself — the graphics list.

**SKY + ENCODER TOGGLES SHIPPED (2026-08-17, user request — the graphics-list items become product knobs):** `Clouds.enabled`, `Atmosphere.enabled`, `Graphics.encode_enabled` — all hot-reloadable, default true (defaults-on gate re-verified at pins; toggles-off run verified pass-free with the solver intact). Semantics: atmosphere-off skips LUT updates + the sky draw AFTER the sun bookkeeping and UBO write, so the sun direction stays live for forward lighting and RT shadows (the LUTs are persistent bindless images — consumers sample last-computed content, no graph-culling cascade; a sun edit while disabled shows only on re-enable); clouds-off skips raymarch + composite; encode-off gates `capture_due` — screenshots unaffected, the replay ring stays empty until re-enabled. Headless p50 unmoved by construction (solve-bound, other queues) — the win is interactive contention. Instrument gotcha found en route: profile dirs are named `Sandbox.<pid>` and Windows pid reuse lets a later run CLOBBER an earlier run's dump — read profiles before launching more runs.

**DILATION LEAKED THE TUMBLERS — the deferred kinematic stage-2 artifact came due (2026-08-17, user-reported: cubes escaping the drums).** Three consumers sat on two clocks: the tumbler driver advanced phase by `fixed_steps_this_frame()` (demanded), `apply_kinematic_targets` moved walls by demanded steps, the gate dispatched ≤1 and dropped the rest — under sustained dilation the drum spins 2-4× the simulated cubes and sweeps through them in one simulated tick ⇒ tunneling. **FIX = one authority: `physics::data::sim_steps_this_frame` (`gse::shared`), computed once in `prepare` (async: `latest_dispatch_complete() ? 1 : 0`; every other path: the clock's steps) and consumed by all three** — `apply_kinematic_targets` advances by it, `update_vbd_gpu` dispatches the STORED value (no re-poll ⇒ prepare and dispatch cannot disagree), and the tumbler system reads it через `shared_view<physics::data>` (it runs before `prepare`, so it consumes last frame's value — a bounded one-tick lag that cannot accumulate, vs the unbounded divergence it replaces). Non-async paths publish `steps` ⇒ provably identical; drop pins re-verified. RULE FOR ANY FUTURE FIXED-STEP CONSUMER (kinematic drivers, spawners, anything advancing world state by tick count): consume `sim_steps_this_frame`, never `fixed_steps_this_frame()` directly — the clock's demand is not the sim's consumption under async.

**ANGULAR TWIN ADDED after the first interactive run exposed the gap (2026-08-17):** the user's slow-stirred tumbler ran the FULL 40 ceiling (21 ms frames, solve 6.2 ms/substep) despite the scale — the converged exit needs BOTH gates, and only the linear threshold was motion-scaled; rotation-dominated slow churn (kinematic drum feeding angular motion, modest linear speed) loosens linear while the fixed 1e-3 rad angular gate never passes ⇒ no exit. Fix: `state_max_angular_speed_index = 76` (header 77), predict writes the clamped angular speed, the angular threshold scales as `max(base, scale × max_ang_speed × sub_dt)`, CPU mirrors identically. **Measured headless (600f, 40 ceiling): checks/substep ~29 → ~16 (−44%), p50 18.8 → 13.7 ms (−27%, was −22% linear-only); drop pins re-verified on the build.** The slow-stir interactive regime is the real test — probe below makes it readable. **TEMPORARY PROBE IN-TREE: the slot-56/57 effective-iterations probe is LIVE in vbd_convergence_check (per the recorded recipe) — the peak line's "stale X of Y" now reads exits-per-batch X / cumulative checks Y, NOT staleness. Revert after the interactive verdict.**

### The parked scale levers, unparked (2026-08-17, thirteenth leg — sweep width measured, coloring split SHIPPED + gate-validated)

Context: the stress scene grew to 12×12×24 drums (~7k bodies / 30-35k contacts, the 7× GPU regime), making both parked occupancy levers live.

**Sweep width (`gpu_sweep_workgroups`), same-block A/B at ~30k contacts, fold on: 32 → 13.80 ms/substep; 64 → 12.57 (−9%, and p95/p99 46/48 → 43/45); 128 → 12.42 but p95/p99 158/170 — barrier starvation churn, the bail machinery contains it but the tails rule it out.** Verdict: 64 is the scale recommendation; default stays 32 (small scenes gain nothing and pay more arrive-traffic); never 128 on this hardware. A future auto-width (from body count, like the colour cap) is the natural follow-up if scene sizes vary widely.

**Coloring build split SHIPPED — the determinism-scarred pass, split bit-exact on the faithful-port rule.** Mechanics: the Jones–Plassmann rounds moved out of the single-1024-thread kernel into full-width round/commit dispatch pairs ping-ponging `coloring_scratch` (the buffer a past session provisioned at 2×max_bodies — layout: [0,max_bodies) live colours as plain uints, upper half next-round scratch); rounds keep the exact two-phase read-all-then-commit semantics (round A computes next colours + counts blocked bodies into `state_color_pending_index = 77` (header 78); commit B copies scratch→live and its body-0 writes the NEXT round's indirect args — `indirect_dispatch_buffer` grew one triple at slot (2+max_colors) — so converged rounds collapse to zero-size dispatches, preserving the early-exit); `collision_reset` initializes the live-colour range to 0xFF per substep; the flatten kernel keeps EVERYTHING from the fallback passes down byte-identical, with `live_color_of`/`store_live_color` re-pointed at the buffer. The colour-cap palette logic is duplicated verbatim in the round kernel (as is `body_priority` — the helper-include route would drag the flatten's ~44KB of groupshared into the round kernel's occupancy; keep the two copies in sync). **ACCEPTANCE: full convention — drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` / heap `0x64457148b09ad048` / pyramid `0x78da245248aa9506` exact; then 3 required-tier VK rounds + 1 DX12 round, 23 checks each, 0 required failures, both backends at the same pins, only the two documented drop/pair identity reports.** The scarred pass survived its careful session.

Measured at ~30k contacts (fresh binary, fold on, width 64): coloring stage 545 µs/substep including the round dispatches (interactive 7k pre-split read ~686); p50 nudged 39.3 → 37.8. The split's payoff is curve-bending — the 1024-thread ceiling is gone, so the 16k-class 6.2 ms/frame cost cannot return. **NEW NEXT-LEVER IDENTIFIED BY THE SAME PROFILE: `vbd_broad_phase` is now the collision front's dominant item at scale — 4.68 ms/substep at 30k contacts (39% of solve, 2× narrow+coloring+adjacency combined).** Scope below.

### Broad phase at density — scope (2026-08-17, not started)

The smell: ~194-350 µs/substep at 4-5k contacts → 4.68 ms at 30k — roughly 7× the contacts for 15-24× the time, super-linear where the pass should be near-linear. Three candidate mechanisms, all structural to the current design (per-body neighbor-cell walk over a 4096-bucket hash grid with `InterlockedCompareExchange` prepend chains, pairs emitted through ONE `InterlockedAdd` counter — the same lock-free-append class the determinism campaign mapped):

1. **Bucket-chain blowup**: `grid_table_size = 4096` buckets for 7k+ bodies concentrated in two dense drums — the table was sized in the hundreds-of-bodies era; hash collisions turn the per-body walk into long chain scans. Prediction if dominant: max chain length scales with body count, cost tracks chains not contacts.
2. **Cell-occupancy quadratic**: a packed pile puts ~8+ bodies per cell; each body tests O(occupancy × neighbor cells) candidates — locally quadratic in density. Prediction: candidates-tested per body explodes while chains stay sane; lever = cell-size heuristic vs measured occupancy.
3. **Hot-counter contention**: 30k+ emissions per substep hammering `collision_pairs[0]` — the sweep-arrive class of L2 serialization. Prediction: cost concentrated in emission, not the walk.

**Instrument FIRST (the standing discipline — one run answers which mechanism):** header-slot probes in the broad phase — per-substep InterlockedMax of bucket-chain length, InterlockedAdd of candidates-tested and pairs-emitted (2-3 new header slots, batch-scoped, surfaced through the existing diagnostics like everything else). Run small vs big drums; the factor that scales super-linearly names the mechanism. The two-pass split-timing throwaway (walk-only vs emit) is the backup if slots are ambiguous.

**Levers, ascending risk, gated on the instrument's verdict:**
- (a) Table size: scale `grid_table_size` with `max_bodies` (or flat 16384). One constant; buffer sizes flow. CAUTION: changes hash→bucket mapping → chain order → pair order → **re-baselines every GPU hash**.
- (b) Cell-size retune against measured occupancy if (2) rules.
- (c) **Two-pass deterministic emission** (count → prefix scan → emit at computed per-body slots): kills the hot counter AND makes pair order body-index-deterministic — which is also one rung of the parked open-nondeterminism ladder (the deterministic-grid experiment proved single-stage fixes just promote the next racy stage to first-differing, so this is justified as PERF FIRST with the ordering rung as a structural bonus, not as a determinism fix). Re-baselines; full protocol.
- (d) Sorted/Morton broad phase — the big rebuild; only if the instrument shows the grid fundamentally mismatched to dense piles.

**Acceptance:** the order-blind `post-broad` pass hash (the existing trace instrument) checks pair-SET equality for any lever; every lever above reorders pairs so the full gate + 3-round + DX12 convention applies (today's coloring session re-proved the protocol is affordable in-session). Perf target: broad ≤ ~1.5 ms/substep at 30k contacts — proportionate to the narrow phase. Vehicle: the big-drum Dev Spawn overrides + same-block A/B; the 40-frame stress repro for any determinism side-reads.

### Making the tick cheaper structurally — scope (2026-08-16, not started)

Target: GPU solve loop = 72% of the tick (12.2 ms/f on stress at 40 iters; interactive it inflates to ~9.3 ms/substep under graphics contention). Constraints from the ledger: effective iterations 93% consumed on hot scenes (the work is honest — no free early-exit); empty-colour launches already bounded (0.3 ms recovered); the update_lambda+joint+convergence micro-fold measured WITHIN NOISE (refuted, do not rebuild blind); folding update_lambda into the colour sweep REJECTED (both-sleeping replay ratchet); any restructure re-baselines every GPU hash → full gate + 3-round protocol.

1. **Persistent-kernel / fewer-launch solve loop (the >1 ms lever).** Today ~19 dispatches × 80 instances/tick ≈ 153 µs of launch+drain per iteration. Fold options in ascending risk: (a) one dispatch per iteration for the jointless colour sweep — colours sequenced inside the kernel via device-scope atomic ticket barriers (cross-workgroup sync: grid must be sized ≤ resident capacity; forward progress formally unguaranteed on VK/DX12, practically fine on NVIDIA at bounded occupancy — needs an occupancy-derived launch bound, a TDR-safe spin with iteration cap, and a fallback path); (b) whole-iteration fold including lambda+convergence (already measured worthless separately — only worth it as part of (a)'s structure); (c) full multi-iteration persistent kernel (highest win, highest risk — the convergence early-exit moves in-kernel where it is actually cheap). Open questions to answer with throwaway instruments BEFORE building: intra-iteration gap profile (extend the per-pass GPU timestamps to per-dispatch granularity on a throwaway build — how much of the 12.2 ms is drain vs SM work; the interactive-vs-headless delta says contention, which a fold does NOT fix); jointed-island path folding (serialized island dispatch stays separate initially — jointless-only prototype on drop/heap first); determinism (per-body accumulation order unchanged by construction if colour order and per-colour traversal are preserved — verify bit-identity on the settling ladder before anything else, else it re-baselines).
2. **Coloring build split (0.5-6.2 ms, scale-dependent).** `collision_build_coloring` single-workgroup disease: 6.2 ms/f at 16k, one JP round per dispatch is the faithful split — but this is the most determinism-scarred pass in the codebase; its own careful session with the full gate + 3-round sweep (per the adjacency-split precedent, which was bit-exact).
3. **Occupancy at scale**: ~40 workgroups per colour dispatch on 170 SMs at 16k — colour-count reduction (fewer, fatter colours) or multi-colour co-residency inside the persistent kernel; measure before designing.
4. **Already-available dials, no build**: `Physics.solver_iterations`/`max_iterations` (parity-preserving shared quality knob — dropping the ceiling shrinks the tick on both solvers together) and the async gate above (stops the tick cost from multiplying into batches).

Recommended order: instrument (1)'s gap profile on stress + 16k pyramid, then jointless persistent-kernel prototype behind a setting on drop/heap with bit-identity as the acceptance gate, then decide (b)/(c) from measured drain share. Treat as its own campaign at the coloring-session level of care.

### Async GPU physics attempt (2026-08-16) — FULLY REVERTED; re-scope recorded

**Status: every code change from this leg is reverted** (the `Physics.async_gpu_readback` setting, the solver's async-reads branch, `frame::queue_fence_signaled`, the dispatch throttle + step debt, the readiness gating in `update_vbd_gpu`). The user A/B-tested builds before and after the final round and the after-build broke the interactive session (camera frozen at origin, input dead — mechanism not yet root-caused; the revert removes the whole surface). Post-revert verified: drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` and domino `0x77b4d98581705e2b` exact, stress canary at full evolution.

**What the attempt taught, kept for the re-scope:**
- **The async design already exists in the engine** — the origin-teleport fix split `retired_snapshot_slot()` (CPU reads, ~2 frames of lag) from `render_snapshot_slot()` (GPU reads). The system was non-blocking by design; the blocking came later, when the mound observation-layer campaign added the **age-1 fence wait** in `wait_for_slot`. The correct re-scope is a few lines in that existing machinery — consume age-2+ slots only (which the frame ring's own begin-wait already guarantees fenced, no new fence API) and never wait in the game path — NOT a new throttle/debt/poll apparatus. Retirement knowledge as a queryable graph API remains the agreed long-term home.
- **Bootstrap trap**: any dispatch throttle keyed on the *retired* slot's readability deadlocks after batch 1 (post-flip the retired slot is the never-used one). Key on the in-flight slot if a throttle is ever needed — but the frame ring already paces dispatch; a physics-side throttle is likely unnecessary.
- **Headless + uncapped async frames TDRs the GPU** (~2,500 fps of full submissions hangs the hardware context ~2 s in; dx12 DEVICE_HUNG 0x887A0006 + Vulkan device-lost simultaneously; `frame_count` freezes after). Any future async mode must never run in an unpaced headless loop.
- **Instrument traps paid**: the stress canary is blind while the diagnostics query sits behind any readiness gate (batch-1 contacts masquerade as an advancing sim), and a frozen `frame_count` in an age-based check means post-TDR, not a logic bug.

Superseded detail from the attempt follows for context only:

- **Non-blocking reads**: `gpu_solver::slot_ready` gains an async branch — age-0 slots are not-ready, age-1 slots are *polled* (`frame::queue_fence_signaled(compute, ring)`, new: `wait_for_fence` with `timeout_ns = 0` — no RHI change needed), age-2+ are ready as before (guaranteed fenced by the frame ring's own begin-frame wait). The poll checks the COMPUTE fence only — the solver's load-bearing leg. The frame-age safety from the mound campaign is preserved exactly: an unfenced slot is never read, only reported not-ready. **Follow-up, agreed with the user: per-slot retirement knowledge belongs in the render graph** (it already tracks slot completion for timestamp readback and command recycling); the solver-side fence poll is the contained interim.
- **The frozen-objects bug, root-caused empirically after two wrong theories**: the first throttle keyed on the *retired* slot's readability — a **bootstrap deadlock**: after batch 1 dispatches, the ring flips so the retired slot is the never-yet-used one, which cannot become readable until a second dispatch, which the throttle forbade. Frozen after one tick in every mode; a headless "sanity pass" hid it because the canary's 252 contacts were batch one's, not an advancing sim (and the diagnostics query sits inside the readiness gate, so the canary is BLIND while reads are unready — instrument trap number N+1). Fix: the throttle keys on the **in-flight batch's completion** (`latest_dispatch_complete()` = the render-slot's fence state), not on read readability. A per-frame state log (steps/ready/throttled/debt/dispatched/retired) then showed gens 1→4 dispatching with reads flowing — the working cadence — before the run died to the separate headless artifact below. The interim all-queues fence poll (graphics fence never signaled at poll time) was a real flaw fixed en route, but it was NOT the freeze.
- **System flow** (`update_vbd_gpu`): one readiness check gates the readback/ECS-consume block, the airborne update (resizes hoisted so spawns stay covered), and diagnostics; when not ready everything keeps last tick's state.
- **Dispatch throttle + step debt**: when the previous batch hasn't retired, the tick is skipped and its steps bank into `d.gpu_step_debt` (capped at 4 — excess drops = time dilation, deliberately not hoarded so the spiral cannot ratchet); the next dispatched batch drains the debt. First-dispatch guarded (`dispatch_generation() > 0`) so startup can't deadlock. The GPU snapshot + body map still publish every frame, so the renderer's existing velocity back-interpolation keeps drawing motion from the latest complete state.
- At 60 fps with a healthy GPU the age-1 fence poll passes every frame → full-rate sim with 1-2 ticks of game-logic latency (the same lag the CPU mirror always had). Under overload the sim runs at the GPU's real rate and the frame never quadruples — this replaces the `max_fixed_steps = 4` death spiral for the GPU path.

Verified: **sync default is provably unchanged** (readiness short-circuits true, throttle can't engage, `dispatch_steps == steps`; gate 9 checks 0 failures, drop/heap/domino hashes exact). Async smoke: stress 300 f completes, canary clean, sync validation 0 hazards, frame p50 0.445 ms headless (the stall is gone).

**Known artifacts, scoped:** (1) **headless async runs TDR the GPU**: the uncapped loop (~2,500 fps of full frame submissions including clouds and the always-on capture encoder) hangs the hardware context ~2 s in — Windows resets it, the encoder's D3D12 interop reports DEVICE_HUNG 0x887A0006 and the Vulkan device goes lost in the same millisecond, begin_frame stops, and `frame_count` freezes (which then pins the async throttle — a run frozen this way shows "count" stuck in any age-based check). This is a real GPU reset, not log spam; async + headless bench is an invalid configuration until the frame loop is paced — the WINDOWED game is display-paced and never enters the regime. (2) Kinematic targets advance in `prepare` by *unthrottled* steps, so under sustained overload kinematic walls (tumblers) run slightly ahead of sim time — bounded by the debt cap; stage-2 fix is advancing kinematics by dispatched steps for the GPU path. (3) Headless async benches are not hash-comparable to sync (frames outpace sim by design); the gate keeps the default.

### Colour-launch bound shipped; dispatch fold refuted; a clock-state trap (2026-08-16, second interactive leg)

Context: the user's newest interactive profile showed a **catch-up spiral** — the fixed-step sim can't fit its ~18.6 ms GPU tick in a 60 Hz budget, so it runs ~1.75 physics ticks per render frame (52.6 ms avg frame, 40 fps dipping to 20). Every ms off the tick pays double there.

**Shipped: the lagged live-colour launch bound** (the "GPU-driven or lagged-bound" approach the empties analysis called for). The physics system already reads `diagnostics()` every tick at an already-synced point; it now pushes `max_used_color` into the solver (`set_color_launch_hint`), and the record path bounds all four colour loops at `min(max_colors, max(hint + 4, 8))`, full-width on reseed or no-hint. The floor-8 + margin-4 guarantee: every determinism-gated scene's colour count (heap 3, mound 4, pile 4, pyramid ~6, stress 7) sits under the floor/margin, so their dispatch streams stay deterministic and no live colour can be skipped; overlap (13) lands at full 16. **Measured same-block: drop 2.10 → 1.37 ms (−35% — on tiny scenes the empty launches were a third of the frame), stress −0.2-0.3 ms; every hash exact (gate 23 checks, 0 failures, both backends), sync validation 0 hazards.** First attempt read the retired readback buffer in the record path and cost +1.2 ms — **`host_read()` of a possibly-in-flight slot pays the frame-age-gated fence wait; never read readback buffers while recording** — the hint-push architecture is the fix.

**Refuted, do not rebuild blind: folding update_lambda + update_joint_lambda + convergence_check into one dispatch.** Built three variants (full fusion; wave-aggregated completion counter with the last-arriving wave running the checker; joints unfused with checker-only fold). All measured **within noise of the unfused baseline on stress in same-block A/B** — the two saved 1-thread-dispatch drains per iteration are worth less than measurement noise post-barrier-batching, and the full fusion also risks contact-path register pressure from the joint branch. Reverted to the original three-dispatch structure. The completion-counter recipe (wave `WaveActiveCountBits` aggregation, atomic add, last wave's first lane fences + checks, self-managing counter because the converged flag is uniform per dispatch) is recorded here for when a fold is actually needed.

**Measurement trap, paid for again: GPU clock state differs across session blocks.** Stress read 16.3 ms in the morning block and 17.4-17.8 in the evening block **for every config including bit-identical ones**, while drop simultaneously read 2.5× FASTER (5.35 → 2.10 ms without the bound) — small launch-latency-bound scenes and big SM-bound scenes shift in OPPOSITE directions with clock/boost state. A cross-block p50 comparison is meaningless; only same-block A/B counts (the CPU drop canary does not detect this — it is CPU-only). The morning "16.3" and evening "17.5" are the same code.

### Binding-repeat fast path in the recorder (2026-08-16 — the interactive-frame leg)

Driven by the user's interactive sandbox stress sitting at 60 fps: the live profile (`%LOCALAPPDATA%/GSE/profile/Sandbox.<pid>/profile.txt`) showed the frame was main-thread-bound — `vbd_gpu::readback` stalls 11.4 ms/frame waiting on GPU solve latency (solve reads 9.3 ms/substep interactively vs 6.1 headless — graphics contention), and `render_graph::record_passes` burned 9.9 ms/frame of CPU recording ~1,600 solver dispatches, each `push_bindings` re-noting all 31 table buffers through hazard tracking (map find + two linear scans each).

**Fix: `recording_context` now detects repeated identical binding pushes** (same pack type + stages + bytes, memcmp against a 512-byte cache) and replaces the 31 per-resource notes with ONE pending union memory barrier — semantically equal-or-stronger (DX12 already collapses to a global UAV barrier; Vulkan gets one `VkMemoryBarrier2` instead of ~31 buffer barriers per boundary). Invalidation is mutation-driven: `note_touched` only kills the cache when a note actually changes tracked state, and the first invalidating note after a store arms a one-shot **companion** (its stages/access fold into every subsequent repeat barrier, sticky for the streak) — that is what lets the cache survive the `dispatch_indirect` indirect-args note, whose hazard otherwise re-fired every dispatch: after one armed hit the companion note becomes a read-after-read no-op and the streak self-sustains. Measured hit rate on stress: **89%** (61,820 hits / 7,875 misses over 60 f). A cache miss only ever means the slow path — never wrongness.

Verified: sync validation **0 hazards** on stress with layers enabled (the recorded arbiter); gate 23 checks 0 required failures both backends; **every absolute hash unchanged** — drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0`, heap `0x64457148b09ad048`, mound `0x53485b21fba22696`, domino `0x77b4d98581705e2b` (cascade 12/12), pyramid `0x78da245248aa9506` (positional 1.721 mm), 16k pyramid `0x2f08a2e73fb4ec65`.
