Numbers: `record_passes` p50 **5,618 → ~2,440 µs** (−3.2 ms of main-thread time per frame); **the small-scene GPU frame floor halved** — heap 5.5 → 2.45 ms, domino 5.37 → 3.08, mound 5.7 → 3.28, pyramid300f 5.44 → 2.85 (driver-side barrier processing was the floor); stress headless p50 unchanged ~16.3 (solve-execution-bound), 16k unchanged ~38-39 (same). Fewer barrier commands also reduces the parked flip class's exposure (flip probability scales with barrier-command pressure, measured 08-15).

**What still caps the interactive stress frame, in order:** (1) GPU solve execution ~18.6 ms/tick under graphics contention — the persistent-kernel fold and/or the parity-preserving `Physics.solver_iterations`/`max_iterations` quality dial are the levers; (2) the readback stall is solve-latency, it shrinks 1:1 with (1); (3) product knobs visible in the user's profile: `video::encode` 2.0 ms/frame (capture encoder live every frame), `cloud_raymarch` 2.1 ms, both graphics-side and user-decidable.

### GPU scaling curve + adjacency split (2026-08-15 night — the performance leg)

**Scaling curve** (pyramid, 150-frame active window, VK, quiet machine, canary 0.073-0.076):

| base | bodies | contacts | CPU p50 active | GPU p50 (before → after split) | GPU vs CPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | 210 | 2.4k | ~7.7 (p95; sleeps mid-window) | 5.31 → ~5.4 (full-300f run: 6.3 → 5.44) | floor-bound |
| 60 | 1,830 | 21k | ~66 (p95) | 19.6 | ~3.4× |
| 120 | 7,260 | 86k | 240 | 34.1 | 7× |
| 180 | 16,290 | 194k | 541 | 48.9 → **37.9** | **14×** |

Two regimes: **small scenes are floor-bound** — the GPU has a ~5.3 ms/frame floor regardless of work (drop with 2 bodies reads the same; it is host record/submit/sync, and `render_graph::record_passes` alone is ~5.6 ms CPU-side on stress), so the CPU solver with island sleeping (0.065 ms asleep) owns that regime. **At scale the GPU wins big and sub-linearly** — 77× the bodies for 9× the time — because occupancy fills in.

**Adjacency split shipped (bit-exact, both backends).** At 16k, `collision_build_adjacency` (single 1024-thread workgroup, seven barrier-phased stages) cost 11.8 ms/frame (24%). Split: the per-body insertion sorts + per-joint warm-start/penalty/c0 prep moved verbatim to a new full-width dispatch (`collision_sort_adjacency.slang`, one thread per body/joint, `vbd_compute` entry, second dispatch in the same `vbd_build_adjacency_stage` pass — intra-pass auto-barrier covers the RAW hazard), and the count-zeroing/copy/Hillis-Steele-scan phases in the remaining single-workgroup dispatch are now bounded by `body_count` instead of `max_bodies` (20480). **Everything moved or bounded is a pure function of unchanged input (scatter arrival order untouched — that is the part that would move hashes), so the output is bit-identical by construction, and measured: drop/heap/mound/domino/pyramid hashes ALL unchanged on VK (gate round, 14 checks 0 failures) and heap/pyramid unchanged on DX12; cascade 12/12; pyramid positional 1.721 mm identical.** Numbers: 16k p50 48.9 → 37.9 ms (−22%); adjacency stage 5,890 → 853 µs/substep (6.9×, sort dispatch included — 11.8 → 1.7 ms/frame); small scenes each gained ~0.5-0.9 ms (heap 6.0→5.5, mound 6.2→5.7, pyramid300f 6.3→5.4, stress 16.5-16.9→16.2).

**Next lever, identified but NOT done — propose before building: `collision_build_coloring` = 6.2 ms/frame at 16k (16%), same single-workgroup disease** (Jones–Plassmann rounds + the deterministic colour-list flatten in one 1024-thread dispatch, once per tick). A faithful split = one dispatch per JP round (synchronous rounds via pass barriers preserve JP semantics exactly) + the flatten kept deterministic-rank; but this is the most determinism-scarred pass in the codebase (the flatten WAS the determinism root cause), so it wants its own careful session with the full gate + a 3-round sweep, not a tail-end edit. After coloring, the remaining structural item at 16k is solve-loop occupancy (~40 workgroups per colour dispatch on 170 SMs); the iteration work itself is honest (93% budget consumed).

### Stabilize joint-colour race fixed (2026-08-15 evening)

The post-stabilize sweep in `GpuSolver.cpp` solved ALL bodies through the global colour lists while the colouring separates only contact neighbours, so two joint-connected bodies could share a colour and race `body_data` within one stabilize dispatch (the joint loop in `solve_body` runs unconditionally and reads joint neighbours live). Fix = the stabilize non-jacobi branch now mirrors the main loop's split exactly: when `joint_count > 0`, jointless bodies stabilize through the jointless colour lists (`jointless_bindings` + `jointless_indirect_dispatch_buffer`, both still valid from the substep-0 colouring — the substep-1 main loop already relies on that) and jointed bodies through the serialized island dispatch (`color_offset = 0xFFFFFFFF`, forward+backward sweep, `live_jac`); `joint_count == 0` keeps the old global colour loop **bit-identically**. This also matches the CPU, whose post-stabilize is a full `solve_iteration(0.0f)` — jointed bodies get main-iteration treatment there too.

Verification (this tree, driver 596.99): drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0`, heap `0x64457148b09ad048`, mound `0x53485b21fba22696` all reproduce exactly (×2 each — jointless dispatch stream untouched, so these MUST NOT move; they didn't). Jointed battery on stress 600f (CPU control + VK ×2 + DX12): cascade 12/12 toppled on all three GPU runs, per-domino final heights within 2.6 cm of CPU, all 18 jointed dynamic bodies (chains, springs, hinge, slider, fixed, distance) bounded with zero NaN and settled jointed groups matching CPU to the millimetre (Fixed Hanging Box y 4.000 both, Hinge Door 2.000 both, Slider Platform 4.150 both); canary healthy (contacts ~4230-4400, dropped/fallback/conflicts 0). Jointed stabilize semantics legitimately changed (island path vs racy colour path): stress joint lambda peak moved 1998.6 → 2005.6 N.

**A/B on the wave-speed observation:** GPU wave arrivals run progressively ahead of the CPU along the domino chain (Domino 12 arrives ~frame 290-297 on GPU vs 363 on CPU; heights still match). A rebuild with the old stabilize path reproduced the same early arrivals (299 for Domino 12, inside the fixed build's spread) ⇒ **the CPU↔GPU cascade propagation-speed gap is pre-existing and unrelated to stabilize** — likely the same impact-resolution family as the restitution-order divergence. Recorded here so nobody attributes it to this fix; the ParityDomino gate check (next steps 2) is the instrument that will track it.

The analyzer is `scripts/stress_battery.py` (cascade + jointed-tether battery + global invariants from state dumps; dominos = fnv1a64("Domino 1..12"), toppled = final centre y < 0.7, arrival = first frame y < 0.7; state dump header is 12 bytes — magic, version, one more u32 — then 64-byte records; an 8-byte header assumption misparses everything).

## Rules the measurements depend on

**Two regimes, two metrics.** Scenes that come to rest (`ParityDrop`, `ParityPair`, `ParityStack`, the pyramid, joints-only) are deterministic and admit exact comparison — hold them to an exact number and treat any regression as a real defect. This is the regime that has found every bug. Scenes that stay in motion (`ParityCluster`, `ParityHeap`, `ParityPile`, `ParityOverlap`) are chaotic; demanding low per-body drift there is chasing Lyapunov amplification. Score those on invariants that do not care which box ended up where — settling frame, mean and max height, bodies shed past spawn reach, final max speed. `--scan-states-summary` reports exactly those. The empirical case: **the piles have found no bugs**, and cost the most time.

**Warmup must be 0.** The default 120 discards the entire onset.

**Never conclude from one run, and check the machine is quiet.** This box produces interference episodes that look exactly like regressions. A 2-body scene reading 2.3x slower is the tell — no solver change can do that. Three clean runs of `parity_drop_cpu` land at 0.108–0.120 ms (re-baselined 2026-08-12 after the merge removal; the pre-chunk-bucket band was 0.142–0.162; a truly cold machine — Editor and everything else closed — reads 0.097–0.100, and the 16k p50 drops ~20 ms below the Editor-open "quiet" readings, so cross-day comparisons must match cold-vs-quiet state); a single run read 0.342 and produced a confident, entirely wrong diagnosis of an engine-wide regression. A contaminated run also shows a p95 several times its siblings' — the 08-12 pile round had clean runs at p95 3.5–3.9 ms next to contaminated ones at 14–20 with identical hashes. Check for background compiles, then take a median of 3. The Editor idles at ~1.3 cores and a running game once invalidated an entire timing round; the drop canary catches both.

**Check the binary contains the change.** One "rebuild" predated the edits and a full round re-tested the previous build — the tells were a per-tag profile identical to the old one and a bit-identical state dump. Compare the exe mtime against the newest source mtime before attributing any result, in either direction, to a change.

**Measure the property, not the mechanism you suspect.** Hypotheses about the colouring fallback were instrumented and refuted four times. What settled it was measuring the invariant directly. The same rule cracked the indirect-dispatch failure: the question was not "why does the narrow phase reject this pair" but "does the narrow phase run at all".

**A clamp value is not a measurement.** Two solvers agreeing at 15.08 m/s when `max_linear_speed` is 15 is saturation, not parity.

**Runs must be long enough to reach the mechanism.** The tumbler turns at 0.6 rad/s, so a revolution takes ~10 s. Use 2400 frames when the tumbler matters.

**Instrumentation cost is not free.** A duplicate-contact check in `collision_build_coloring` cost 35 ms/frame — a 760x regression on that stage — and invalidated every GPU timing taken while it was in.

**Profile tags are not all self time.** `update_wall:::` spans are wall-clock and absorb shared stalls: on a 2-body scene they read ~180 µs each across unrelated systems and sum to ~1600 µs against a 342 µs frame. A system that is disabled still shows a full-looking number. Read `vbd::*` tags for solver work, and remember the parallel ones are summed across workers.

**`physics_stress` p50 is pacing-bound**, not compute-bound — it reported exactly 10.022 ms across three different builds. Do not use it to judge solver cost.

**A headless DX12 run can lose the device silently and still hand you valid-looking numbers.** A GPU hang device-removes (`DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG`), but headless mode has no present to notice and the removal logging is gated on validation — the run completes all its frames on a world frozen at the last retired snapshot, prints a plausible world-state hash, and two such runs agree bit-exactly because a frozen world is trivially deterministic. Measured 2026-08-13: six runs, three scenarios, "identical finals," all vacuous. The tells, in the order they were found: the traced generation stops advancing with no missing-body warning; the state dump shows a one-tick rollback then bit-frozen positions mid-fall; `vbd gpu peak: contacts` pins at 0 on a scene that must have contacts. Before trusting any DX12 hash — especially a surprising agreement — check one of those, and when in doubt rerun with `Graphics.validation_layers_enabled=true`, which surfaces the removal and a DRED walk that names the hung pass.

## Phase 2: GPU parity (deferred)

Do not start this until Phase 1 is done. Recorded now because the knowledge is expensive and perishable.

### Baseline 2026-08-12: the defect is Vulkan-specific — DX12 is already at parity

Fresh both-backend baseline on the current tree (RTX 5090, driver 596.75, exe newer than every source, machine quiet, backends selected with `--engine-setting Graphics.backend=vulkan|dx12` and verified from the device-init log lines). Two instruments, both point the same way.

Shadow-step residuals against the CPU, steps ≥ 20, two runs per backend:

| scene | backend | median dv | max dv | max dp | steps differing run-to-run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cluster (27 bodies) | Vulkan | 0.083 | 12.19 | 0.252 | **102 of 229** |
| cluster (27 bodies) | DX12 | 0.053 | 1.55 | 0.011 | **0 of 229** |
| pile (216 bodies) | Vulkan | 1.19 | 19.44 | 0.153 | **120 of 129** |
| pile (216 bodies) | DX12 | 0.191 | 3.28 | 0.027 | **3 of 129** |

The medians are not comparable to the 08-07 table below because the harness now carries a benign residual floor, measured precisely by the detail dumps: an awake-but-resting body reads dv = 0.1633 = 9.81/60 — exactly one frame of gravity — **byte-identically on both backends** (e.g. body `158162`: VK 0.1633, DX 0.1633), so it is a backend-independent harness artifact, not a defect. Fully sleeping bodies read exactly 0 on both backends — `build_body_states` uploads `sleep_counter` and the GPU honors it — so fully settled steps read 0. The discriminators are max dv and the run-to-run differing count, and they reproduce the 08-07 structure exactly: Vulkan returns a different answer for the same input on most steps; DX12 is deterministic to the digit on cluster and near it on pile.

Pyramid base 20 (210 blocks, 300 frames), trajectory metrics from the state dumps:

| path | apex@60 | apex@150 | apex@end | scattered | final max speed | world-state hash ×2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CPU | 0.01 | 0.01 | 0.01 | 0% | 0.00 | — |
| GPU Vulkan | **−8.95** | −4.93 | 18.25 | **91%** | 13.63 | two different hashes |
| GPU DX12 | 0.02 | 0.02 | 0.02 | **0%** | 0.00 | **bit-identical** |

**DX12 stands the pyramid at CPU-grade quality and its 210-body, 300-frame trajectory is bit-identical run-to-run** (`0xc84c1c2cb7aaa228` twice). The explosion, and the trajectory nondeterminism it rides on, is Vulkan-only. That overturns two standing claims: the 08-11 "GPU explodes on stacks" table below was measuring the Vulkan backend, not the GPU solver, and the "determinism boundary between 9 and 217 bodies" was likewise Vulkan-only — DX12 is bit-stable at 210. One more scale marker: peak speed inside the Vulkan blowup reads 126 m/s in the dump, 8× the 15 m/s linear clamp, so the corrupted state is not an unconverged solve — it is state that never went through the clamp path intact.

What this reframes: the solver — the same Slang shaders, the same recorded dispatch stream — is already at near-parity where the backend executes it correctly. Phase 2 is not a solver-math hunt; it is a Vulkan backend defect hunt (barrier translation on the Vulkan path of the auto-barrier derivation, availability/visibility semantics, descriptor-index mapping, or a SPIR-V-side miscompile), with DX12 as the working reference implementation of the very same shaders.

### Localization 2026-08-12: the divergence rides contact-chain towers, and the barrier seam is asymmetric by backend

Ran the entry instrument the same day: `ShadowStep.detail_step` at steps 90 and 115 (chosen from the aggregate column — run-to-run-differing on Vulkan, mid-run, DX12 quiet at the same steps), Vulkan ×3 per step, DX12 ×1 as control, plus a CPU state dump for owner→grid mapping. The cluster settles into **nine independent 3-box towers** (0.75 m spacing on 0.5 m boxes kills lateral contacts), which makes the contact graph legible per body.

What the per-body dumps show:

- **The divergence is contact-graph-structured, whole-tower at a time, amplifying up the chain.** At step 90 the entire x=1,z=0 tower is wrong: dv 4.55 (bottom) → 5.85 (middle) → 9.30 (top), dp 0.038 → 0.076 → 0.109, out of a pre-state that is nearly at rest (pre speed ≤ 0.027, cpu dv ≤ 0.004). DX12 reads 0.002–0.012 on the same bodies at the same step. Bodies in untouched towers read exactly 0.0 on both backends.
- **Two failure modes coexist.** The step-90 tower answer is *identically wrong across all three runs* — 9.2956 to the fourth decimal ×3 — a deterministic-per-step wrong answer (the earlier baseline run had read 9.6855 at the same step, so it is not absolutely stable either, just usually-repeatable). Step 115 shows the racy mode: tower x=0,z=2 reads 5.9954 in run 2 and 0.026–0.028 in runs 1 and 3. Both modes live on towers; both are Vulkan-only.
- **The affected sets are not memory-layout-structured.** The step-90 tower's solver body indices are 9/12/15 — non-contiguous, with clean bodies interleaved — which rules out stale-buffer-region classes (a stale cache line or page would hit contiguous indices) and implicates the stages that walk the contact graph: the color sweeps and the dual update.
- A hit tower's bottom box can stay clean while the boxes above it blow (step 90, tower x=0,z=2: gy=1,2 flip, gy=0 reads 0.0000) — the error rides box-box contacts and does not need the floor contact.

Where the code says to look next. The whole iteration loop — `num_colors` × `dispatch_indirect` color sweeps plus `vbd_update_lambda`, for all iterations — records back-to-back inside **one pass** (`vbd_solve_iterations_stage`, `GpuSolver.cpp:1390-1436`), so ordering between those dispatches rests entirely on the intra-pass derivation in `RecordingContext.cpp`: `push_bindings` → `note_touched` per bound resource (`.writable = true` → `shader_storage_write`, and the solver sets `.writable` throughout) → `emit_intra_pass_barrier` emits a barrier on any write-side hazard. That machinery is backend-shared and looks sound. The asymmetry is in translation:

- **DX12** (`Dx12/Device.cpp:436`): a memory barrier with an unordered hazard becomes a **global null-UAV barrier** (`pResource = nullptr`) — the strongest sync D3D12 has, covering *every* UAV access on the queue regardless of which resource the hazard named.
- **Vulkan** (`Vulkan/Commands.cppm:423`, `build_vk_dependency_info`): the same hazard becomes a **precise per-buffer** `VkBufferMemoryBarrier2` naming exactly one buffer with translated stage/access masks.

So DX12 is structurally over-synchronized and Vulkan is exactly-as-synchronized-as-the-tracking-is-complete. Any single buffer that the solve shaders mutate but the tracker never sees — reached through a bindless `DescriptorHandle` index carried in push constants rather than through `binding_args`, or aliased under a second descriptor authority — is unsynchronized on Vulkan while DX12's global barrier incidentally covers it. That failure shape produces exactly what the towers show: dispatches ordered (execution dependency intact via the *other* buffers' barriers) but one buffer's writes intermittently invisible to the next sweep, wrong contact forces compounding along chains, usually-stable-sometimes-flipping by cache timing.

### Step-1 audit result, 2026-08-12: no untracked buffer — but readwrite bindings never request READ visibility

The write-set audit ran the same day and the "untracked buffer" hypothesis is dead, for a structural reason worth keeping: the whole VBD family shares **one binding table** (`shader_binding_types`, 31 buffers, `GpuSolver.cpp:228`), every entry binds all of it, and the C++ declarations *generate* the Slang binding preamble — `ssbo_readonly` emits `StructuredBuffer` (writes do not compile), `ssbo_readwrite` emits `RWStructuredBuffer`. A scan of every VBD shader found zero writes to readonly-declared buffers, zero manual `.Handle` casts, zero descriptor arrays. The per-shader write sets, for the record: `vbd_solve_color` writes `body_data`/`collision_state`/`solve_deltas`; `vbd_update_lambda` writes `contact_data`/`collision_state`; `vbd_update_joint_lambda` writes `collision_state`/`joint_data`; `collision_narrow_phase` writes `contact_data`/`collision_state`; `vbd_update_sticking` writes `contact_data`. Tracking is therefore *conservative* — every readwrite buffer is noted on every dispatch, and barriers are emitted between every consecutive dispatch pair in the solve pass.

**What the audit found instead is a spec-level defect in the noted access itself.** `register_one_bindless` (`RecordingContext.cppm:331`) noted a readwrite binding as `shader_storage_write` **only** — the read side was never recorded. Every derived barrier therefore carried `dstAccessMask = STORAGE_WRITE` with no `STORAGE_READ`. Per the Vulkan memory model, visibility is granted per access type: that mask makes prior writes visible to subsequent *writes* but never to subsequent *reads* — and the iteration loop's central dependencies are exactly RAW (sweep N writes `body_data`, sweep N+1 reads neighbor positions; `update_lambda` writes `contact_data`, the next sweep reads lambda/penalty). The flags flow into cross-pass barriers too (`finalize_pass` buckets `m_touched` into `pass.writes` with the same access), so the under-specification was engine-wide on Vulkan. DX12 is provably immune: its translation collapses these to access-agnostic UAV barriers (`unordered_hazard_between` already fires on write-side access), which is exactly why the working backend is the one with the *cruder* barrier model. The mask translation itself is faithful (`Types.cppm` maps `shader_storage_read/write` → `VK_ACCESS_2_SHADER_STORAGE_{READ,WRITE}_BIT` correctly), and `resource_for_slot` registration covers every solver buffer (`write_storage_buffer` registers each slot).

**Fixed 2026-08-12** (one line, `RecordingContext.cppm:331`): readwrite bindings now note `{ shader_storage_read, shader_storage_write }`, which fixes src-side availability and dst-side RAW visibility everywhere the flags flow, intra-pass and cross-pass, buffers and storage images alike. Barrier count is unchanged — only masks widen. DX12 emission is bit-unaffected.

**Verified 2026-08-12, same day — the fix is a complete cure on Vulkan.** Post-fix matrix (exe 14:28 > fix 14:24, machine quiet; note the CPU reference re-baselined between rounds because lever-4's sticking change landed in the same build, so flip counts are within-build metrics, which is what they measure anyway):

| scene | backend | differing steps ≥ 20 (pre-fix) | max dv vs CPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| cluster ×3 pairs | Vulkan | **0 of 229** (was 102) | 1.552787066 |
| pile | Vulkan | **0 of 129** (was 120) | 3.418156862 |
| cluster | DX12 | 0 of 229 (was 0) | 1.552787066 |
| pile | DX12 | 2 of 129 (was 3) | 3.418156862 |

The backends now agree with *each other* to the digit — identical max-dv against the CPU on both scenes — so what remains in those numbers is the genuine CPU-vs-GPU algorithmic gap, finally measurable without backend noise. Pyramid base 20 on Vulkan: apex drop 0.02, **0% scattered** (was 91%), overall max speed 0.82 (was 126), final 0.00, and the trajectory hash is **bit-identical ×2** (`0x143b6e44d7e6a42f`) — Vulkan went from exploding-and-nondeterministic to standing-and-bit-stable in one mask change. DX12 quality is identical (apex 0.02, 0%, 0.82).

One observation, attributed but not yet closed: DX12's pyramid hash now wobbles run-to-run (2 distinct hashes in 3 runs; yesterday's `0xc84c1c2cb7aaa228` ×2 was on the pre-lever-4 binary, so the absolute hash legitimately moved). The step-level controls prove this is not the barrier fix — DX12's cluster (0/229) and pile (2/129 vs 3/129) counts are unchanged pre/post — it is the pre-existing DX12-side per-step residue at high contact counts, now the *only* GPU nondeterminism left, and notably absent on post-fix Vulkan (0/129 on pile). Prime suspect is the known narrow-phase atomic contact-slot class from the determinism saga, or the new sticking path; it is a much smaller hunt than the one just closed.

Where this leaves Phase 2: the backend execution defect is fixed engine-wide (the mask widening applies to every readwrite storage binding, all passes, both barrier tiers — rendering paths inherit it too). The remaining parity work splits clean: (a) the per-step GPU-vs-CPU residual, now identical on both backends (cluster max 1.55, pile max 3.42); (b) DX12's 2-of-129 pile residue if trajectory-level determinism on DX12 ever matters.

### Chasing the per-body gap, 2026-08-12: the shadow residual decomposes, and most of it is the harness

The per-body chase ran the same day and the "algorithmic gap" is not one thing. Decomposition, from the post-fix logs plus dump forensics:

**1. The settle-phase floor is the seed-tick transient, re-triggered every shadow tick — not a solver defect.** The dominant persistent residual (`cpu dv 0.000000, gpu dv 0.16334` on stacked bodies, 23–27 bodies over 1e-3 on every settled step, one body on 65 of 230 steps) has the exact arithmetic of two substeps of unresisted free fall: dv = g·dt = 0.16334 and dp = 3·g·sdt² = 2.04 mm. The affected body's support contact does not exist in the GPU pipeline on that tick (grounding can't save it — `grounded_bits` derive from the same contacts, `collision_build_adjacency.slang:46`). The decisive fork: scanning the **real-path** post-fix pyramid dumps for that signature finds it **only at the seed frames** — DX12 frame 2 shows 186 of 210 bodies at exactly dy −2.04 mm / vy −0.163 from rest, Vulkan a 15-body echo at frame 5, and **zero events on either backend for the remaining ~295 frames** (CPU: zero anywhere). So the mechanism is: the first tick after a (re)seed integrates gravity before the body has collision results, free-falling it ~2 mm for one tick. On the real path that fires once per spawn/reseed and self-heals next tick. The shadow harness **reseeds every tick by construction**, so it re-triggers the transient every step — which is why the class looked persistent. This confirms and sharpens the old harness note ("forcing a reseed every tick wipes the warm-start cache… must not be read as a GPU defect"): reseed artifacts include a full seed-tick contact gap, not just cold lambda.

**2. What is genuinely real on the real path: the seed-tick transient itself.** Small (2 mm, one tick) but it is a true CPU/GPU behavioral difference — the CPU never does it (its continuous `accel_weight = clamp(−accel_y/g, 0, 1)`, `Solver.cppm:481`, self-measures to 0 for supported bodies regardless of contact availability, and its collision results precede integration). Fix direction, one of: run the collision pipeline once at seed before the first predict; or seed `accel_weight`/`grounded_bits` from the uploaded body state (the CPU-side airborne flag is already in hand at upload). Fixing it kills the spawn free-fall on the real path AND unclogs the shadow instrument — every shadow tick stops paying the transient, so what remains in shadow residuals becomes the true algorithmic gap.

**3. The impact-window class is the remaining candidate for real algorithmic divergence — re-measure after fixing 2.** Cluster steps 17–36 carry the large residuals (max 1.5528 at s22: cpu dv 0.2324 vs gpu dv 1.3204 — the GPU responds ~1.1 m/s harder on the center body; at s17–18 the CPU responds harder). Suspects: restitution structure (separate `vbd_apply_restitution` pass vs the CPU's in-solve handling) and warm-start-dependent impact response. But impact steps are also where the per-tick reseed (cold lambda, zeroed warm starts) bites hardest, so these numbers are contaminated too — do not chase them until the seed-tick fix lands and the shadow floor collapses; what survives then is real.

The instrument-level rule this adds: **shadow-step absolute residuals vs CPU are upper bounds contaminated by per-tick reseed; only backend-vs-backend shadow comparisons cancel the artifact. Before chasing any shadow residual class as a solver defect, check the real path for the same signature** (the dump dip-scan above is the template). Real-path post-fix quality for the record: pyramid VK apex 0.02 / CPU 0.01, both 0% scattered, overall max speed 0.82 vs 0.65 — the trajectory-level gap is already small.

### The reseed free-fall mechanism hunt, 2026-08-12 — eliminations and the pinned facts

The seed-tick fix needs the mechanism, and the mechanism has resisted every static theory; what survives is a set of measured facts that any explanation must satisfy. The shadow aggregate line now carries `contacts N ws M` (narrow-phase count + warm-start hits from the diagnostics readback), and the detail line carries per-body `aw / sleep / grounded` (readback of what predict actually computed). The discriminator matrix so far, all on `parity_cluster_cpu`, Vulkan, post-barrier-fix:

| experiment | result |
|---|---|
| contacts on free-fall ticks | **flat 108** (= full settled manifold set; the contacts exist) |
| ws per tick | 0 on all 249 (reseed cold-lambda confirmed, as designed) |
| substeps=1 | **class vanishes entirely** (0 free-fall steps, 0 settled residuals; was 13/30) |
| Jacobi (substeps=2) | **persists, worse** (33 steps) — not the color lists; solve-path-independent |
| iterations 15 → 40 | **dv pinned at 0.1633** — the contact contributes zero force to the affected body at any solve effort |
| detail dp at s122 | 2.08 mm ≈ 3·g·sdt² — **both substeps free-fall**, yet substeps=1 is clean |
| affected bodies | tower tops + their mid partners, one tower at a time; often `cpu dv 0.000000` (asleep or exact-equilibrium on CPU) |

Statically eliminated (each checked against source): upload completeness (positions/predicted/AABBs/half-extents all filled), `apply_body_inputs` gating and placement (full copy at top of tick, before collision), per-substep pipeline order (reset → grid → broad → prepare-indirect → narrow → prepare-contact → adjacency → coloring[sub 0] → predict → solve → derive), indirect-args freshness (prepare between broad and narrow; narrow re-guards on live count), the cross-env filter (inert single-env), the adjacency scan/fill/sort logic (single-workgroup Hillis-Steele + bounds all correct), grounding directionality (upper body grounded by its support contact's normal test). The contradiction the mechanism must resolve: substep 0 of a substeps=2 tick free-falls a body that the identical machinery at substeps=1 handles correctly, deterministically per tick, on both backends.

Side finding, retracted on inspection: both solvers use gravity 9.8 (`Solver.cppm:433` `gravity_mag = 9.8f`, `vbd_predict.slang:122`) — the measured free-fall dv 0.16333 = 2·9.8/120 is consistent on both sides and there is no gravity-constant divergence.

### The free-fall class, root-caused and killed 2026-08-12 — and the instrument made warm

The full-state probe (per-body aw/sleep/grounded readback, then a 512-contact + adjacency readback) closed the hunt. The failing body's adjacency was perfect and its contacts healthy; the tell was the contact table: awake towers ramping penalty from **1 N/m** while their λ erected from zero every tick. Root cause chain, every link measured: the shadow's per-tick reseed wipes warm starts (by design, for spawn renumbering) → contacts restart cold at `penalty_min = 1` because the mass-scaled floor is **gated on `active_normal`** (cached load or `C0 < −1e-4 m` — `collision_narrow_phase.slang` seeding, and the CPU has the *identical* gate at `Solver.cppm:362`) and resting stacks sit shallower than the −0.1 mm threshold → the cold ramp cannot erect the resting load within early iterations → the stack sags about one gravity-tick → α=1 post-stabilize semantics adopt the sagged pose as the new rest → BDF1 reads the sag as the 0.16333 velocity signature. Not a solver divergence at all — both solvers share the gate and the ramp — but a **cold-restart fragility of resting stacks** that the CPU never exposes (its contact cache persists) and the GPU real path exposes only at spawns. Confirmed eliminations along the way, for the record: adjacency `[unroll]` scan (byte-identical under `[loop]`), frozen-jacobian content (live-jac run kept the class), scorched-earth per-tick buffer clears (byte-identical — the class is generated within-tick from clean state).

**Fix: the shadow now preserves warm starts across its reseeds** (`gpu_solver::set_preserve_warm_starts`, shadow-only — its world never renumbers, so feature-keyed warm data stays valid; real spawns keep the wipe). Result on cluster: free-fall steps **69 → 0**, settled steps read 1e-5-to-0, median dv 0.053 → 0.030, and warm-carry is fully deterministic (0 of 229 steps differ across three runs). Pile: median 1.19 → 0.139, max 19.44 → 2.28. The scorched-earth reseed clears stay in as seed hygiene.

### Congruence scorecard, 2026-08-12 — full ladder, both backends

Real-path trajectory congruence after the day's fixes (barrier visibility, shadow warm preservation, serial restitution, accel-weight feedback port). 300 frames, Vulkan; DX12 spot-checked. Deterministic scenes scored on final per-body position deltas, chaotic scenes on invariants:

| scene | bodies | final per-body Δp mean/max | settle C/G | mean height C/G | shed C/G |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| drop | 2 | **0.00 / 0.00 mm** | 57/60 | exact | 0/0 |
| pair | 3 | **0.00 / 0.00 mm** | 49/57 | exact | 0/0 |
| stack | 9 | 2.7 / 4.4 mm | 61/71 | 1.716/1.719 | 0/0 |
| cluster | 28 | 12.4 / 34.6 mm | 85/97 | 0.705/0.705 | 0/0 |
| heap | 65 | (chaotic) | 289/**never** | 0.977/0.977 | 0/0 |
| mound | 126 | (chaotic) | 298/**never** | 1.235/1.236 | 0/0 |
| pile | 217 | (chaotic) | never/never | 1.443/1.479 | 5/0 |
| overlap | 217 | (chaotic) | never/never | 0.859/0.738 | 79/99 |

Pyramid base 20: apex drop 0.021, 0% scattered, final max speed 0.001, bit-stable hashes — on **both** backends. Cross-backend: cluster final positions VK-vs-DX12 within 6.1 mm. The shadow instrument (warm) reads settled steps at 1e-5-to-0 and is run-to-run deterministic.

**Accel-weight feedback ported to the GPU, 2026-08-12.** The GPU's `accel_weight` was binary grounded-derived (recomputed in predict from `grounded_bits`); the CPU's is continuous self-measuring feedback (`clamp(−accel_y/g, 0, 1)` from the body's own velocity delta). The GPU now mirrors the CPU dataflow: `vbd_derive_velocities` computes the feedback from the pre-overwrite velocity and carries it in `body_state.accel_weight`; predict consumes the carried value, forces aw = 1 for unconstrained bodies (the CPU's catch-all equivalent — without it a spawned free body with aw = 0 would hover, since unconstrained bodies are not in the color lists and predict is their only mover); the sleeping and locked branches pin aw = 0 like the CPU; uploads seed aw = 0 (CPU spawn parity — previously the uploaded binary value was dead state, predict overwrote it); the shadow preserves the GPU-evolved aw across its per-tick reseeds via a `preserve_accel_weight` push-constant flag tied to warm preservation. Verified no regressions: drop/pair still exact, stack/cluster unchanged, pyramid perfect on both backends. It did not cure the heap jitter (hypothesis refuted — the binary duty-cycle was not that oscillator), but it removes a real structural divergence and its own oscillation class.

**Remaining gaps, named and bounded (superseded 2026-08-12 evening — gap 1 is FIXED, see "The warm-start lineage split" below; the list as it stood):**
1. **Heap/mound never fully settle on GPU**: 3–5 bodies sustain a tangential rocking limit cycle (±0.18 m/s horizontal swings over ~20-frame periods, 17–33 mm x/z travel, positions otherwise correct; CPU damps the same bodies to exact zero and sleeps). Sticking formula and carriers are identical on both sides; the suspect is tangential-floor/anchor-recycling state flicker across ticks, and the next probe is a per-tick contact λ/sticking trace for one wanderer on the real path (the readback instrument exists; it needs a logging consumer on the real-path loop, or a shadow variant that doesn't resync).
2. **Impact-window per-step deltas** (max ~0.9 cluster / ~1.4 pile against the CPU at first impact): different Gauss–Seidel color partitions legitimately answer differently during violent transients; trajectory-level effect is the 4–35 mm final spread above. Only chase if trajectory-level invariants ever drift.
3. **DX12 2-of-129 pile-class per-step nondeterminism residue** (pre-existing; Vulkan post-fix reads 0) — reattributed and broadened below: the same atomic-order class produces run-to-run *trajectory* nondeterminism on the Vulkan real path at mound scale.

### The real-path contact trace, and the warm-start lineage split — found and fixed 2026-08-12 (evening)

The probe gap 1 prescribed was built as its own system: `gse::physics::contact_trace` (`Physics/VBD/ContactTrace.cppm/.cpp`, registered by the existing namespace walk, zero production-path branches). `--engine-setting ContactTrace.body=<owner>` logs, once per retired dispatch on the real GPU path, one body line (pos/vel/avel/aw/sleep/grounded/total contacts) plus one line per contact touching that body — λ, penalty rows, floor, c0, normal, approach speed, sticking flag, and **both local anchors**, which is the field that cracked the case. It consumes the readbacks the solver already performs every dispatch, so it is behaviorally inert — verified by identical world-state hashes with and without the trace. Caveats: the contact dump caps at 512 entries (the trace warns once on overflow); readback lags the sim by the usual two frames; owner ids come from a state dump of a prior run of the same deterministic scenario.

What it showed on the heap (65 bodies; the "3–5 wanderers" are actually **three 3-box towers**, amplitude growing with height, the same tower shape every localization in this campaign has produced): the sticking formula inputs were healthy, but every sticking contact's anchors **alternated between two exact bit patterns with period 2 ticks**, and warm penalties (≈2.8e6) survived one-tick feature gaps that should have cold-restarted them to ~hundreds. Bit-exact period-2 state is not physics — it is the double buffer. **Root cause: `warm_start_buffer` lives in `per_frame_data`, the per-substep `contact_data → warm_start` copy is same-slot, and the slot flips every dispatch — so the warm-start chain was split into two disjoint lineages, each tick warm-starting λ, penalties, and the recycled sticky anchors from t−2 instead of t−1.** A held anchor therefore always gripped a two-tick-old pose in alternating phase — a rocking pump. The CPU cache is a single store updated every substep (always t−1). On settled scenes both lineages converge bitwise, which is why drop/pair/stack/pyramid never exposed it; on a marginal tower the two-lineage tug sustains the oscillation forever, and the resulting pose wobble also drives the observed manifold feature-identity churn (10–13 feature keys per pair where a stable box-box manifold holds ~4), whose warm-start misses inject further cold-restart kicks — at one traced event the whole feature set flipped for two ticks and every contact's λn collapsed to ≈0 N for one tick before slamming back to thousands, re-accelerating a body that had reached sleep counter 4.

**The fix is one buffer copy** at the top of the recorded dispatch (`vbd_clear_state_buffers_stage`): `other.contact_buffer → f.warm_start_buffer`, gated on `!m_apply_all_body_inputs || m_preserve_warm_starts`. Semantics: normal ticks warm-start from the previous tick's final contacts (single lineage, CPU cadence); real reseeds skip the copy so `commit_upload`'s zeroed-sentinel write keeps hiding old-numbering warm data for exactly one cold tick (unchanged spawn semantics); the shadow (reseed + preserve) now gets the t−1 chain, so **shadow residual baselines re-baseline**. Two invariants to keep: the cross-slot copy is load-bearing (removing it reinstates the two-lineage split with no visible error anywhere else), and the reseed gate is load-bearing for the sentinel's CPU write — under this scheme `f.warm_start_buffer`'s last GPU write stays two dispatches back, which is what makes the `host_write` fence-safe; an end-of-tick cross-copy placement instead would race it.

**Verified 2026-08-12 evening (Editor open — trajectory instruments only, load-immune per the standing protocol):**

| check | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| heap VK | never settles, 10 late movers (3 towers) | **settles f344**, 0 movers f500+, hash ×2 bit-stable |
| heap DX12 | never settles | **settles**, 0 movers f500+ |
| mound VK | never settles, 3–5 movers | 120/126 frozen; one bistable corner tower (below) |
| drop/pair vs CPU | 0.00 mm final Δp | **world-state hash IDENTICAL to CPU** (`0x81020d38da418ad7`, `0x84d9df458567a744`) |
| stack final Δp | 2.7/4.4 mm | **1.1/1.9 mm** |
| cluster final Δp | 12.4/34.6 mm | 13.9/50.1 mm (single-run chaotic spread) |
| pyramid b20 VK | apex 0.021, 0%, overall max 0.82 | apex **0.016**, 0%, overall max **0.65 = CPU's number**, hash ×2 |
| pile invariants | mean height 1.479, shed 0 | mean height 1.489, shed 0 |

CPU side untouched: `parity_drop_cpu` still hashes `0x81020d38da418ad7`, the recorded baseline.

### Mound residual, reattributed: run-to-run nondeterminism, amplified by a bistable tower

The one mound tower that still moves is not a rocking defect — it is a **coin flip**. Six mound runs produced six distinct world-state hashes; the traced 600-frame run froze the tower (sleep counter pinned at 60, anchors bit-frozen for 299 straight ticks) while the 900-frame run rocked it continuously. Heap (65 bodies) and pyramid (210) are trajectory-bit-stable ×2 on the same build, so the boundary is not body count alone. First divergence between two mound runs: **frame 21 — inside the impact window — at ULP scale, on the corner-tower bodies**. That is the known atomic contact-emission-order class (narrow-phase `InterlockedAdd` slot assignment varies run to run → the serial restitution pass and per-body accumulation sum in different orders → ULP deltas → chaos), the same class as DX12's 2-of-129 pile residue. Coloring is exonerated again (mound peak: 503 contacts, 0 fallback, 0 conflicts, 0 dup). The corner tower is marginally damped, so it amplifies the ULP seed into settle-or-rock bistability; the CPU damps its one (canonical-order) trajectory robustly.

**Next step for full parity, both backends: deterministic contact ordering.** Sort contacts into canonical `(body_a, body_b, feature_key)` order between the narrow phase and the adjacency build (the key is unique — dup 0 — and everything downstream rebuilds from `contact_data` each substep, so a sort pass leaves adjacency/coloring/restitution automatically consistent; `warm_start_buffer` is dead scratch between its narrow-phase read and the end-of-substep copy if a gather target is needed), or replace the atomic slot grabs with two-pass count/scan/emit compaction in broad and narrow phase. Either kills the run-to-run class on both backends, makes the GPU's serial restitution order CPU-canonical (removing the last named sequencing divergence), and should be scored against the ladder plus a mound ×3 hash-stability round. This is a multi-shader pipeline change — scope it as its own step.

### Deterministic contact ordering — implemented and verified 2026-08-12 (late evening); Vulkan is now fully run-to-run deterministic

Recon first shrank the change: the adjacency build **already content-sorts every body's sublist** (insertion sort keyed on other-body/anchor/feature at `collision_build_adjacency.slang:149-184` — slot-order-immune), so per-body solve accumulation and the coloring were already deterministic. Only two consumers were ci-order-sensitive: the **serial restitution pass** (walks atomic emission order — matching the measured f21 impact-window divergence onset exactly) and **warm-lookup stale-tail hits** (the sentinel scan runs past the live count into run-to-run-varying stale entries). One new pass fixes both.

`collision_sort_contacts.slang`, dispatched once per substep between the narrow phase and `prepare_contact_indirect`: a single-workgroup (`adjacency_workgroup_size` = 1024 threads) in-place bitonic sort of `contact_data[0, n)` with `AllMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync` between phases — the established adjacency-shader idiom. **The key is `(body_a, body_b, original-slot-index)`, with the original index stamped into the `replayed` field** (GPU-dead, CPU-only semantics, zero ABI change). The tiebreak is the load-bearing design choice: a pair's contacts sit at consecutive slots in manifold-candidate order pre-sort, so this key groups pairs canonically while preserving each pair's internal candidate order — the *result* is run-to-run identical even though the tiebreak values differ per run, single-pair scenes are bit-unchanged (drop/pair CPU-hash identity survives), and bitonic's instability is irrelevant because keys are unique. Padding to the pow2 network size writes sentinel keys (`body_a = body_b = 0xFFFFFFFF`) into `[n, m)`, and a final lane caps `[m]` (or `[n]` for n ≤ 1) — since the warm-start scan breaks on `body_a == body_b`, **the stale tail beyond the live count is now unreachable**, killing the resurrection class as a side effect (n exactly a power of two — the heap sits at exactly 256 — is why the explicit cap write exists). Cost shape: phases = log²(m), all traffic scales with the *live* count (uniform loops off a runtime `n`); at today's scales (≤ 2400 contacts, m ≤ 4096, 78 phases) it is noise, and if a scene ever runs at the 262144 cap the single-workgroup sort is the thing to replace with a multi-dispatch radix sort.

**Verified same evening.** Primary acceptance: mound 900 ×4 runs — **one hash** (`0x795148e57c776313`, was six distinct hashes in six runs), and the now-unique trajectory **settles at f277, earlier than the CPU's 298** — the bistable corner tower damps when it is not being ULP-kicked. Heap ×2 bit-stable, settles f344. Pyramid base 20 Vulkan: **bit-identical to the pre-sort build** (`0xd9ad17ee8c004aa9` ×2, apex 0.016, 0% scattered, overall max 0.65) — the sort is a no-op where order already didn't matter, which is the cleanest possible regression evidence. Drop and pair keep their **CPU-hash identity** on Vulkan. Stack 1.1/1.9 mm held exactly; cluster 14.4/51.4 mm (in family); heap/mound re-baseline by design (restitution order changed). **Every Vulkan ladder scene tested is now run-to-run deterministic.**

**DX12 after the sort: functional but still wobbling — its residue is now proven to be a separate, DX12-specific class.** The sort works there (pyramid apex 0.016 / 0% / 0.65 identical to Vulkan, heap settles f500+, drop hash-identical to CPU), but pyramid still reads 2 distinct hashes in 3 runs and mound 2-in-2. With contact order canonical on both backends, atomic emission order is **excluded** as the DX12 cause — the pre-existing 2-of-129-class hunt is now cleanly scoped to DX12-side execution variance (suspects: DXIL codegen/scheduling, the stubbed `fill_buffer` leaving DX12 without the clear passes Vulkan gets, or driver-level reordering). Also for the record: DX12's pair run is self-deterministic (`0xf3ee97057ad6b2b7` ×2) but differs from the CPU hash that Vulkan matches bit-exactly — a stable DXIL-vs-SPIR-V rounding difference (FMA contraction class), not noise.

Scorecard after the two 2026-08-12-evening fixes (lineage + ordering), Vulkan: drop/pair CPU-hash-identical; stack 1.1/1.9 mm; cluster ~14/51 mm; heap settles f344; mound settles f277; pyramid apex 0.016, 0% scattered, overall max 0.65 = CPU; everything bit-stable run-to-run. Remaining, in order: (a) DX12-specific run-to-run residue (above), (b) impact-window per-step deltas (unchanged status — chase only if trajectories drift). The isotropic-inertia divergence that stood third here landed 2026-08-12; this whole scorecard predates it and re-baselines.

### The sort's performance cliff, and the compact-then-serialize replacement — 2026-08-13

**The single-workgroup bitonic did not survive contact with a real world.** The user's live profile (game scene, 4,720 contacts, joints active, video encode on) read 125 ms/frame with `vbd_sort_contacts_stage` as the **top GPU pass at 6.16 ms/frame** (3.98 ms per dispatch — m = 8192 ⇒ 91 phases of global-memory compare-exchange plus a device-scope barrier each, all on one SM), with the pre-existing single-lane restitution walk costing another 0.95 ms/frame (660 µs per call over 4,720 contacts). The whole-array sort is deleted.

**The replacement keeps the determinism result at a fraction of the cost, per this plan's own standing prescription ("compact-then-serialize if profiles complain").** Recon that made it cheap: the restitution gate is entirely on **frozen per-contact fields** (`restitution`, `lambda.x`, `approach_speed` — all captured before the pass), so a parallel compaction reproduces the serial loop's qualifying set exactly; only the impulse math reads live velocities and stays serial. The rewritten `vbd_apply_restitution.slang` (one dispatch, `adjacency_workgroup_size` threads): all lanes gate-scan the contacts and append qualifying `(key, ci)` pairs to shared memory (capacity 2048; beyond it the shader falls back to the exact old serial-over-all loop — the one regime where order variance returns, flagged here rather than hidden); a shared-memory bitonic sorts them; lane 0 applies the two-sided impulses in sorted order. **Key = `((body_a << 16 | body_b) << 8) | candidate_index`** — `max_bodies` is 20480 so a pair packs in 32 bits, and the narrow phase now stamps the manifold candidate index into the GPU-dead `replayed` field at emission, which reproduces the deleted full sort's serial sequence exactly (pairs canonical, intra-pair in candidate order). The warm-scan boundary sentinel moved into `vbd_prepare_contact_indirect` (single-thread pass right after the narrow phase writes `contact_data[n].body_a = body_b = 0xFFFFFFFF`). Sort cost now scales with *qualifying* contacts (zero once nothing bounces — the pass is a ~no-op on settled scenes) instead of all contacts.

**Verified 2026-08-13:** drop and pair keep their CPU-hash identity **exactly** (the pure-restitution scenes — the rewrite's arithmetic and sequencing are right); mound final state `0x795148e57c776313` ×3 and settles f277 (both matching the sorted build); heap final `0x9c0b3af4808b1280` on 6 of 7 runs and settles f344.

**The third pole, and the true quadratic: the narrow phase's warm-start lookup — fixed 2026-08-13.** With the planner fixed the frame still degraded to ~83 ms, and the next profile moved the blame to the GPU: `wait_fence` 42.9 ms, with `vbd_narrow_phase_stage` the top GPU pass at 9.3 ms/frame (3.76 ms per substep at 4,720 contacts) — multiplied by the fixed-step catch-up running 5+ substeps per slow frame. The cost was not SAT or clipping: **`warm_start_lookup` linearly scanned the whole warm buffer per contact point — O(n²) per substep, ~11M dependent loads at world scale** — invisible at parity-ladder counts, quadratic at world counts, and the second gain element of the same death spiral. Fix: **adjacency-indexed lookup.** At narrow-phase time the contact_counts/offsets/adjacency tables still describe the *previous substep's* contacts (the adjacency build runs after the narrow phase and fully rebuilds them — `collision_reset`'s per-substep wipe of counts/offsets was redundant and is deleted), and `warm_starts` is exactly that generation's contact array — so the lookup walks the body's ~dozen-entry adjacency list and exact-matches `(body_a, body_b, feature)` instead of scanning thousands: O(degree) per candidate. The tables ride the same cross-slot lineage copy as the warm buffer (counts/offsets/adjacency copied `other → f` in the clear stage, same reseed gate), and real reseeds disable lookups outright via the existing `apply_all_body_inputs && !preserve_accel_weight` push-constant pair — reproducing the sentinel's all-miss semantics without the scan (the slot-0 sentinel write stays as belt-and-braces). Equivalence: the adjacency is complete (`max_contact_adjacency = max_contacts × 2` holds exactly two slots per contact, no drops), so the walk probes the same live set the scan covered and unique keys give the same answer. **Measured: pyramid base-50 GPU p50 34.9 → 6.58 ms (5.3×).** Verified on the merged tree (the isotropic-inertia fix from a parallel session landed between builds, re-baselining every absolute hash): drop and pair keep CPU↔GPU hash identity at their new values, mound is final-state deterministic ×2 and settles f277, heap settles f344. The heap's final-state bistability (the documented impact-window transient class) re-rolled its dice on the re-baselined trajectories and showed 2 finals in 2 runs — same open item, same next instrument (orientation-extended dump), not a lookup regression (equivalence + identity + mound determinism + unchanged settle frames all hold).

**The second pole, found by the same profile: `graph::plan` — fixed 2026-08-13.** With the sort gone the user's frame improved then *degraded* back to ~83 ms, and the profile named the render-graph planner: 38 ms/frame, 45.7% of the frame. Mechanism: the planner ran an O(n²) pass-pair loop where every pair did full **cross-products over the passes' read/write resource lists** (VBD passes bind the 31-buffer table, so ~2,900 pointer compares per physics pair) plus a dense `vector<vector<bool>>` reachability matrix updated at O(n²) per edge — and n is not the quiet-frame ~50: at degraded frame rates the fixed-step clock records 5+ steps ⇒ ~10 substeps ⇒ **~130–170 passes per frame**, where the planner goes superquadratic. That is a feedback loop with gain above one — slow frame ⇒ more recorded substeps ⇒ costlier plan ⇒ slower frame — which is exactly the observed "starts fine, degrades, sticks". Fix, semantics-preserving by construction (same edges in the same order, same warnings, same topological result): per-pass **sorted resource-pointer sets** intersected with a two-pointer merge instead of cross-products, and **bitset reachability rows** (flat `uint64` words) replacing the bool matrix. Verified: pyramid base-50 world-state hash bit-identical pre/post (`0x069d744fbdbd156f` — the planner emits the identical plan), and `graph::plan` p50 **247 → 80 µs** at bench pass counts; at the 130–170-pass degraded state the old cost was ~40 ms and the new is ~1–2 ms, so the feedback loop's gain drops below one and the degradation should not re-arm. Side observation from the same profile, recorded for honesty: `contact_trace` shows ~386 µs of `update_wall` even when disabled — that is blocked-on-schedule wall time (the shared-view edge against physics), not work; per the standing "profile tags are not all self time" rule it is not a cost to chase.

### The transient-variance hunt, 2026-08-13 late — instruments landed, seed cornered, blocked at the finish by an unrelated boot assert

Session goal (user directive): iterate toward `physics_stress` CPU↔GPU parity. Progress this leg, in order:

**DX12 `fill_buffer` implemented** (`Dx12/Commands.cppm` — chunked `CopyBufferRegion` from a lazily-created zeroed DEFAULT-heap source on `dx12::device::fill_source_buffer()`; asserts on nonzero fill values, which no caller uses). **Result: the DX12 pyramid hash wobble is DEAD — ×3 identical** (was 2-distinct-in-3); DX12 drop keeps CPU-hash identity. `record_buffer_fill_u32` (pass-marker breadcrumbs) remains a stub — diagnostics-only, `WriteBufferImmediate` is the right tool when wanted.

**The state dump now carries orientation + angular velocity** (`state_dump_record` v2, 64-byte records; writer fills from transform/motion). Analyzer `diff_dumps64.py` in the session scratchpad does field-level first-divergence.

**The remaining variance class, cornered by measurement (DX12 mound = strongest repro, 2-in-2 final-state flips):** two runs are bit-identical through tick 13 — body state AND all traced contact fields — then at tick 14 (the landing tick: first contacts, restitution fires) **position diverges by exactly one stabilize-correction quantum (0.935 mm) while velocity, angular velocity, orientation, and every contact field stay bit-identical**, and the offset persists unchanged while the bodies are airborne (velocity-invisible propagation: both `position` and `old_position` carry the offset, so BDF1 cancels it; contacts are inactive until relanding, where chaos finally forks the runs). Interpretation: one run applies a landing-tick post-stabilize penetration correction the other doesn't. **Refuted along the way (each instrumented):** the same-pass indirect-args seam (split `prepare_color_indirect` into its own `vbd_prepare_color_indirect_stage` pass — kept, it mirrors the main loop — but the flip survived), and the ExecuteIndirect fetch itself (direct full-width dispatches for the stabilize sweeps — the solve shader self-guards by color count so this is behavior-identical — flip survived; reverted). Note the stabilize's prepare writes byte-idempotent args (same values the coloring pass wrote), so stale-args theories are dead on arrival — the fetch cannot read wrong *values*.

**The decisive instrument is now in the tree and compiled: always-on per-pass GPU state hashes.** `vbd_hash_state.slang` (single workgroup, order-independent XOR over `body_data` position+predicted, thread-combine via shared `InterlockedXor`) dispatched after the solve iterations, derive, restitution, post-stabilize, and finalize (stage slots 1–5; slot 0 spare), per substep, into 12 new `collision_state` header slots (`state_pass_hash_base_index` = 16, header grown 16 → 28 — an ABI constant bump mirrored into the generated shader preamble; `collision_reset` preserves the hash slots at substep > 0 the same way it preserves coloring diagnostics). The readback rides the existing collision-state copy; `contact_trace` prints a per-tick "pass hashes" line. **Next action when the tree boots: two DX12 mound runs with `ContactTrace.body` set, diff the pass-hash lines (script pattern in the session scratchpad `ph_a/ph_b` stanza) — the first differing slot names the racing pass directly.** If it names post-stabilize, the remaining suspects inside that pass are the color-sweep-to-color-sweep intra-pass barrier translation on DX12 and the restitution→stabilize boundary; if it names an earlier pass, the velocity-identity argument was masked by clamping and the hunt re-aims.

**Second leg of the hunt (same evening): the instrument went full-width and the seed bisected to the collision front.** The hash shader now covers 11 stages per substep (post-grid and post-broad included) and hashes full body state (orientation, angular velocity, AABBs), contact content, coloring assignment, the grid chains (set-invariant), and the pair set. Result on DX12 mound ×3 pairs: **the first divergence is the post-grid hash — the grid's (bucket, body) content itself — with the entire previous tick bit-identical.** Grid-build logic is set-deterministic given identical inputs, so the seed is a write-visibility gap at DX12 pass boundaries in the collision front. Probes this leg: the grid insert now publishes CAS-last (fields before publish — kept, canonical lock-free form, Vulkan-neutral: mound hash unchanged ×2), and DX12 memory barriers involving transfer access now also emit a null/null aliasing barrier (the documented copy-vs-UAV requirement July's validation warned about — kept, not the cure). Neither cured the DX12 flips. **The next discriminator, for whoever resumes: hash the same pipeline point twice in back-to-back passes into the two spare header slots (38/39) and compare within one run — disagreement proves the reader-side visibility gap at that exact boundary; agreement with cross-run difference proves the grid build's own output varies.** DX12 mound remains final-state nondeterministic; everything Vulkan and the whole ladder are unaffected (drop CPU-hash identity and mound determinism re-verified after all of tonight's changes).

**Verdict of the first leg (the tree booted again once the camera refactor completed, and the instrument ran):** the first differing per-pass hash on a DX12 mound pair sits at **substep 0, stage 1 — post-solve** — with the two runs' stabilize behaving differently downstream (one corrected, one no-op'd). So the racing pass is in the substep's front half — predict, grid/broad/narrow phase, adjacency, coloring, freeze, or the solve/lambda loop — and the earlier tick-14 stabilize-quantum signature was one downstream expression of it, not the seed. **Next bisection: widen the instrument into the front half** (slot 0 is spare and the header has room — hash after predict, narrow, adjacency, coloring, freeze; hashing contact/color content needs an order-independent combine over content tuples since slot order legitimately varies), rerun the two-run diff, and the seed's pass falls out. The pattern is proven; extend it rather than inventing a new instrument.

**Also blocked mid-leg (now resolved):** the working tree briefly stopped booting mid-verification on a scheduler closed-graph assert in `sandbox::orbit_camera` (reads a state no registered system provides, plus a follow_component ordering cycle) — this comes from a parallel session's in-flight Sandbox camera/spawn refactor (uncommitted edits to RuntimeSpawns/Scenarios/OrbitCamera.cpp/CharacterController), not from solver work; earlier binaries tonight booted fine and the physics changes are all Engine-side. Resume by rebuilding once that refactor completes, then run the pass-hash diff above. After the seed dies: promote the ladder + `physics_stress` (the 1062-body mid-run-spawn reseed test) to the standing CPU↔GPU regression gate — spawn-path parity is the last unexercised seam on the way to stress parity.

**Instrument correction, recorded so no one repeats the mistake: `world-state hash` is a FINAL-STATE hash (`world_state_hash(e.registry())` at bench end), not a trajectory hash.** Every "bit-stable ×N" claim in the two sections above is therefore final-state-grade, not trajectory-grade — including the sorted build's acceptance. Byte-comparing full state dumps exposed what the hash cannot: two heap runs with identical final hashes diverged at **frame 34 (the second-layer impact)** by up to 0.07 m/s across 64 bodies, then reconverged to bit-identical rest (the lattice is a strong attractor); one run in seven escaped to a different final state entirely. So a run-to-run transient variance class at impact frames survives on this build — and quite possibly existed on the full-sort build too, which was never checked at trajectory grade. The current dump cannot localize the seed: `state_dump_record` carries position and linear velocity only, so an ULP-scale angular or warm-start divergence in earlier frames is invisible until it amplifies. **Next probe when this class is worth closing: extend the state dump (or a shadow-style readback) with orientation and angular velocity, byte-diff two runs at the first divergent tick, and walk backward to the first differing field.** Candidate seeds, in suspicion order: a key tie or subtle race in the new restitution compaction (keys are argued unique — pair emission is single-source and candidate indices are per-pair unique — but this is argued, not instrumented), wave/atomic scheduling in a pass not yet exonerated, or driver-level nondeterminism in a wave intrinsic. Final-state congruence with the CPU is unaffected; this is a transient-repeatability question, not a correctness one.

### The read-race discriminator and the standing parity gate — in tree 2026-08-13; discriminator round RUN same day, verdict below

Both prescribed next moves are implemented; the build landed 2026-08-13 morning (exe 09:08 > every edit) and the discriminator round ran immediately after. The gate's first round is still pending.

**Move 1 — the double-hash discriminator at the post-grid boundary.** Two additional `vbd_hash_state` passes now record back-to-back immediately after the existing post-grid hash (stage 10), substep 0 only, writing the two spare header slots through the slot formula's own naming of them: `pc.substep = 2` with `pc.iteration = 0/1` lands `16 + 2·11 + {0,1}` = slots 38/39 (`spare_hash_substep` in `GpuSolver.cpp`; no shader change — the write formula already reaches the spares). `collision_reset`'s substep > 0 preserve widened from `[16..37]` to everything at or above `state_pass_hash_base_index`, so the substep-0 samples survive the substep-1 reset and reach the end-of-tick readback; substep 0 still zeroes them, keeping the samples per-tick fresh. `contact_trace` appends them to the pass-hash line as ` rehash XXXXXXXX XXXXXXXX`. Why the three samples are conclusive: between the three consecutive hash passes the only write anywhere is each pass's own header slot, which the hash function never reads — so all three read bit-identical inputs by construction, and the comparison is pure. Reading the result, per the second leg's prescription: within ONE run, compare s0's final token (stage 10) against the two rehash tokens — any disagreement proves the reader-side visibility gap at exactly the grid→reader pass boundary; all three agreeing within-run while differing across runs proves the grid build's own output varies. The round: two traced DX12 mound runs (`--engine-setting ContactTrace.body=<owner>`), diff the pass-hash lines, read the rehash tokens at the first divergent tick. Vulkan writes the same slots (the instrument stays always-on and backend-shared), giving the control for free.

**Discriminator round run 2026-08-13 — the read-race arm is REFUTED; the grid content genuinely varies.** Protocol: DX12 mound, 900 frames, one seed run with a state dump to harvest an owner (126 distinct owners, deterministic spawn), then two traced runs on that owner. Within each run, the three consecutive post-grid samples (s0's stage-10 token, rehash A, rehash B) agreed bit-exactly on **898 of 898 ticks — 1,796 sampled ticks across both runs with zero disagreement** — so barrier under-delivery at the grid→reader boundary is dead, and the per-pass hashes are proven-trustworthy reads. Cross-run: gens 1–24 bit-identical at every hashed point; **gen 25 diverges at every slot of both substeps at once, including the tick's chronologically first hashed point** (post-grid — slot order in the printed line is not chronological; stage 10 dispatches first). The impact window then flickers: the runs reconverge bit-exactly at gens 27, 31, 33, 35–36, 38–39, and 42 — the lattice attractor damping ULP-scale differences back to identical state, the same reconvergence the heap dump comparison showed — before diverging permanently from gen 43. Three finals in three runs (`0x0aa3ba386247a99b`, `0x0bbbcba8fe838976`, `0x24326ac7ddc61b89`), the strongest mound repro yet. What the shape pins, sharper than the second leg could: with gen 24's *entire* tick identical (post-stabilize and finalize included) and gen 25's post-grid already wrong, the seed executes in the window between gen 24's last hashed point and gen 25's post-grid hash — the end-of-tick copies, `apply_body_inputs`, `collision_reset`, `grid_build` — or rides state the hashes never cover (warm buffer, adjacency tables, `old_position` / `accel_weight` / `sleep_counter`). **Next probe, now that the spare slots have answered their question: repurpose slot 38 as a pre-grid sample (dispatched between `collision_reset` and `grid_build`; reset zeroes the grid heads so the chain walk sees an empty set, leaving bodies + zeroed contacts/pairs + carried colors) and keep slot 39 as the post-grid re-read so the within-run instrument-trust guard stays continuously verified; rename the trace tokens accordingly (`pregrid` / `regrid`).** Reading it: pre-grid differing = the seed is upstream of the collision front (the tick's entry state already differs — suspicion moves to the end-of-tick tail after the last hash and the un-hashed body fields); pre-grid identical with post-grid differing = the grid build races on identical hashed input, and since the grid hash is set-invariant that means the (bucket, body) *set* itself varies out of the CAS insert. **Implemented the same day:** slot 38 now samples between `collision_reset` and `grid_build` (substep 0 only — reset has zeroed the grid heads, contact count, and pair count by then, so the sample is tick-entry body state plus the carried colors and nothing else), slot 39 keeps the post-grid re-read so the within-run trust guard stays continuously verified, and the trace tokens read ` pregrid X regrid Y`. Rebuild, rerun the same two-run traced protocol (the harvested owner stays valid — the spawn is deterministic), and the pregrid token at the first divergent gen names the arm.

**Pre-grid round run 2026-08-13 (exe 09:48 > edits; two traced DX12 mound runs, two more distinct finals `0x61472590df16e72e` / `0xbdce32b8a8a4c996`) — the seed SPLITS into two mechanisms, one per divergence onset.** The trust guard held again (s0 stage-10 vs regrid: 0 violations in 1,796 samples), and this pair produced exactly two onsets (segments: same 1–21, diff 22–26, same 27–41, diff 42–898 — a 15-gen bit-exact reconvergence mid-impact-window):

- **Onset gen 22 — the tick-boundary arm.** `pregrid` already differs (`b287e706` vs `7251a3a5`), so the tick's *entry* body state diverged before the collision front ran, with gen 21 bit-identical at every hashed point. The write happened in the window between gen 21's chronologically last hash (stage 8, substep 1) and gen 22's pre-grid sample. The known writers in that window are the end-of-tick tail after the last hash, the tick-top cross-slot copies in the clear stage, and `apply_body_inputs` — which the earlier eliminations recorded as a **full body copy at the top of every tick**, making it the prime suspect: a cross-slot body copy occasionally reading or landing stale on DX12 is exactly the warm-lineage class one buffer over. Recon task: enumerate every `body_data` writer between the last hash dispatch and the next tick's reset, and check what `apply_body_inputs` copies from (`other` slot?) and under which barrier translation.
- **Onset gen 42 — the narrow-phase arm.** The *entire* substep 0 is bit-identical across runs — all 11 stages plus pregrid plus regrid — and substep 1 is identical through post-grid (s1[10]) and post-broad (s1[9]); the chronologically next hash, post-narrow (s1[1]), diverges, and everything downstream follows. Identical bodies, identical grid set, identical pair set in; different contact **content** out — and the contact hash is slot-order-invariant (XOR over per-contact tuples including the candidate-index stamp), so this is not the old emission-order class. The narrow phase's only un-hashed inputs are the warm path (warm_starts plus the counts/offsets/adjacency tables, which at substep 1 describe substep 0's contacts); the standing order-immunity argument for that path (content-sorted sublists, key-exact matching, unique keys) must therefore have a hole, or the narrow phase has an internal execution race.

**Prescribed next instrument extension (an ABI bump — do it deliberately, both mirror sites in one rebuild): grow the header 40 → 44** and spend the four slots as (a) a tick-entry hash dispatched before `apply_body_inputs` — splits the tick-boundary window in half (entry differing = previous tick's tail; entry same with pregrid differing = `apply_body_inputs`/clear-stage copies); (b) a per-substep pre-narrow warm-input hash — an order-invariant XOR over the live warm entries and adjacency table content, dispatched between the post-broad hash and the narrow phase, ×2 substeps — which convicts or exonerates the warm path for the narrow arm directly; (c) the fourth slot spare. Keep pregrid/regrid exactly as they are: the trust guard has now validated the instrument on 3,592 consecutive samples and the pregrid split is what produced this verdict.

**Recon result and the extension, implemented 2026-08-13 — both arms sit on transfer copies adjacent to un-hashed windows.** The recon collapsed arm 1's suspect list to one candidate: on a normal tick `vbd_apply_body_inputs` writes only locked and reset-pending bodies (from host-written `body_input_data`, CPU-deterministic; the full-copy branch fires only on reseed via `apply_all_body_inputs`), `collision_reset` writes no body fields, and the clear-stage copies never touch body state — so **the only writer of dynamic body state in the tick-boundary window is the end-of-tick cross-slot copy `f.body_buffer → other.body_buffer`** (`GpuSolver.cpp` state-copy stage), whose destination is literally the next tick's entry state. The tail after the last per-substep hash (stage 8) also holds `vbd_update_sticking` (writes contact sticking, un-hashed thereafter) followed by the per-substep same-slot `f.contact_buffer → f.warm_start_buffer` copy inside the same pass — which is exactly the product substep 1's narrow phase warm-reads, so arm 2's un-hashed window is [stage-8 hash → update_sticking → warm copy]. Both arms are the warm-lineage/copy-vs-UAV class shape. The instrument: header 40 → 44 (`state_tick_hash_base_index` = 40, derived in `vbd_limits`, flows to the generated preamble — one authority), every spare-substep slot addressed through the one existing write formula (substep 2, iterations 0–5): 38 pregrid, 39 regrid, **40 tick-entry** (full-state hash dispatched as the tick's new first pass, before `apply_body_inputs` — it reads the previous tick's body-copy destination), **41/42 per-substep pre-narrow warm-input hashes** (`vbd_hash_warm_inputs.slang`, new: an order-invariant walk of exactly the lookup-visible warm set — per body through counts/offsets/adjacency into `warm_starts` content: keys, λ, all three penalty rows, both anchors in full, sticking; slot indices excluded so legitimate emission-order variance cannot alias as content variance), 43 spare. `collision_reset` now never touches slots ≥ 40 at any substep ([16..39] semantics unchanged), and the trace line grows to ` pregrid X regrid Y entry Z warm0 W warm1 V`. Reading the next round at each divergence onset: `entry` DIFF → the body cross-slot copy is convicted; `entry` SAME + `pregrid` DIFF → the apply/clear/reset window injected it (realistically only host-input divergence, near-impossible); substep-0 stages all identical + `warm1` DIFF → the substep-0 [stage-8 → sticking → warm-copy] window is convicted; `warm0` DIFF with the previous tick identical → the tick-top cross-slot warm copy is convicted; every probe SAME with post-narrow DIFF → a narrow-phase-internal execution race is the last option standing.

**The entry/warm round is BLOCKED — the 10:52 build carries a DX12-only physics-freeze regression (2026-08-13, unattributed).** On DX12 the CPU mirror freezes mid-run and never recovers: mound bit-frozen from frame 16 (mid-fall, vy −2), drop from frame 38 (mid-fall, **zero contacts yet** — vy −5.6), pyramid base 20 from frame 3; the traced mound stops printing at gen 14 with no missing-body warning, meaning the retired generation itself stopped advancing. The t5/t6 "identical finals" are therefore **vacuous** — a mid-air frozen world is trivially deterministic; discard them. CPU and Vulkan are healthy on the same binary (mound falls and settles; Vulkan runs all three new probe passes and the new warm-inputs shader — the instrument itself is VK-verified sound). Onset scales with per-frame GPU cost (heavier scene → earlier freeze), not with contacts (drop froze pre-contact) and not body count linearly. The regression window is [09:48 → 10:52): it contains this session's header bump (+3 read-only hash passes per tick, header 44, reset predicate, `vbd_hash_warm_inputs.slang`) AND the parallel shapes session's rung-2 GPU narrow-phase shape-kind change plus spawn/COM work (their memory records those gates as unrun) — note the 09:08 build already ran +2 probe passes (pregrid/regrid) on DX12 for 898 gens clean, so "extra hash passes on DX12" per se are proven fine. **Leg (a) verdict, 2026-08-13: the freeze SURVIVES the stub — this session's instrument is exonerated.** On the stubbed build (exe 11:28) DX12 pyramid base 20 froze from frame 3 again with the same frozen-state hash (`0x8f7a6c38746b7b06`, third occurrence).
