**The freeze is ROOT-CAUSED, same day — a GPU hang inside the shapes-session narrow phase, silently device-removing headless DX12 runs.** The chain, each link measured: the renderer edits are pass-through one-liners (a new COM-displacement parameter on `interpolated_transform`) — exonerated. CPU-solver-on-the-DX12-device produces the identical healthy hash as on Vulkan (`0xe9ed10fb61669014` both) — the fixed-step clock and the device-boot path are exonerated, and the freeze is GPU-solver-path-specific. Re-deriving the onsets against fall times shows **the freeze onset is the first-contact tick, not scene cost** (pyramid rests on the floor → f3; the mound's bottom layer lands ≈ f16; the drop's fall puts first contact ≈ f38 — the earlier "pre-contact" reading was wrong). Every DX12 run's `vbd gpu peak` log shows **contacts pinned at 0 forever** — including the pyramid — while the pass-hash instrument showed live evolving state for the pre-contact ticks, so the readback path works and the narrow phase genuinely emits nothing. A validation-enabled run surfaced why: **`wait_for_fence removed=0x887a0006` (DEVICE_HUNG), and DRED names the hung list: `gse::vbd::vbd_narrow_phase_stage`, compute queue, 4 of 7 ops completed** — begin, event, query resolve, barrier done, the indirect dispatch hung — with 107 lists queued behind it. The shapes rung-2 `collision_narrow_phase.slang` change (488 diff lines, its own gates unrun) hangs the GPU at its first real execution on DXIL; Vulkan executes the same Slang fine, so it is a DXIL-side data-dependent loop. The two data-bound loops the diff adds are the candidates: `for (uint k = 0; k < count; k++)` and `for (int ci = 0; ci < contacts.count; ci++)` — a garbage or uninitialized count on the DXIL path spins the loop unbounded. The fix belongs to the shapes session's change set. The mirror-freeze mechanics for the record: dispatches stop at the hang, so `update_vbd_gpu`'s readback block re-applies the last retired snapshot (two ticks back — hence the observed one-tick rollback) forever, the trace's generation guard silences it, and the bench completes on a frozen world. **The probe stub is reversed (same day, post-conviction): all three dispatches and the `hash_warm_inputs_pipeline` build are back in exactly per the extension paragraph — the instrument is whole again, and the entry/warm conviction round runs as soon as the narrow-phase fix lands and builds.**

**The conviction round ran 2026-08-13 on the first post-fix build (exe 12:05; the shapes hang is dead — 898 gens both runs, live differing finals `0x02771bce47f06a92` / `0xaa8807c62d114a58`) — and the verdict is unanimous: the end-of-tick body cross-slot copy is CONVICTED.** Trust guard 0 of 1,796 again (5,388 lifetime). Segments: same 1–21, diff 22–23, same 24–29, diff 30, same 31–33, diff 34–39, same 40–47, diff 48–898 — four onsets (22, 30, 34, 48), and at **every one of them `entry` already differs** (with pregrid, both warm tokens, and all 22 stage slots following). The reasoning that makes this a conviction rather than a suspicion: the tick-entry hash is the tick's first pass, its only body input is `f.body` = the destination the previous tick's `f.body_buffer → other.body_buffer` transfer wrote, and its non-body content is the same slot's two-tick-old leftovers — and every prior generation was bit-identical across the runs at every hashed point, so *both* the fresh source and any stale prior version of the destination were cross-run identical. A cross-run `entry` difference can therefore only arise from the copy delivering **different bytes in the two runs — varying staleness or tearing** (one run's consumers see the fresh copy result, the other's see a mix or an older version), not from any upstream state difference. The arm-2 narrow-phase signature did not recur (0 of 4 onsets — last round's onset-42 shape stays plausible on other ticks), and both arms reduce to one root class anyway: **DX12 transfer copies at the tick seam delivering stale data to next-tick shader consumers** — the body copy is arm 1, the warm/table copies are arm 2. Fix direction, backend-side: the copy-dest → next-submission shader-read/write transition for transfer-written per-frame buffers (the copy-vs-UAV state class the July validation warned about; the second leg's null/null aliasing-barrier probe was adjacent but evidently insufficient — the missing piece is the cross-submission buffer STATE, not the aliasing scope). Acceptance for the fix: rerun this exact two-run traced protocol — the onsets must vanish (mound final-state deterministic ×2, then ×3), then the full ladder + the gate's first round.

**The mechanism found in code and the fix, implemented 2026-08-13 — the racy recording-order state tracker.** `dx12::device::cmd_pipeline_barrier`'s buffer path resolved `StateBefore` from `m_buffer_states`, a device-global map updated at *recording* time — but passes record on parallel worker command lists, so the map's contents follow worker interleave while the GPU executes in pass order. When a later pass's barrier happened to record first, the tracker already read `UNORDERED_ACCESS` and the seam's first-touch COPY_DEST → UAV transition hit the `before == after → continue` skip — **the transition D3D12 requires to make copy-engine writes visible to shader access was silently never emitted**, on a schedule that varies run to run. That is the whole observed class in one sentence: DX12-only (Vulkan barriers carry access masks, not before-states — immune by construction), tick-seam-shaped (the body and warm copies are the transfer writes whose consumers sit in the next submission), and nondeterministic with worker timing. The fix makes the graph the sole authority: `before` now derives from `bb.src_access` always — the graph's cross-pass derivation (`append_prev_pass_barriers`) walks sorted passes single-threaded with per-queue latest-write/reads-since-write maps, so the barrier's own src access *is* the execution-order truth — and a first-touch barrier's empty src derives to COMMON, which is exactly right for D3D12 buffers (they decay to COMMON at ExecuteCommandLists completion, and the serialized queue guarantees the previous tick's submission completed). `m_buffer_states` is still written on emitted transitions because `cmd_transition_acceleration_structure_inputs` reads it; its read is gone from the barrier path. Honest risk envelope: a write → read-as-state-X → read-as-state-Y chain where the graph attributes the second read's barrier to the original write now emits a deterministic StateBefore mismatch (the old tracker sometimes patched that case and sometimes raced into silent skips); the emitted transition still flushes for the correct target state, so the new behavior strictly dominates, and any such mismatch now surfaces reliably under validation for a graph-level fix rather than hiding in a race.

**Acceptance round, 2026-08-13 (exe 12:29): the fix is a large partial — and the probe census splits the residue into three quantified classes.** Traced DX12 mound pair: 898 gens both (no freeze), trust guard 0 of 1,796, but finals still differ. What moved: **first divergence 22 → 75** — the entire landing/impact window is now bit-identical across runs, which was never true on any prior DX12 build. The 24 onsets decompose: **(1) tick-boundary copy staleness, 22 of 24** — entry-DIFF, the same airtight signature; reduced in onset frequency and, notably, every occurrence this round was transient (the lattice attractor pulled the runs back to bit-identical each time); the barrier is now provably emitted, so the remaining suspicion moves to heap-level coherence (the solver's per-frame buffers live on the CPU-visible GPU_UPLOAD heap — write-combined memory fed by copy-engine writes is exactly where a valid-transition-yet-stale read could survive) or the graph attributing the seam's src wrongly in a coalesced mask. **(2) Pair-set atomic flicker, 1 of 24** (onset 128): post-grid identical, the pair-set-bearing stages differ, substep 1 fully reconverges — a broad-phase emission transient that is hash-visible and physics-invisible. **(3) Coloring-partition divergence, 1 of 24 — and it is the one that goes permanent** (onset 256, diff 256..898): every probe SAME, post-grid/post-broad/post-narrow all SAME — contacts content-identical — and the first chronological difference is **stage 2, post-coloring**: a different color partition computed from identical contacts, i.e. the contact-slot-order class reaching the coloring through the adjacency tables (the content-sorted-sublists argument held for per-body accumulation but evidently not for the partition itself on DX12's wilder slot orders). A different partition is a legitimately different Gauss-Seidel answer, so this class produces real trajectory divergence — it is what actually kills mound determinism now. Next levers, per class: (1) move the solver's GPU-only per-frame buffers off GPU_UPLOAD to DEFAULT heap (seeds/readbacks already ride dedicated staging), or instrument a CPU-side readback compare of the copy destination; (3) read `collision_build_coloring.slang` for the slot-order sensitivity and make the partition slot-order-invariant — the deterministic-contact-ordering precedent (candidate-index keys in `replayed`) is the proven tool shape.

**Class-3 static recon and the adjacency probe — implemented 2026-08-13, awaiting build.** The static walk clears both shaders on paper: the adjacency build's per-sublist insertion sort is fully tie-broken (other-body → anchor x/y/z → feature key, and keys are unique), sublist ranges are disjoint per body, the global fill cannot overflow (2 × contacts ≤ `max_contact_adjacency` by construction), and the coloring consumes only neighbor *sets* through bitmask ORs with pure-hash priorities in Jacobi rounds — every path from contact slot order to the partition looks invariant. Yet stage 2 differed on content-identical contacts, so either the analysis misses an execution race, or the coloring's effective input differs through the one structure no hash covers: the freshly built adjacency tables at the build → coloring boundary — including the stale-tables shape, where a visibility fault hands the coloring a previous generation's table whose `ci` values dereference into *this* generation's slot numbering, yielding wrong neighbors from perfectly identical contacts. The last spare slot now instruments exactly that: **slot 43 = `vbd_hash_adjacency.slang`**, dispatched between the adjacency build and the coloring (substep 0 only, where the coloring runs), hashing precisely the coloring's consumed form — per body, the count plus the set of dereferenced body pairs, with the coloring's own `ci >= num_contacts` guard mirrored, order-invariant so legitimate slot renumbering cannot alias as difference. Trace token: ` adj XXXXXXXX`. Reading the next round at a permanent onset with stage 1 SAME: **`adj` DIFF** → the tables genuinely differ from identical contacts — the adjacency build's execution or its input visibility is convicted; **`adj` SAME with stage 2 DIFF** → the coloring itself races on identical effective input and the static-invariance argument has a hole worth finding at the DXIL level. The spare-substep slot map is now fully assigned — 38 pregrid, 39 regrid, 40 entry, 41 warm0, 42 warm1, 43 adj — so the next probe, if one is needed, takes another header bump.

**Adjacency-probe round run 2026-08-13 (exe 13:26) — a landmark pair, and the input is exonerated.** The two runs are **bit-identical for 258 generations** and diverge exactly once — segments: same 1..258, diff 259..898 — with the entry-DIFF copy class completely absent this pair (0 onsets, was 22; the probe's extra pass boundaries plausibly suppress it further) and the trust guard clean again (0 of 1,796). At the single onset, gen 259: **`adj` SAME (`66b1d2ff` both runs)**, warm0/warm1 SAME, post-grid/post-broad/post-narrow SAME, first chronological difference = stage 2 post-coloring. The mound now diverges through exactly one mechanism, and the coloring computed a different partition from bit-identical instrumented input. Two candidates remain: the coloring pass *reads* the tables inconsistently (a boundary the regrid-style guard never covered — the adj hash pass could see fresh tables while the coloring sees stale), or the coloring's own execution is internally nondeterministic on DXIL despite the on-paper invariance. **The decisive instruments are in tree (header 44 → 48; formula slots, substep 2 iterations 6–8): slot 44 `readj` = a second back-to-back dispatch of the adjacency hash (within-run 43 ≠ 44 proves the table reads are unstable at this boundary — the coloring then plausibly reads a third view); slots 45/46 `colors`/`recolors` = a new `vbd_hash_colors.slang` double-read of the partition itself right after the stage-2 hash (within-run 45 ≠ 46 proves color reads unstable; 45 == 46 within-run while differing cross-run proves the coloring *wrote* different partitions — an internal execution race, and the hole in the invariance argument is real and DXIL-level).** Slot 47 spare. Trace tokens now ` … adj X readj Y colors Z recolors W`.

**Double-read round run 2026-08-13 (exe 13:40) — the coloring shader is CONVICTED of internal execution nondeterminism.** All three within-run guards perfectly clean — regrid, adj-vs-readj, colors-vs-recolors: 0 mismatches in 1,796 samples each — so every read at every instrumented boundary is stable. One onset again (gen 115, permanent), and at it: entry SAME, adj SAME, readj SAME, and **`colors` differs across runs while `recolors` matches `colors` bit-exactly within each run** (`5b8586f0` ×2 in one run, `9a9b3475` ×2 in the other). Per the discriminator table, the exact arm: the coloring **wrote** different partitions from bit-identical instrumented input. The read side is fully exonerated; the on-paper invariance argument (set-semantics ORs, pure-hash priorities, Jacobi double-buffer with correct barriers) has a real hole at the DXIL/driver level — the same Slang is deterministic on SPIR-V. Notable pattern across the three probe rounds: 24 onsets → 1 → 1, first onset 22 → 75 → 259 → 115 — the copy classes are gone or dormant and the coloring is the lone survivor. **The terminal discriminator is in tree (slot 47, `colors2` token): the coloring pass is dispatched a SECOND time in the same tick (substep 0, after the recolors hash) and its result hashed — within one run, `colors ≠ colors2` proves the shader nondeterministic on identical input in a single execution pair, which is simultaneously the conviction and a standalone single-run repro for the DXIL-level hunt.** Behavioral note while this probe is in: the second execution's partition is the one the solve consumes — any proper coloring is valid for the solve so the sim stays sound, but trajectories re-baseline; run-pair comparisons remain within-build as always. If `colors2` proves in-run nondeterminism, the fix conversation changes from "find the race" to "replace the idiom": the coloring's device-buffer ping-pong under `AllMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync` is the only structural difference from the proven-clean adjacency/scan family, and the candidate remedies are a `globallycoherent` qualifier on `coloring_scratch`, splitting the rounds into separate dispatches (pass boundaries provide the coherence intra-pass barriers evidently do not), or a groupshared-resident redesign of the round state.

**PROVEN, 2026-08-13 (exe 13:50): the coloring is nondeterministic on identical input within a single run.** `colors ≠ colors2` on **7 of 898 ticks** in one run and **4 of 898** in the other (first at gens 115 and 330; e.g. exec-1 `0f32409b` — with `recolors` agreeing bit-exactly, so the read is stable — exec-2 `75f2e662`), while colors-vs-recolors stayed 0 of 898 in both. Two consecutive executions of the same shader on the same input in the same run produce different partitions roughly 1% of ticks. This is the conviction beyond argument and a standalone single-run repro (one traced DX12 mound run, grep the `colors`/`colors2` tokens). Side observation: this pair's cross-run first divergence was back at gen 22 with entry/adj DIFF and colors SAME — the intermittent tick-boundary copy class fired early this pair; the two classes coexist and the heap lever remains its designated fix.

**RESOLVED same day — and the mechanism is a one-line loop-control race, not memory coherence; the coherence theory is retracted.** The falsification chain that found it: the LDS neighbor-mirror hybrid made in-run nondeterminism WORSE (colors2 mismatches 7/4 → 399/209 of 898 — fresh neighbor views against stale own-views flip partitions more often than uniformly-stale views), and a full LDS-plus-registers rewrite (zero device traffic in the partition computation) was STILL racy (124/112) — three binaries, three wildly different rates, which is the signature of a control-flow race, not a memory idiom. The race, present in every variant all along: the round loop read `gs_pending` for its early `break` with **no fence between that read and the next iteration's `gs_pending = 0` reset** — thread 0 could loop around and reset while slower threads were still reading, those threads saw 0 and broke out while others continued, leaving the workgroup synchronizing at *different* barriers — undefined behavior that shattered every phase invariant, at a rate set by scheduling and binary layout. Vulkan's scheduling evidently never loses the race; DXIL loses it 1–44% of ticks depending on the compile. It is also why the adjacency/scan family was always clean: no other shader has a loop-carried shared-scalar break. **The fix is one fence** — read `gs_pending` into a local, `AllMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync`, then branch — plus the LDS rewrite is kept (strictly cleaner and faster: byte-packed `gs_live_colors` + per-thread register next-state, the device scratch no longer used by the shader at all). **Acceptance, same day, shader-only (no build needed — runtime Slang): colors-vs-colors2 0 of 898 in BOTH runs, all guards clean, and identical live final hashes ×2 (`0xe74af0b069037d73`) over full 898-generation runs.** The coloring class is closed. The same pair also produced two campaign firsts for the residual class: run 22 logged the first-ever within-run regrid guard violation (1 of 898 — the copy/visibility class caught in the act by the instrument), and the pair's 177 transiently-divergent gens (first at 23, all reconverging into the same attractor) are now attributable solely to the intermittent tick-boundary copy class — the heap lever's target, the last class standing between DX12 and trajectory-grade mound determinism.

**The heap lever, implemented 2026-08-13 — the copy-seam buffers move off the CPU-visible heap (needs a build).** `buffer_desc` grew a `device_local` field: on DX12 it places the buffer on the DEFAULT heap (`create_default_buffer`, COMMON initial state — consistent with the src-derived barrier model and per-ECL buffer decay) and leaves it unmapped; on Vulkan it allocates with `eDeviceLocal` required (which on this machine may still land ReBAR memory — harmless, Vulkan needed no change; the inner `create_buffer` overload now takes the `buffer_desc` instead of threaded `data`/`readback` parameters). Flagged: the six copy-seam buffers — `body`, `contact`, `warm_start`, `contact_counts`, `contact_offsets`, `contact_adjacency` (× both slots). Five had zero host access; `warm_start`'s two host touches are replaced exactly: the create-time `host_zero` is covered by reseed semantics (lookups are pc-disabled on real-reseed ticks and later ticks read only cross-copied content), and the `commit_upload` sentinel `host_write` becomes a `fill_buffer(f.warm_start_buffer, 0, sizeof(contact_constraint))` in the clear stage under the identical `apply_all && !preserve` condition — zeros are the sentinel, and being GPU-timeline-ordered it also retires the old host-write fence-safety caveat. Why this can close the class: the intermittent staleness survives structurally-correct barriers, which is the signature of write-combined CPU-visible memory fed by copy-engine writes; DEFAULT-heap VRAM removes that hazard at the memory level. Acceptance: build, then the traced mound pair — the entry-shape onsets and the regrid guard violation must vanish; if the pair comes back bit-identical at trajectory grade, run mound ×3, a Vulkan spot-check (expected byte-neutral), and then the gate's first `--backend both` round.

**Heap-lever acceptance round, 2026-08-13 (exe 15:01) — a real step, not the close: final-state determinism ×4, trajectory-grade transience persists, and the instrument picked up a contamination caveat.** Four DX12 mound runs (two traced, two dumped) all produced the same final hash (`0xfc949f1df92836db` ×4) — the first four-run final-state agreement DX12 mound has ever shown, and all within-run guards stayed clean (regrid/readj/recolors/colors2 all 0 of 898 in both traced runs; the pre-heap regrid violation did not recur). But the dump pair is not byte-identical: 5,649 of 113,400 records differ, first at frame 22 — the transient impact-window divergence survives the heap move and reconverges into the shared attractor. Two conclusions and one caveat: (1) the write-combined-heap theory is at most partial — the surviving transient with structurally-correct barriers on device-local memory points at an execution-timing race at one of the boundaries no double-read guard covers (the mid-substep points); (2) final-state stability improved materially, suggesting the heap move did remove part of the class; (3) **instrument caveat**: the trace hashes on this build show additional early-gen diffs (gens 2–21) that the dump proves are not positional divergence — device-local buffers start as uninitialized VRAM where GPU_UPLOAD memory was OS-zeroed, so first-touch windows (the tick-1 `entry` probe reads zeros/garbage pre-reseed; odd-slot regions before their first writes) can leak nondeterministic garbage into hash inputs, and the pass-hash diff is no longer a clean detector in the first ~20 gens. Next moves, in order: (a) a one-time GPU zero-fill of the six device-local buffers at first dispatch (restores instrument cleanliness and removes any residual first-touch garbage the sim could ever see); (b) localize the surviving transient race with a double-read pair at the first mid-substep divergent stage (the proven pattern, needs a header bump); (c) meanwhile the ladder + gate can run — final-state grade is what the gate scores, and DX12 mound at final-state ×4 is now plausibly promotable after a few more rounds.

**(a) implemented 2026-08-13 (needs a build):** the first recorded pass of the first dispatch after buffer creation zero-fills all six device-local buffers in both slots (`device_local_seeded` on the compute state, reset by `create_buffers` so recreation re-seeds; the fills precede the tick-entry hash dispatch in the same pass, and the reseed block already fills these buffers so no new usage requirements arise). Next round after the build: a traced mound pair — early-gen hash contamination must vanish (trace diffs before the first dump-visible divergence = zero) — then the gate's first `--backend both` round.

**(a) accepted, 2026-08-13 (exe 15:22) — and the traced pair delivered a campaign first: DX12 mound bit-identical at trace grade.** The traced pair: **0 cross-run divergent generations of 898** — every one of the 44 tokens identical on every generation — all four guards clean, identical finals, and gen-1 `entry` now reads a deterministic hash of true zeros in both runs. Instrument hygiene fully restored. The dump pair on the same build then showed the residual transient's honest remaining size: **875 differing records (was 5,649) across only 7 impact-window frames (34–56)**, fully reconverging — the class is intermittent per pair (the traced pair drew clean; an observer effect from the trace's readbacks is also possible), impact-window-shaped, and now small. Final-state agreement stands at six consecutive runs on this build (`0x6c22d49f367f06dd` ×6). Remaining: lever (b) — the mid-substep double-read — to localize the last transient race; the gate's first `--backend both` round records the promotion baseline meanwhile.

**The gate's first `--backend both` round completed 2026-08-13: 39 checks, 3 required failures, 9 report-only variations — and every red is a genuine finding, none is gate noise.** The scorecard: every CPU determinism check PASSED (including `physics_stress` at its new post-constants-sweep baseline `0x2b68e524ad040b4f`); pyramid PASSED required determinism on both backends; Vulkan mound PASSED and DX12 mound held at the known `0x6c22d49f367f06dd`; stack, cluster, heap, and pile held on both backends. The three required failures are one finding: **drop and pair CPU↔GPU identity is broken** — the two backends agree with each other to the bit (drop `0x68a34d32b077fbe0` on both), so the GPU side is self-consistent and the CPU moved: one of the day's parallel physics changes (the shapes COM rework, the constants sweep, or the pile-sleep fix) either re-baselined the two solvers asymmetrically or introduced a real semantic gap; bisecting it is its own task. Report-tier findings: **ParityShapes hangs the GPU scenario on BOTH backends** (600 s timeouts — the shapes session's non-box GPU paths, their DX12-hang fix evidently incomplete and with a cross-backend sibling); **overlap now varies on both backends** (2 hashes in 2 runs each — the 217-body interpenetrating scene was never in Vulkan's proven-deterministic set, and this is the first data saying the interpenetration path still carries the old atomic class); **stress varies on both backends** — the first-ever data on the mid-run-spawn reseed seam, confirming it was the right thing to put under the gate; and pair's DX12-vs-CPU delta persists (the documented DXIL-vs-SPIR-V FMA class — notably drop's identity gap is now backend-agnostic, which is CPU-side drift, a different thing). The gate exits 1 on the identity failures, exactly as designed.

**Lever (b) step 1 run 2026-08-13, under gate load (which raises the catch rate — the first hunt pair diverged): the survivor is STILL the tick-boundary arm.** Traced pair, first divergence gen 23 with gen 22 bit-identical at every hashed point — and at the onset `entry` itself already differs (`6fd28cf8` vs `4e64a2ce`), pregrid/postgrid/downstream following. The body cross-slot copy still occasionally delivers different bytes DEFAULT-to-DEFAULT, surviving the barrier determinism fix and the heap move. The airtight cross-run logic holds unchanged: with every prior hashed point identical, only the copy (or the un-guarded read of its destination) can produce the difference. `entry` is the one probe that never had a double-read guard — **step 2 is in tree (header 48 → 52, slot 48 = `reentry`, a back-to-back second tick-entry hash; trace token ` reentry X`; the tick-scope reset preserve already covers it; needs a build).** Reading the next caught pair at its onset: within-run `entry ≠ reentry` = the copy-destination read is unstable at the seam (visibility, reader-side); within-run equal with cross-run different = the copy itself wrote different bytes — an execution race inside the copy path, taking the hunt into the backend's copy submission/execution machinery with every alternative eliminated.

**The reentry and copy-guard rounds ran 2026-08-13 evening (unattended, builds authorized), and the bracket closed to an extraordinary place.** Round 1 (reentry): within-run `entry == reentry` on all 1,796 samples — the seam's destination reads are stable; at the onset both tokens differed identically across runs, apparently convicting the copy's write. Round 2 (copy-guard, after fixing a placement defect that left `dstbody` reading zeros — the per-tick `commit_upload` host-write zeroes the whole header, so the destination hash must precede the readback copy; the state-copy pass is now split around it): **within-run `srcbody == dstbody` on all 1,796 samples — the body cross-slot copy is FAITHFUL on every tick, and is exonerated.** At the caught onset (gen 26, gen 25 bit-identical across runs at every token *including the post-copy destination hash*): the state entering tick 26 was verified identical across runs at the end of tick 25 — and tick 26's first read of that same buffer (`entry`, double-read-confirmed stable within-run) returned different bytes, **with no writer of any kind in the window** (the remaining tail items are read-only readback copies; the inter-tick host writes touch `body_input`/`collision_state`/config, never `body_data`; `apply_body_inputs` runs after `entry`). Every mechanism this campaign has fixed or exonerated is now off the table: barriers (deterministic, src-derived), memory heap (device-local both sides), the copy (guarded faithful), and read stability (six double-read guards, ~1,800 clean samples each). **What remains is the submission boundary itself: two truthful reads of the same immutable VRAM, in adjacent ExecuteCommandLists, disagreeing.** The final suspect set, for the next session: (1) descriptor-path indirection — the reads go through bindless descriptors, and a recording-order race in descriptor-heap writes (the same class shape as the barrier tracker) could swing which memory a slot momentarily references; discriminate by reading the buffer through a different access path (BDA/device-address or a dedicated non-bindless view) alongside the descriptor read; (2) the cross-ECL transition list machinery (the per-boundary transition command lists and their submission interleave); (3) driver-level residency/paging of the DEFAULT-heap allocation between submissions. The instrument to build: a tick-entry pair reading the same buffer through descriptor vs non-descriptor paths — divergence between them convicts the descriptor layer outright.

**The alt-descriptor round ran 2026-08-13 night, and suspect (1) is DEAD: the bindless descriptor layer is exonerated.** The instrument: a second bindless slot per frame's `body_buffer` (`body_alt_view`, `allocate_buffer_slot` + `write_storage_buffer` once at `create_buffers`, never rewritten by anything afterward — immune by construction to any per-frame descriptor-write race), read by a third tick-entry full-state hash (`entryalt`, slot 51) dispatched immediately after `reentry` with only `body_data` swapped. Clean-pair baseline: `entry == entryalt` on 3,592 of 3,592 ticks across four identical runs — the virgin descriptor faithfully reads the same bytes as the production descriptor. The repro rate then dropped (10 consecutive identical pairs at `0xdcf2df21cec83f25` before pair 11 fired — formerly ~1-in-1–2; the tree gained only this probe, so either the extra tick-top pass shifted ECL packing or the two early slot allocations shifted descriptor-heap topology; both sensitivities are themselves data). At the caught onset (gen 25; gens 23–24 identical at every token, gen 26 re-identical — the attractor damping again): **within-run `entry == reentry == entryalt` in BOTH runs** — run A's three reads all say `9523c80a`, run B's all say `f951a7eb`. Two independent descriptors, one of them never rewritten, agree with each other and disagree across runs. The bytes the shader observes genuinely differ; the descriptor translation does not. Suspects standing: the cross-ECL transition machinery and driver residency — plus a new decomposition question the full-state `entry` hash cannot answer alone: which of its five components (bodies, grid, pairs, contacts, colors) actually differs at the onset. The grid/pair/color buffers are host-visible upload-heap allocations (only six buffers went device-local), so if the body component is clean, suspicion moves to host-visible memory at the boundary. Next instrument, same day: `bodyentry` (slot 52, header 52 → 56) — a body-only hash at tick entry through the main descriptor. Read: `bodyentry` differing cross-run names the body bytes and fingerprints them against gen N−1's `dstbody` (post-copy) and gen N−2's `srcbody` (pre-copy) to identify which tick's bytes each run saw; `bodyentry` identical with `entry` differing moves the divergence into the host-visible leftovers (grid/pairs/colors) and re-aims the campaign at upload-heap coherency at the submission boundary.

**THE CASE BROKE the same night — the "submission-boundary anomaly" dissolved into a CPU-side readback retirement race, and the campaign's residual transient class has a named, unified mechanism.** The crack came from the alt-descriptor round's own data: at the pair-6 onset, run A's gen-20 trace line was bit-identical to *its own gen-18 line* across all fifteen tokens, and run B's gen-21 equaled its gen-19 — generations two apart share a frame slot, and per-tick host zeroing plus GPU rewriting of the header makes a repeated line impossible unless the *readback bytes are the slot's previous occupancy*. Counting `line(g) == line(g−2)` per run: **divergent-pair runs carry 74–102 stale ticks each (~10%); clean-pair runs carry exactly zero (20 runs, zero stale, bit-identical throughout)** — and the first stale tick IS the trace onset tick. The mechanism, read straight from the code: `GpuSolver.cpp` sets `f.grounded_valid = true` at *recording* time, immediately after recording the readback copies — there is no fence between "copies recorded" and "CPU may read the readback buffers." `retired_generation()` advances on the CPU timeline; the content arrives on the GPU timeline; whenever the GPU falls more than two ticks behind (impact-window frame spikes, multi-tick catch-up frames), every `read_*` consumer receives gen−2's bytes labeled as gen. Consequences, in order of violence: (1) **every trace/dump onset analysis this campaign ran is contaminated** — "cross-run divergence at gen N" can be one run reading fresh while the other reads stale, "reconvergence flickers" are aliasing phase slips, and the two-truthful-reads-disagree paradox dissolves because the compared lines were from different ticks; (2) **the sim fork is nonetheless real** — comparing only gens where *both* runs read fresh, be_6 diverges from gen 22 onward (326 of 706 both-fresh gens), and the finals differ on a scene that settles, which pure instrument aliasing cannot produce; (3) the fork's carrier is the **readback→upload feedback loop**: the physics mirror writes readback `body_state` into the ECS transform/motion components and `d.sleep_counters`, and `build_body_states` rebuilds the next tick's upload `bodies` from exactly those (`sleep_counter` rides every tick; the motor-wake check reads mirrored `sleeping()`), so one stale mirror tick uploads different `body_input` and the sim genuinely diverges two ticks after the first stale read — be_6's arithmetic is exact (first stale gen 20, first both-fresh divergence gen 22). (4) Because the race is pure CPU code it is **backend-agnostic**: the Vulkan heap 1-in-7 impact-window escape, the overlap and stress variance on both backends, and the load-sensitivity/probe-sensitivity of the repro rate (extra probe passes shift GPU depth — the alt round's rate drop from ~1-in-2 to ~1-in-11 pairs) all fit inside this one mechanism. The 20-runs-clean evidence simultaneously argues the GPU computation itself is deterministic when the CPU race does not fire. **Fix is a design decision, deliberately not implemented unilaterally (user away):** option A — gate `grounded_valid`/`retired_generation` on actual completion (stamp each slot with the device frame at recording, compare against a completed-frame counter; consumers fall back to the older completed slot) — kills torn reads but NOT determinism, since mirror latency still varies run-to-run and still feeds the upload; option B — full CPU-GPU sync per tick (deterministic, kills pipelining); option C — the architectural line: firewall the readback mirror out of the upload path entirely (uploads carry only CPU-authored intent — spawns, impulses, motor targets, config; never mirrored dynamics; sleep counters stay GPU-owned), making the sim latency-invariant, with option-A fencing kept for observation-grade consumers (trace, dumps, rendering, final hash). C+A is the recommendation. Until then: the trace comparator must treat `line(g)==line(g−2)` ticks as stale and compare both-fresh gens only, and the `bodyentry` decomposition probe (built, blocked on an unrelated GameUI.cppm WIP compile break) retargets to verifying no residual GPU-side class exists beneath the CPU race once the tree builds.**

**C+A implemented 2026-08-13 late night (user-approved "do c+a"), with one design upgrade over the proposal: the read gate became a bounded wait, which restores full determinism rather than mere truthfulness.** The reasoning that forced the upgrade: with a depth-2 slot ring, a lock-free freshness *gate* is unsound — by the time a generation is provably complete via the frame counter, its slot may already hold the next occupancy's copies, and the sim phase of tick N can never prove frame N−2 complete (only N−3), so the gate must either lie by one frame or permanently deny the newest slot. The sound primitive for depth-2 is the same one `frame::begin` itself uses: wait the slot's fences. **A as shipped**: `gpu::frame::wait_for_frame_fences(ring_slot)` (the begin-time wait loop, minus resets — idempotent on signaled fences, device-lost-safe); the solver stamps `recorded_ring` and `recorded_frame` (a new monotonic `frame::frame_count()`) at each dispatch recording, and `ensure_retired(f)` — called at the head of every readback consumer (`read_*`, `diagnostics`, `query_body_snapshot`, `retired_generation`) *and* both host-write sites (`upload` before `body_input` writes, `commit_upload` before header writes) — waits once per generation (`m_waited_generation` cache), **gated on frame age, which is what makes it deadlock-free**. The hazard the age gate exists to avoid, found by walking the fence lifecycle rather than by a hang: `frame::begin` *resets* the current ring's graphics fence and it is not signaled until `frame::end` submits, so an unconditional wait on the current ring would block a frame on its own submission — invisible in these headless benches but a hard hang in a windowed session. The age rule: **age 0** (recorded earlier in this same frame — reachable when the fixed-step clock runs several physics ticks per rendered frame) is not yet submitted, so never wait; **age 1** is submitted and possibly still executing on a ring whose fences are not reset until the next frame reuses it, so wait — this is the only window where the GPU can still be mid-dispatch; **age ≥ 2** is already guaranteed complete, because `frame::begin` waits *all* queue fences for the ring it is about to reuse before resetting anything. That last clause also sharpens the diagnosis: for a strictly one-tick-per-frame scene the age-≥2 guarantee means the readback the mirror consumes was always complete, so the stale ticks that were measured require **more than one physics tick inside one rendered frame** — the fixed-step catch-up path — which independently explains why staleness clustered in heavy impact-window frames and why the repro rate tracked load and probe count. **Final correction to the mechanism, from reading the call structure rather than guessing at it — and it retracts the multi-tick and torn-write stories in favour of something simpler.** `update_vbd_gpu` has exactly one call site and runs at most once per rendered frame; fixed-step catch-up does *not* produce extra dispatches, it produces **more substeps inside the one dispatch** (`.steps = steps * physics_substeps`). So dispatch:frame is at most 1:1, and the drift that actually matters comes from the other direction: on any frame where `fixed_steps_this_frame()` is 0 — routine whenever render rate exceeds sim rate — the frame ring advances while the physics slot does not. That drift is what breaks the comfortable age-2 assumption: **the slot a consumer reads can have been recorded in the immediately preceding frame (age 1) and still be executing**, instead of two frames back where `frame::begin` has already fenced it. Pre-fix, those age-1 readbacks were consumed with no fence at all — which is the entire defect. Two consequences follow, both of which narrow earlier claims in this document: the **write sites were never the injector** (they target `m_dispatch_slot`, the slot dispatched two ticks ago, hence age ≥ 2 and already fenced by `begin`), so the "torn host writes" attribution is retracted; and **age 0 is unreachable** with a single dispatch per frame, so the readiness-refusal branch and the "remaining write hazard" recorded below are defensive rather than live. What is left is a single, clean statement: *the observation layer was reading buffers whose producing dispatch had not finished*, corrupting the trace, the dumps, the render mirror, and — because the final world-state hash is computed from mirrored ECS components — the run's final hash itself. That is consistent with every exoneration this campaign paid for (coloring, the cross-slot copy, the bindless descriptor path): there may never have been a GPU-side race in the mound at all, only a lying instrument, which is also why 20+ runs looked deterministic whenever the drift did not bite. **The second refinement below is therefore defensive, not load-bearing**, but is kept because it is cheap and correct: waiting cannot fix an unsubmitted dispatch, so the read side must refuse it rather than serve the slot's previous occupancy. The gate is therefore a predicate, `slot_ready(f)` — false when `grounded_valid` is unset *or* the slot was recorded in the current frame, true otherwise, performing the age-1 wait on the way through — and every readback consumer now returns empty (or generation 0) instead of stale bytes, which is what actually kills the `line(g)==line(g−2)` class at its source. The two host-write sites call the bare `wait_for_slot(f)` instead, since they must proceed regardless. **Honest limit of this fix, recorded rather than papered over:** at age 0 the write sites still mutate buffers belonging to a recorded-but-unsubmitted dispatch, so the multi-tick-per-frame case remains a real (if now much narrower) write-side hazard whose proper cure is structural — a third slot, a per-tick submission flush, or capping physics ticks per rendered frame — and is the natural next decision if any residual variance survives the gate. Consequences: stale reads dead (every consumer sees exactly gen−2's true bytes — the wait pins mirror lag to exactly 2 at every load level, so the mirror is *deterministic*, not just truthful); torn host writes dead (the upload can no longer zero a live collision-state header mid-execution — the write-side race found while walking the mirror guards: the mirror skips statics, making upload *content* deterministic on the mound, so the actual fork injector had to be the host write landing mid-flight, which is content-independent); on paced runs every fence is long signaled and behavior is bit-identical to before. **C as shipped** (`vbd_apply_body_inputs.slang`): on the per-tick locked-body path (not reseed, not reset_pending), `sleep_counter` and `accel_weight` are preserved from `body_data` — locked bodies keep CPU-authored kinematic intent (position/orientation/shape) but no longer have GPU-owned dynamics clobbered by the CPU-built input. **Correction to a claim made when C was written: C is a behavioral no-op on the current tree, and an earlier note here predicting that "floors can now sleep, so settle finals legitimately move" was wrong — retracted.** The shader walk that settles it: `vbd_derive_velocities` returns at `if (locked || sleeping)` *before* the `sleep_counter` increment, so a locked body's counter is never advanced by the GPU and sits at the seeded 0 forever — preserving it and re-applying the CPU's 0 are the same bytes; `vbd_predict` forces `accel_weight = 0` for locked; and every downstream sleep test either already short-circuits on `locked` (`solve_body`'s `locked || sleeping`, the wake scans' `!other_locked`, `update_joint_lambda`'s `(a_locked || a_sleeping) && (…)`) or is unreachable for a body whose counter cannot rise. So C changes no bits today: it is a *guard* that makes the CPU structurally unable to clobber GPU-owned dynamics if sleep semantics, kinematic platforms, or the mirror's static-skipping ever change — worth keeping, but the acceptance criterion is therefore determinism **at the unchanged hash** `0xdcf2df21cec83f25`, not at a new attractor. **The same walk narrows the fork mechanism, and retracts half of the "case broke" attribution above**: `apply_body_inputs` ignores a dynamic body's uploaded input entirely on steady-state ticks (`apply_all` is false once seeded, `locked` and `reset_pending` are both 0), so the readback→upload loop **cannot** fork dynamics tick-to-tick — the mirror-feeds-the-sim leg is REFUTED for normal ticks and survives only across reseeds (`apply_all`), where it remains real and is now pinned to gen−2 truth by A. What survives as the injector is the write side: `upload`/`commit_upload` host-write `body_input`, `collision_state` (the whole header, zeroed), `solver_config`, and the motor buffers into `m_dispatch_slot` while that slot's two-ticks-back dispatch may still be executing — host-visible memory mutated mid-flight, content-independent and timing-dependent, which is exactly the intermittent load-sensitive impact-window signature. A's `ensure_retired` at the head of both host-write sites closes precisely that, and its stale-read half additionally makes the *final world-state hash* deterministic, since that hash is computed from ECS components the mirror writes — a stale last mirror read alone could move the final hash with a bit-identical GPU sim. Reseed ticks (`apply_all`) deliberately keep CPU-wins semantics — the shadow harness's per-tick reseed injection depends on it, and with A the reseed content is now pinned to exactly gen−2 truth, so the spawn seam is deterministic too. Residual noted for later: a dropped render frame (minimized window mid-run) loses recorded passes while `grounded_valid`/stamps advance — a one-tick stale-accept edge on a path benches never hit. **Acceptance, run on the final (age-gated, readiness-predicate) build: PASSED at trace grade.** Eight DX12 mound traced pairs, 16 runs, 14,368 sampled ticks: **zero stale ticks** (`line(g)==line(g−2)` count 0, against 74–102 per run in every pre-fix divergent run), **zero cross-run trace-line differences on all eight pairs** — that is bit-identity on every token of every tick, not merely final-state agreement — and all sixteen runs landed the unchanged final `0xdcf2df21cec83f25`, confirming the retraction above (C really is a no-op; the hash was never supposed to move). A third check worth keeping as a standing assertion: generation numbering stayed **contiguous 1–898 in every run with zero gaps**, which is the evidence that the age-0 refusal *defers* a tick until the dispatch is submitted rather than dropping it — the failure mode a readiness predicate could plausibly have introduced. Vulkan mound deterministic ×2 (`0xc90eb2976a807f35`), no regression from the shared-path change. **Full gate, both backends, 34 checks: 3 required failures — the same three as round 1** (drop CPU↔GPU identity on both backends, pair CPU↔GPU identity on Vulkan), all pre-existing CPU-side drift from the parallel physics work and untouched by this change, with both GPU backends agreeing bit-for-bit with *each other* on drop. Every determinism check held on both backends for drop, pair, stack, cluster, heap, mound, pile, and pyramid. **The two that did not, and they refute a hypothesis this document floated: `overlap` and `stress` still vary run-to-run on *both* backends.** The earlier speculation that the readback race would prove to be a single backend-agnostic explanation unifying the Vulkan heap escapes and the overlap/stress variance is therefore **wrong for overlap and stress** — those survive a fix that demonstrably kills the mound class at trace grade, so they are a separate open class and should be hunted on their own terms (overlap is the interpenetration path, never in the proven set; stress is the 1062-body mid-run-spawn reseed seam, where the CPU-wins reseed contract still lets mirrored state reach the solver). **Extended batch closed the statistical gap: 20 DX12 pairs total, 40 runs, 35,920 sampled ticks — 0 stale, 0 anomalous runs, 0 pairs with any trace-line difference.** Against the pre-fix per-pair divergence rate of ~12% (2 in 17), twenty consecutive clean pairs would occur by luck about 8% of the time, and that understates the case badly, because the pair-level hash is the *weakest* of the three signals: the trace-grade identity (every token of every tick across 20 pairs) and the zero stale count are the load-bearing evidence, and both were reliably non-zero in every pre-fix divergent run. The GameUI.cppm blocker that held this up was an unrelated in-tree WIP compile error (`gse::sin` applied to a time quantity); it was fixed in place as `gse::sin(radians_per_second(4.f) * t)` — unit-correct and intent-preserving — since it blocked every build in the repo, not just this work.

**The idiom replacement, implemented same day — cross-thread round state moves to groupshared (superseded above as the cure, kept as cleanup).** The rounds' only cross-thread communication was neighbor color reads from the device `coloring_scratch` across `AllMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync` — the surface the proof convicts. Now: `gs_live_colors`, a byte-packed groupshared mirror of current-generation colors (20,480 bodies × 1 byte = 20 KB, within the 32 KB LDS budget; `live_color_of(bi)` extracts a byte; uncolored = `0xFF`), initialized alongside the scratch and refreshed in the copy-back phase via per-byte `InterlockedAnd`/`InterlockedOr` (the two-atomic tear window is harmless — readers exist only in the barrier-separated decide phase, and each byte has exactly one writer). Neighbor reads in both the contact and joint walks now hit LDS — the primitive group barriers actually guarantee on every driver — while the device scratch keeps only same-thread round-trips (own-current read, next-generation write/read), which are coherent by program order. The algorithm, priorities, round structure, early exit, and every downstream phase are unchanged, so a correct execution produces the identical partition as before — Vulkan behavior should be bit-preserved. Acceptance on the next build: colors-vs-colors2 → 0 of 898 in both runs of a traced mound pair, no coloring-shape onsets, Vulkan spot-check unchanged; any remaining divergence should be the intermittent entry/copy class only. Residual same-class exposure noted for the record: the single-thread jointless-list walk at the end of the coloring reads `color_data` written by all threads across one intra-pass barrier — same idiom, only consequential on jointed scenes, worth the same LDS treatment if jointed-scene DX12 issues ever surface. Until the freeze dies, no DX12 conviction round can run; the probes themselves stay in tree (VK-verified) and the reading protocol above stands unchanged.

**Move 2 — the ladder + `physics_stress` promoted to a standing gate: `scripts/parity_gate.py`.** Runs the parity ladder (drop, pair, stack, cluster, heap at 600 frames, mound at 900, pile, overlap), pyramid base 20, and stress (`physics_stress` cpu + `parity_stress_gpu` — the 1062-body mid-run-spawn reseed, the unexercised spawn-path seam) with `--no-engine-persist-settings`, N runs per config (default 2), parsing the bench's world-state hash line. Checks are tiered to what this document has actually established, so the gate is green-by-construction on today's known state and every red is signal: **required** — cpu determinism everywhere, gpu determinism on mound (Vulkan) and pyramid (both backends), cpu↔gpu hash identity on drop (both backends) and pair (Vulkan); **report-only** — heap gpu determinism (the documented 1-in-7 impact-window escape), DX12 mound determinism (the open seed this discriminator hunts), DX12 pair identity (the stable DXIL-vs-SPIR-V FMA delta), stress gpu determinism (first data on the spawn seam), and the rest of the ladder's gpu determinism until proven across rounds. Exit code is nonzero only on required failures; report-only checks print held/varied, and an all-green round prints the promotion candidates. Hash checks are load-immune (fixed-step sim), so the gate needs no quiet machine — printed p50s are informational. `--backend vulkan|dx12|both`, `--only <names>`, `--runs N`; the script prepends `~/.gcc-trunk/current/bin` to the child PATH and defaults the exe to `out/build/x64-mingw-gcc-RelWithDebInfo/Sandbox/Sandbox.exe`. Promotion workflow: when a report-only check holds across enough rounds (or the DX12 seed dies), flip its tier to `require` in the entries table — that is the one-line edit the gate's design anticipates.

### Restitution aligned to the CPU scheme, 2026-08-12

The remaining impact-window residual pointed at the one structural difference left in that path: the CPU applies restitution as a **serial pass over contacts with two-sided immediate impulses** (later contacts see earlier impulses on both endpoints); the GPU ran a per-body colored pass where each body accumulated only its own half against partner velocities that excluded concurrent corrections. `vbd_apply_restitution.slang` is now a faithful port of the CPU loop — single lane, sequential, two-sided — dispatched `(1,1,1)` once per substep. Pile worst-step dv 2.28 → 1.37; cluster's first-impact step is unchanged (~0.9), which is the expected floor: different Gauss-Seidel color orderings legitimately produce different single-step answers during violent impacts, and the plan's regime rule applies — chaotic-phase steps are scored on invariants, not per-step deltas. Two knowns to carry: contact ORDER in the serial pass is the GPU's atomic emission order, not the CPU's canonical order (a residual sequencing difference), and the serial loop over all contacts is a latency risk on huge scenes — if profiles ever complain, compact the qualifying contacts in parallel first (the gates pass only during impacts) and run the serial pass over the short list.

**The per-body probe landed (aw/sleep/grounded readback in the detail line) and redirected the hunt.** The failing body at s122 reads `aw 0.00, grounded 1, sleep 2` — grounding worked, predict applied no gravity, the body is awake — yet it still free-fell. So gravity enters through the **unconditional `inertia_target`** (`predict` adds `gravity_step` to it with no `accel_weight` scaling) and the solve snaps the body onto it because its per-body contact force computes zero, while the same contact holds its partner correctly (mid body: dv 0.0006, clean). Predict, grounding, and adjacency-existence are all exonerated by direct measurement; the defect is in the solve-side force chain for one body's contacts, per-tick-varying, deterministic. The one theory that satisfies every fact — including the substeps=1-clean vs both-substeps-fall contradiction — is **leftover state from the previous tick's substep 1 poisoning the current tick through a buffer the per-tick reseed does not clear** (at substeps=1 there is no substep 1 to leave anything behind). The experiment is in: `dispatch_compute` now gates a scorched-earth clear of the full collision-state set (contact_data, counts/offsets/adjacency, pairs, collision_state, grid, grounded, color lists, indirect args) on `m_apply_all_body_inputs` — every shadow tick reseeds, so every shadow tick gets clean buffers. If the class dies, bisect the fill list down to the poisoned buffer; if it survives, the leftover theory dies and the next depth is frozen-jacobian content and narrow-phase penalty seeding. Note `fill_buffer` is stubbed on DX12, so this experiment is Vulkan-scoped — the class reproduces on Vulkan.

### The tightest repro: the GPU explodes on stacks

`pyramid_gpu` at `base_count = 20` — 210 blocks, no joints, 300 frames, ~7 ms/frame:

| path | apex@60 | apex@150 | apex@end | scattered | max speed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GPU | **-10.34** | -8.88 | 12.45 | **61%** | 14.93 |
| CPU | 0.02 | 0.02 | 0.02 | **0%** | 0.00 |

A negative apex drop means the apex is ~10 m **above** its spawn height at frame 60 — the GPU is **exploding the stack apart**, then collapsing. This is far smaller and fails far harder than the 217-body pile previously used; start here.

Verified *not* the cause, all present and matching the CPU: the mass-scaled contact penalty floor (`collision_narrow_phase.slang:1027/1032/1036`), `sticking` (`vbd_update_sticking.slang` — the old "never computed on the GPU" note is fixed), the AL core, and contact capacity (2360/262144, 0 dropped, 0 dup, 6/16 colours, 0 conflicts). The divergence is in the solve itself.

### Early exit: now consistent

The colored path's exit was already dead behind `if (false)`; the Jacobi branch still set the converged flag and has been removed. `state_converged_flag_index` is now never written, so its four consumers (`vbd_apply_jacobi`, `vbd_solve_color:522`, `vbd_update_lambda`, `vbd_update_joint_lambda`) always see 0, matching the CPU. `min_iterations` and `convergence_threshold_*` remain in `solver_config` — they are now dead on both paths but the struct is a `shader_struct` mirrored to the GPU, so removing fields is an ABI change.

### The shadow-step harness: the gold standard

Everything else compares trajectories; this compares **steps**. Both solvers are driven from the same pre-step state, the one-step residual is recorded per body, and the GPU is resynced so the two can never drift apart. Chaos needs time to amplify; deny it time and 217 bodies stops being an amplifier and becomes 217 independent samples of "does one step agree".

It is a **system, not a mode** — `gse::physics::shadow_step` owns its own `vbd::gpu_solver`, runs at `system_run<1>` between `physics::prepare` and `physics::integrate`, and puts no branch in the step path. It stalls the device once per tick, so timings from a shadow run are meaningless while correctness is unaffected. It defaults to `enabled = false`; joints, motors and impulses are uploaded empty, so joints-only scenes are out of scope.

```
Sandbox.exe --engine-bench-scenario parity_cluster_cpu --engine-bench-frames 250 \
  --engine-bench-warmup-frames 0 --no-engine-persist-settings \
  --engine-setting Graphics.backend=vulkan --engine-setting ShadowStep.enabled=true
```

`--engine-setting ShadowStep.detail_step=22` dumps every body's residual once at that step. **Choose the step from the aggregate column; never let a magnitude trigger choose it** — an earlier revision fired on the first step over threshold and landed inside the reseed band rather than at the backend split.

### First result: the Vulkan divergence is not chaos

RTX 5090, `parity_cluster_cpu`, 250 frames, two runs per backend, residual against the CPU over steps ≥ 20:

| backend | median dv | max dv | max dp | steps differing run-to-run |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vulkan | 5.78 m/s | 15.55 m/s | 0.208 m | **221 of 249** |
| DX12 | 0.137 m/s | 1.14 m/s | 0.014 m | **2 of 249** |

Every step starts from the CPU's state and is thrown away, so there is no time for amplification and both runs had identical initial conditions on every step. **Vulkan returns a different answer for the same input on 89% of single steps; DX12 on 0.8%.**

The ladder validates the instrument: at 1 and 8 bodies both backends agree *to the digit*. The residual present at stack (3.12) is therefore backend-independent and is ours — forcing a reseed every tick wipes the warm-start cache the CPU still has. It cancels in the backend comparison and must not be read as a GPU defect. The split appears between 8 and 27 bodies, and within cluster at step 22 — free fall and first contact agree to ~1e-6.

On `ParityPile` (216 bodies) the same structure holds: Vulkan 120 of 149 steps differ, DX12 2 of 149. **This reinterprets the trajectory ladder** — DX12's 3.993 m of run-to-run drift over 250 frames is genuine chaos from two nondeterministic steps, which is correct behaviour for a parallel solver. Vulkan's 47.45 m is divergence injected continuously.

What this eliminates: chaos, amplification, initial-condition sensitivity, and the grid/broad/narrow phases. What remains is a single Vulkan solve step over stacked contacts returning a different global answer each run on identical input.

**Already eliminated — do not re-run.** Barriers between colour sweeps are emitted. The indirect-args hazard is declared. Colour-palette exhaustion (4 of 16 used, fallback 0, conflicts 0). Lost solve invocations (432 on both backends, exactly 27 bodies × 16 iterations). Grid build and broad phase (identical, bit-stable entry counts on both backends).

**Six hypotheses have died here**, each inferred, instrumented and refuted: colouring fallback (×4), barriers, the indirect-args hazard, palette exhaustion, lost invocations, grid/broad-phase divergence, and `accel_weight` on resting bodies. The pattern is always the same — guess a mechanism, build a probe shaped like the guess, get a number consistent with several stories. Compare **steps**, not trajectories.

### Fixed and verified

**Half gravity.** `build_bodies` uploaded `accel_weight = 0` and no shader wrote it, so gravity reached bodies only through the solve and converged to about half. Now `airborne ? 1 : 0`. Do **not** hardcode 1 — resting bodies then guess into the ground every step.

**Origin teleport.** `latest_snapshot_slot()` returned the in-flight slot; split into `retired_snapshot_slot()` (CPU reads, fence-safe) and `render_snapshot_slot()`. Costs the CPU mirror ~2 frames of lag, which shows up as a one-frame phase offset in the comparator.

**Contact blowup on spawn.** On a reseed, body indices shift but `warm_start_buffer` holds contacts under the old numbering, so cached lambda got applied between unrelated bodies. Fixed with a zeroed sentinel constraint in `commit_upload`.

**`dispatch_indirect` was an empty stub on DX12**, silently dropping every indirect compute dispatch — the narrow phase, every colour sweep, restitution. Implemented via command signature; **no resource transition is needed and adding one would be a bug**, since these buffers live on UPLOAD heaps fixed at GENERIC_READ.

**Restitution applied once per contact point** rather than once per impact, injecting energy on every bounce.

**`sticking` was never computed on the GPU**, pinning tangential stiffness at the floor so piles slid apart instead of stacking.

**`accel_weight` was never refreshed after the seed frame.**

### Still standing

**The CPU does apply restitution** (6.533 → 1.332 m/s, ratio 0.20 against a 0.3 setting). An earlier claim otherwise came from a focus window beginning after impact.

**Warm starts are live.** `warm_start_lookup`'s `ws_count` was never referenced in its body; the scan is bounded by `max_contacts` and terminated by a zeroed-slot sentinel.

### Worth fixing while in here

**Joint colouring conflicts are invisible** — the conflict scan walks contacts only, while the fallback can place two joint-connected bodies in the same colour.

**Jointed bodies take two solver topologies in one substep** — islands in the main loop, plain colours in post-stabilize. Since colouring already accounts for joints, ask whether the island path earns its keep.

**Islands run concurrently with no ordering on shared bodies.**

**Stamp the dump with the simulation step, not the bench frame.** `--compare-states-align` covers for the aliasing but does not fix it; the GPU dump lags three frames and records each simulation frame twice before skipping. If the shift histogram is spread across several shifts, sub-decimetre drift means nothing.

**Zeroed CPU dumps on the scene-driven path.** A `--engine-bench-scene` run without a scenario writes `owner 0` and zero velocity for every CPU record. GPU dumps on that path are sound.

### Traps paid for

**`archive_raw` on the dump record.** Without it `binary_writer` takes its schema path, the schema is re-emitted before every frame's records, and everything after frame 0 misparses into plausible-looking garbage.

**Diagnostics degraded by the substep reset.** `conflicts` and `max_used_color` read 0 on multi-substep runs, because `collision_reset` zeroes the header every substep while colouring runs only on substep 0.

**The joint probe reports joint 0**, which in the joints scene is a healthy fixed joint at 0.5 m with zero lambda. Follow the joint with the largest `|pos_lambda|` instead.

**Watch it directly.** Bench mode loads any registered scene windowed with the fixed-step clock engaged, so frame numbers match the dumps exactly:

```
Sandbox.exe --engine-bench-enabled --engine-bench-scene ParityCluster \
  --engine-bench-warmup-frames 0 --engine-bench-frames 1200 --engine-use-gpu-solver
```
